L618: ## 8.1.5. Renal Impairment
L619: No dosage adjustment is required for subjects with renal impairment.
L620: The renal clearance of remibrutinib was determined in mass balance Study X2104 in which healthy subjects received oral or intravenous (IV) doses of radiolabeled remibrutinib. Both oral and IV administration were evaluated as there was incomplete recovery of radioactivity in the oral arm due to technical issues. For additional details, refer to Section 14.2.2. Following oral administration, about 24% of the dose was recovered in the urine. The amount of unchanged remibrutinib excreted in urine was minimal, accounting for 0.65% of the oral dose, while the most abundant metabolite excreted in urine accounted for about 3.5% of the oral dose. Following IV dosing, about 26 to 30% of the dose was recovered in the urine. The percentage of unchanged remibrutinib excreted in urine was 2.93%. These findings suggest that renal excretion is a minor elimination pathway for remibrutinib. As additional supportive evidence in Study X2101 the renal clearance of remibrutinib following administration of multiple doses of 100 or 200 mg BID was found to constitute &lt;1% of the total blood clearance (refer to Section 14.2.1.1).
L621: PopPK analysis did not identify creatinine clearance (CrCL; range 23 to 275 mL/min) as a significant covariate affecting the clearance of remibrutinib (Figure 14). The data represent 904 subjects (78.5%) with normal renal function (CrCL \ge90 mL/min), 222 subjects (19.3%) subjects with mild renal impairment (CrCL 60 to 90 mL/min); 25 subjects (2.2%) with mild-to-moderate renal impairment (CrCL 45 to 60 mL/min); and 1 subject (0.1%) with severe renal impairment (CrCL 15 to 30 mL/min). Based on the pharmacokinetic data from Studies X2104 and X2101, as well as the popPK analysis, a dedicated renal impairment study was not considered necessary. Therefore, no dose adjustment is required for CSU subjects with renal impairment.
L622: **Figure 14. Remibrutinib Dose-Normalized Observed C_{max,ss} Concentrations vs. Creatinine Clearance in Pooled Data, Log Scale**
L623: Source: Population pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib to support labeled dosage in adult subjects with chronic spontaneous urticaria, Figure 7-7
L624: C_{max,ss} refers to the averaged concentration at time after dose between 0.75 hour and 1.25 hours. Blue dots represent BID regimen and red dots represent QD regimen. The black solid curve is smooth line, and shared grey area is the 95% confidence interval.
L625: Abbreviations: BID, twice daily; C_{max,ss}, maximum plasma concentration at steady state; QD, once daily
L626: ## 8.1.6. Hepatic Impairment
L627: The use of remibrutinib should be avoided in subjects with mild, moderate, or severe hepatic impairment. Refer to key safety review issue in Section 7.7.5 for details.
L628: ## 8.2. Extrinsic Factors
L629: ## 8.2.1. Food Effect
L630: Remibrutinib can be administered with or without food.
L631: The effect of food on exposure to remibrutinib was assessed in healthy subjects in Study A02104 following administration of 25 mg BID in the intended to-be-marketed FCT formulation. For additional details, refer to Section 14.2.4. Notably, the food effect was evaluated at steady state. However, the goal of food-effect evaluation is to assess the food effect on drug absorption. The about 2-fold accumulation of remibrutinib systemic exposure at steady state is not contributed by drug absorption. Therefore, the food effect on remibrutinib at steady state can be extrapolated to single dose, given the short half-life of remibrutinib (1 to 2 hours).
L632: When remibrutinib FCTs at a dosage of 25 mg BID were coadministered with a high-fat, high-calorie meal (1000 calories, 50% fat), the pharmacokinetic parameters were not significantly altered (see Section 14.2.4). Specifically, the AUC0-12h increased by approximately 33%, while Cmax decreased by roughly 5%. Additionally, the Tmax was delayed by 1 hour. These differences in exposure are not considered to be clinically meaningful. Therefore, remibrutinib may be administered with or without food.
L633: ## 8.2.2. Effect of Remibrutinib on Other Drugs
L634: The potential inhibition or induction of CYP enzymes and transporters by remibrutinib was investigated through in vitro and in vivo DDI studies. Notably, in in vivo studies, the effects of remibrutinib on the pharmacokinetics of substrates for CYP3A4, CYP2C9, CYP1A2, P-gp, BCRP, and organic anion transporting polypeptide (OATP)1B1 were studied at a higher dosage (i.e., 100 mg BID) than the recommended 25-mg BID dosage. PBPK simulations were conducted to estimate the clinical DDI risk of remibrutinib at the recommended dosage of 25 mg BID. The review team concluded that the findings from the studies conducted at the higher dosage of remibrutinib are applicable to the recommended 25-mg BID dosage.
L635: Based on in vitro studies and static drug-interaction potential assessments, remibrutinib is not expected to significantly affect the pharmacokinetics of concomitant drugs that are substrates of CYP enzymes (CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A) or transporters (bile salt export pump [BSEP], OATP1B1, OATP1B3, organic cation transporter [OCT]1, OCT2, organic anion transporter [OAT]1, OAT3, multidrug and toxin extrusion protein [MATE]1, MATE2-K, and sodium taurocholate cotransporting polypeptide), with the exception of P-gp and BCRP (Table 28).
L636: ## **CYP3A4 Substrates**
L637: In vitro studies have demonstrated that remibrutinib acts as a reversible inhibitor (unbound inhibition constant [reversible inhibition] [K_{i,u}]=14.1\muM), time-dependent inhibitor (unbound inhibition constant [time-dependent inhibition] [K_{i,u}]=6.9\muM) and inducer (E_{max}=28.1 and half-maximal effective concentration [EC_{50}]=5.08\muM) of CYP3A4. Consequently, the net effect of remibrutinib on CYP3A4 function may exhibit time-dependent characteristics. The observed reversible inhibition effect is likely more pronounced at the initiation of treatment, while the induction effect may be most evident at steady state, following administration of multiple doses of remibrutinib.
L638: Although there is low potential for remibrutinib to have a clinically relevant impact on the pharmacokinetics of CYP3A substrates based on static DDI assessment (Table 28), the potential of remibrutinib to inhibit or induce CYP3A4 enzymes was evaluated following administration of multiple oral doses of remibrutinib 100-mg capsules in healthy female subjects using midazolam (administered on Day 3 and Day 11, following the initiation of remibrutinib dosing) as the probe substrate for CYP3A4 (Study X2102, see Section 14.2.6.3). When midazolam was coadministered with multiple doses of remibrutinib 100 mg on Day 3, the plasma concentrations of midazolam were not clinically meaningfully affected, with 36% increase in C_{max} and 45% increase in AUC. Similar results were observed on Day 11, with less than 27% increase in midazolam C_{max} and 43% increase in midazolam AUC. Based on the observed effects of remibrutinib administered at four times the recommended dosage on midazolam exposure on Days 3 and 11, it can be inferred that remibrutinib has a low potential to inhibit or induce CYP3A4 substrates at the recommended remibrutinib dosage of 25 mg BID.
L639: PBPK simulations did not predict a clinically relevant DDI effect on the exposure to the sensitive CYP3A substrate midazolam following coadministration with remibrutinib at the recommended dosage of 25 mg BID on Days 3 and 11. PBPK simulations estimate an 18% increase in midazolam C_{max} and 20% increase in midazolam AUC on Day 3; and a 22% increase in C_{max} and 25% increase in AUC on Day 11 (see Section 14.5.2).
L640: To further assess the potential of remibrutinib to affect the pharmacokinetics of CYP3A4 substrates, Study X2102 also evaluated the DDI potential between multiple oral doses of remibrutinib 100-mg capsules and combined oral contraceptives (OCs), containing ethinylestradiol (EE) and levonorgestrel (LVG), administered on Day 15 following the initiation of remibrutinib dosing. Multiple doses of remibrutinib 100 mg BID did not significantly impact the exposure to EE and LVG, with 28% increase in the C_{max} and 18% increase in the AUC of EE and 36% increase in the C_{max} and 39% increase in the AUC of LVG (see Section 14.2.6.3). PBPK simulations did not predict a clinically relevant DDI effect on EE pharmacokinetics following coadministration with remibrutinib 25 mg BID (7% increase in C_{max} and 8% increase in AUC) (see Section 14.5.3).
L641: Taken together, remibrutinib has low potential to inhibit or induce CYP3A in vivo. Sensitive CYP3A substrates or combined OCs containing EE and LVG may be coadministered with remibrutinib. No clinically relevant impact on the efficacy of oral contraceptives is expected following coadministration with remibrutinib.
L642: ## CYP2C9 Substrates
L643: In vitro studies have demonstrated that remibrutinib acts as a reversible inhibitor (K_{i,u}=7.7\muM) and inducer (E_{max}=3.02 and EC_{50}=5.1\muM) of CYP2C9. However, based on static DDI assessments, remibrutinib is not expected to have a clinically relevant impact on the pharmacokinetics of CYP2C9 substrates in vivo (Table 28). Despite this, the potential for multiple oral doses of remibrutinib 100 mg BID to inhibit or induce CYP2C9 was evaluated in healthy female subjects using tolbutamide as the probe substrate (Study X2102, see Section 14.2.6.3). Tolbutamide was administered on Day 3 and Day 11 following the initiation of remibrutinib treatment. Results demonstrated a 27% increase in tolbutamide plasma concentration measured at 24 hours postdose (C24h) on Day 3 and a 30% increase in tolbutamide C24h on Day 11 following coadministration with remibrutinib 100 mg BID. The Applicant selected tolbutamide C24h as a metric for CYP2C9 activity due to its purported strong correlation with tolbutamide clearance (Jetter et al. 2004). However, based on the findings from Jetter et al. (2004), tolbutamide C24h may overestimate the magnitude of drug interaction between remibrutinib and tolbutamide compared to that determined using C_{max} and AUC. As multiple oral doses of remibrutinib 100 mg yielded an approximate 30% increase in tolbutamide C24h, a lower magnitude may have been observed based on C_{max} and AUC. Hence, remibrutinib 100 mg is unlikely to have clinically relevant impact on the exposure to CYP2C9 substrates.
L644: The outcomes of a static DDI model, which indicated no significant clinical interaction risk with CYP2C9 substrates, and the absence of a complete pharmacokinetic profile for tolbutamide, obviate the necessity for PBPK simulations for the impact of remibrutinib 25 mg BID on CYP2C9 substrates.
L645: ## CYP1A2 Substrates
L646: In vitro studies have demonstrated that remibrutinib acts as a reversible inhibitor (K_{i,u}=35.3\muM) and inducer (E_{max}=5.47, EC_{50}=4.55\muM) of CYP1A2. However, based on static DDI assessments, remibrutinib is not expected to have a clinically relevant impact on the pharmacokinetics of CYP1A2 substrates in vivo. The potential for multiple oral doses of remibrutinib 100 mg BID to inhibit or induce CYP1A2 was evaluated in healthy female subjects using caffeine as a probe substrate (Study X2102, see Section 14.2.6.3). Caffeine was administered on Day 3 and Day 11 following the initiation of remibrutinib treatment. The administration of multiple doses of remibrutinib 100 mg BID did not affect caffeine plasma concentration measured at 6 hours postdose (C6h). In addition, there was little change in the paraxanthine to caffeine ratio at 6 hours, with reductions of 3% and 12% on Day 3 and Day 11, respectively.
L647: The outcomes of a static DDI model, which indicated no significant clinical interaction risk with CYP1A2 substrates, and the absence of a complete pharmacokinetic profile for caffeine, obviate the necessity for PBPK simulations to assess the impact of remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
L648: ## P-gp Substrates
L649: In vitro, remibrutinib inhibited intestinal P-gp (inhibition constant [reversible inhibition] [K_i]=0.382\muM) and static DDI assessments predict a potential impact of remibrutinib on P-gp substrates with R-value =517. The impact of remibrutinib on the pharmacokinetics of digoxin, a P-gp substrate, was investigated in healthy subjects in Study A02103 (see Section 14.2.6.2). In healthy subjects, the C_{max} and AUC_{last} of digoxin increased by 108% and 45%, respectively,
L650: following coadministration with remibrutinib 100 mg BID (see Section 14.2.6.2). PBPK simulations predicted [REDACTED] (b) (4) in the C_{max} and AUC, respectively, of digoxin when coadministered with multiple oral doses of remibrutinib 100 mg BID. The PBPK simulations tend to mis-predict the observed magnitude of the interaction between remibrutinib 100 mg BID and digoxin (see Section 14.5.3). Therefore, due to the inherent limitations of model predictions and confidence in the predicted results, the clinical findings from Study A02103, conducted with the 100-mg BID dosage of remibrutinib, should be applicable to subjects receiving the lower dosage of 25 mg BID. The magnitude of effect at a remibrutinib dosage of 25 mg BID is expected to be lower than that at 100 mg BID. However, the difference in effect is unclear without the conduct of a dedicated clinical study.
L651: It is therefore recommended to monitor more frequently for adverse reactions when remibrutinib is administered with P-gp substrates that have a narrow therapeutic index (i.e., drugs for which minimal concentration changes can lead to clinically significant differences in efficacy or safety).
L652: ## **BCRP/OATP1B1 Substrates**
L653: In vitro, remibrutinib inhibited intestinal BCRP (K_i=0.467\mu M) and static DDI assessments predicted a potential impact of remibrutinib on BCRP substrates with R-value =423. Although remibrutinib inhibited OATP1B1 (K_{i,u}=2.36\mu M) and OATP1B3 (K_{i,u}=7.55\mu M) in vitro, static DDI assessment predicted no drug-interaction potential between remibrutinib and OATP1B1/3 substrates (e.g., rosuvastatin). The impact of remibrutinib on the pharmacokinetics of rosuvastatin, a BCRP and OATP1B substrate, was investigated in healthy subjects in Study A02103. In Study A02103, the C_{max} and AUC_{last} of rosuvastatin increased by 56% and 65%, respectively, following coadministration with remibrutinib 100 mg BID. To distinguish between inhibition of BCRP and OATP1B, changes in coproporphyrin I exposure were also assessed. As there was no change in the plasma exposure to coproporphyrin I, it is expected that inhibition of OATP1B by remibrutinib is negligible (see Section 14.2.6.2). Given that in vitro data predict a low risk of inhibition of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 at clinically relevant concentrations, the increase in rosuvastatin plasma exposure can be attributed to an inhibitory effect of remibrutinib on BCRP.
L654: PBPK simulations tended to mis-predict the observed magnitude of the interaction between remibrutinib 100 mg BID and rosuvastatin (see Section 14.5.3). Therefore, due to the inherent limitations of model predictions and confidence in the predicted results, the clinical findings from Study A02103, conducted with the 100 mg BID dosage of remibrutinib, should be applicable to subjects receiving the lower dosage of 25 mg BID. The magnitude of effect at a remibrutinib dosage of 25 mg BID is expected to be lower than that at 100 mg BID. However, the difference in effect is unclear without conduct of a dedicated clinical study.
L655: It is therefore recommended to monitor more frequently for adverse reactions when remibrutinib is administered with BCRP substrates that have a narrow therapeutic index (i.e., drugs for which minimal concentration changes can lead to clinically significant differences in efficacy or safety).

L662: ## NDA 218436
## RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
L663: 4 Hepatic net effect calculated as AUCR = 1/((1/A_h \times 1/B_h \times 1/C_h) \times f_{m,sys} + (1-f_{m,sys})), where f_{m,sys} is equal to 0.9 (midazolam, CYP3A), 0.8 (theophylline, CYP1A2), 0.64 (efavirenz, CYP2B6), 0.58 (repaglinide, CYP2C8), 0.91 (warfarin, CYP2C9), 0.87 (omeprazole, CYP2C19), 0.9 (desipramine, CYP2D6) or 1 (worst-case assumption for all other CYP's). For net effect calculations unbound total maximal hepatic input plasma concentration ([I] = f_{u,p} \times (C_{max} + (F_g \times F_g \times k_a \times Dose/Q_r/RB))) and unbound inhibition constants were used. A_h and B_h are equal to 1 in the absence of any inhibition effect. A_h is reversible inhibition of hepatic CYP enzymes, A_h = 1/(1 + [I]/K_i); B_h is time-dependent inhibition of hepatic CYP enzymes, B_h = k_{deg,h}/([k_{deg,h} + ([I]_h \times k_{inact})/[I]_h + K_i]); C_h is induction of hepatic CYP enzymes, C_h = 1 + d \times E_{max} \times [I]_h/[I]_h + EC_{50}.
L664: 5 Intestinal net effect calculated as AUCR = 1/((1/A_g \times 1/B_g \times 1/C_g) \times (1-F_g) + F_g), where F_g is equal to 0.51 (midazolam, CYP3A). For net effect calculations [I]_g = F_a \times k_a \times Dose/Q_{en} and unbound inhibition/induction constants were used. A_g is reversible inhibition of gut CYP enzymes, A_g = 1/(1 + [I]_g/K_i); B_g is time-dependent inhibition of gut CYP enzymes, B_g = k_{deg,g}/([k_{deg,g} + ([I]_g \times k_{inact})/[I]_g + K_i]); C_g is induction of gut CYP enzymes, C_g = 1 + d \times E_{max} \times [I]_g/[I]_g + EC_{50}. F_{u,p} is the unbound fraction in plasma, a value of 0.0465 was used. F_a is the fraction absorbed after oral administration, a value of 1 was used. F_g is the fraction available after intestinal metabolism, a value of 1 was used. K_a is the first order absorption rate constant in vivo, a value of 0.0145 min^{-1} was used based on popPK modeling. Q_{en} is the blood flow through enterocytes, a value of 0.3 L/min was used. Q_h is the hepatic blood flow, a value of 1.45 L/min was used. RB is the blood-to-plasma concentration ratio, a value of 0.813 was used. d is a scaling factor determined in a calibrated hepatocyte batch based on positive control inducers. If not determined, it is assumed to be 1.
L665: 6 R-value for reversible inhibition calculated as R = 1 + C_{max,ss,u}/K_i.
L666: 7 IC_{50} values converted to K_i (K_i = IC_{50}/(1+[S]/K_m)).
L667: 8 Combined effect of hepatic and intestinal DDI using the net effect approach
L668: Abbreviations: AUCR, ratio of area under the concentration-time profile; BCRP, breast cancer resistance protein; BID, twice daily; BSEP, bile salt export pump; C_{max,ss}, maximum plasma concentration at steady state; CYP, cytochrome P450; DDI, drug-drug interaction; DMPK, drug metabolism and pharmacokinetics; EC_{50}, half maximal effective concentration; E_{max}, maximum effect; IC_{50}, half-maximal inhibitory concentration; K_i, inhibition constant; MATE, multidrug and toxin compound extrusion; MRP2, multidrug resistance-associated protein 2; NA, not applicable; NTCP, sodium taurocholate cotransporting polypeptide; OAT, organic anion transporter; OATP, organic anion transporting polypeptide; OCT, organic cation transporter; P-gp, p-glycoprotein; popPK, population pharmacokinetic
L669: ## Antithrombotic Agents
L670: The risks and benefits of coadministration of antithrombotic agents with remibrutinib should be considered due to BTK inhibitor drug class effect which inhibits the platelet activation. Refer to safety review issue in Section 7.7.1.
L671: ## **8.2.3. Effect of Other Drugs on Remibrutinib**
L672: In vitro data indicate that metabolism of remibrutinib is predominantly driven by CYP3A4 with a minor contribution from CYP2C19. In vitro studies also determined that remibrutinib is a substrate for P-gp, but not for other efflux and uptake transporters. For additional details, refer to Section 14.1.
L673: The effect of other drugs on remibrutinib as an object was evaluated in two clinical DDI studies, X2103 and A02103. In Study X2103, the impact of ritonavir (CYP3A4/P-gp inhibitor) and grapefruit juice (intestinal CYP3A4 inhibitor) were evaluated. In Study A02103, the impact of carbamazepine (CYP3A4 inducer) was assessed. For additional details, refer to Section 14.2.6.1 and Section 14.2.6.2. PBPK modeling and simulation was also used to predict the impact of moderate and strong CYP3A4 inhibitors and inducers on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
L674: ## CYP3A Induction or Inhibition
L675: Use of remibrutinib with moderate or strong CYP3A4 inducers and inhibitors should be avoided. Refer to safety review issue in Section 7.7.6.
L676: ## P-gp Inhibition
L677: In vitro studies suggest that remibrutinib exhibits high passive permeability at concentrations of 50\muM and above, while at lower concentrations (e.g., 0.5\muM), its permeability is reduced due to
L678: an apically directed P-gp-mediated efflux process. When remibrutinib is administered at 25 mg, the intestinal local concentration of remibrutinib is expected to be approximately 197\muM, suggesting a high passive permeability at the recommended dose of 25 mg twice daily.
L679: Based on clinical human absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion (ADME) data, unchanged remibrutinib was not detected in feces following intravenous administration. Following oral administration, the amount of unchanged remibrutinib in feces was 1.21% of the oral dose. Therefore, DDI effects mediated via P-gp inhibition are not expected.
L680: ## Acid-Reducing Agents
L681: No clinically relevant changes in remibrutinib exposure are expected when remibrutinib is coadministered with acid-reducing agents (ARAs).
L682: Remibrutinib is a weak base with pH-dependent solubility and, thus, remibrutinib absorption may be reduced at higher pH (e.g., coadministration of ARAs). The proportion of subjects who coadministered remibrutinib with proton-pump inhibitor (PPIs) (e.g., omeprazole, esomeprazole) in Trials A2201, A2301, and A2302 were 11% (N=24), 13% (N=53), and 12% (N=45), respectively. In addition, 27 (12%), 75 (19%), and 51 (14%) subjects coadministered remibrutinib with antacids in Trials A2201, A2301, and A2302, respectively.
L683: Based on dose-normalized observed C_{\text{max,ss}} of remibrutinib, there was no difference in exposure to remibrutinib between subjects who coadministered remibrutinib with ARAs and subjects who did not coadminister ARAs (Figure 15). Refer to Section 14.5.1 for details.
L684: **Figure 15. Plot of Remibrutinib Dose-Normalized Observed C_{\text{max,ss}} Concentrations (Log Scale) vs. the Use of Acid-Reducing Agents**

L687: Most common antacids used as comedication in Trials A2201, A2301, and A2302 include calcium carbonate, magnesium oxide/sodium alginate/sodium bicarbonate, aluminum hydroxide/magnesium carbonate, aluminum hydroxide/magnesium hydroxide/simethicone, aluminum hydroxide, and magnesium oxide.

L690: ## 8.3. Plans for Pediatric Drug Development
L691: The Applicant has an agreed upon pediatric study plan that includes deferred pediatric studies in patients 12 to &lt;18 years of age and 6 to &lt;12 years of age and a partial waiver of studies in children under 6 years of age because studies would be highly impracticable in this age group. Although CSU development programs may evaluate adolescents in the phase 3 trials in adults, the Agency advised the Applicant to defer pediatric studies until the benefit/risk in adults was better characterized given the novelty of the mechanistic target for this indication, the symptomatic and self-limited nature of the condition, and the potential risks associated with BTKi.
L692: Modeling and Simulation Activity Part 1 will use adult data to select appropriate doses for adolescents, while Part 2 will incorporate data from both adult and adolescent studies to determine optimal dosing for children aged 6 to 11 years.
L693: For adolescents aged 12 to less than 18 years, the plan includes Study CLOU064F12301, a double-blind, placebo-controlled, nonconfirmatory study designed to evaluate pharmacokinetics, efficacy, and safety of remibrutinib. This study will enroll at least 75 adolescent subjects with CSU who have inadequate response to H1-antihistamines, with coprimary endpoints of absolute change from baseline in ISS7 and HSS7 at Week 12. The study includes a 24-week treatment period followed by a 4-week follow-up period and was ongoing at the time of the original NDA review.
L694: For children aged 6 to less than 12 years, Study CLOU064F12302 is planned as an open-label, uncontrolled trial focusing primarily on pharmacokinetics
L695: Given ocular and neurobehavioral findings in the completed juvenile toxicity study in rats, an additional PREA PMR will be issued for a repeat juvenile toxicity study in older rats that approximate children 6 years of age. The results of the juvenile rat study will need to be submitted and reviewed prior to initiation of clinical study CLOU064F12302.
L696: Age-appropriate formulation development is planned,
L697: with final selection based on stability, dose flexibility, and palatability criteria.
L698: The Pediatric Research Equity Act PMRs to be issued at the time of approval action are discussed in Section 24.

L703: Labeling Section	Nonclinical Data
Pregnancy	In pregnant rabbits administered remibrutinib during organogenesis, remibrutinib did not cause adverse effects to the fetus at exposures up to 23 times the MRHD.1,2 Increased fetal external malformations (open/opaque eyes, small jaws, hyperflexion of forelimbs) and maternal toxicity (transiently reduced food consumption and adverse clinical signs) occurred at 141 times the MRHD.
In pregnant rats administered remibrutinib during organogenesis, remibrutinib did not cause adverse effects to the fetus at exposures up to 126 times the MRHD.3
In a pre- and postnatal development study in rats, remibrutinib did not cause adverse effects for maternal animals or offspring at exposures up to 58 times the MRHD in males and females, respectively.4

8.3 Females and Males of Reproductive Potential	In a fertility study in male and female rats, remibrutinib had no effect on fertility and reproductive indices up to 15 times and 63 times the MRHD in males and females, respectively.5
Remibrutinib can cause teratogenicity and maternal toxicity when administered orally to rabbits during organogenesis (see Section 8.1).

13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility	Remibrutinib did not show evidence of carcinogenic potential in a 6-month rath2 mouse study at oral doses up to 1500 mg/kg/day6 and a 2-year rat carcinogenicity study at oral doses up to 8x and 98x the MRHD based on AUC for males and females, respectively.7
Remibrutinib was not mutagenic in a bacterial mutagenicity (Ames) assay and was not clastogenic in an in vitro micronucleus assay in human peripheral lymphocytes or in an in vivo blood reticulocyte micronucleus assay in rats.
Fertility data are described in Section 8.3 above.

L722: ## **Pregnancy, Lactation, and Reproductive Potential Assessment**
L723: The review team consulted the Division of Pediatrics and Maternal Health (DPMH) to evaluate the proposed pregnancy and lactation labeling. For more details, refer to the DPMH consult memo dated August 28, 2025, in DARRTS (Reference ID 5649755).
L724: The phase 3 trials excluded pregnant and lactating women from enrollment; however, nine pregnancies occurred across ongoing and completed studies, with treatment discontinued upon discovery. Five of the nine pregnancies had a known outcome: two full-term healthy infants, one spontaneous abortion in a 33-year-old subject with risk factors (obesity and oral contraceptive use) that was not attributed to study treatment, and two elective abortions due to subject decision with no additional information provided.
L725: The results of DPMH's literature search using terms related to BTK inhibitors and pregnancy outcomes revealed limited data to inform remibrutinib use during pregnancy. Based on the literature review and animal data, DPMH concluded that available clinical pregnancy data are insufficient to identify drug-associated risks. DPMH did not recommend including language in Warnings and Precautions for embryo-fetal toxicity, but they recommended including the nonclinical data in Subsection 8.1 "Risk Summary" and "Data-Animal Data." Additional recommendations were to include a statement on the U.S. background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage at the end of the "Risk Summary" in Subsection 8.1, per the Pregnancy and Lactation Labeling Rule.
L726: For lactation considerations, the DPMH found no nonclinical or clinical data on remibrutinib's presence in animal or human milk, nor any data on its effects on breastfed infants or milk production. Despite the likelihood of remibrutinib transfer into human milk based on the drug's characteristics, DPMH determined that remibrutinib does not share the same severe adverse event profile as other BTK inhibitors and recommended including standard developmental benefit-risk language in labeling Subsection 8.2.
L727: DPMH did not recommend including labeling Subsection 8.3 because there are no concerns for embryo-fetal toxicity when remibrutinib is used at clinically relevant doses and no fertility concerns based on animal fertility studies.
L728: Because animal reproduction studies with remibrutinib suggested malformations at high dose multiples, animal reproduction studies in other drugs in the class have demonstrated embryofetal toxicity at clinically relevant doses and remibrutinib is systemically absorbed and likely to be transferred to the fetus, DPMH recommended issuing PMRs for a pregnancy registry and database study. Additionally, there is the potential for remibrutinib use during lactation, for which DPMH recommended a PMR for a clinical lactation study (see Section 24).

L752: ## Biopharmaceutics
L753: The Applicant is seeking approval for the proposed drug product remibrutinib film-coated tablets, 25 mg, via the 505(b)(1) pathway. It is an immediate-release dosage form for oral administration. The clinical development of remibrutinib for CSU has been conducted under IND 131325. Remibrutinib is a new molecular entity, and it is the free form of a weak base showing high permeability and low solubility at physiological pH (Biopharmaceutics Classification System Class 2). The Applicant developed 25-mg, 50-mg, and 100-mg strengths but this NDA only includes the 25-mg strength. All strengths have formulation proportionality.
L754: The Applicant requested to approve RHAPSIDO as the proprietary name for the proposed drug product on May 15, 2023, under IND 1313251 and it was conditionally approved by the Agency on October 31, 2023. There is no dissolution method listed in FDA dissolution methods database or United States Pharmacopeia monograph. The Applicant developed an in-house method (Apparatus 1/basket with 900 mL of 0.1 N-hydrochloric acid, at 100 rpm, 37\pm 0.5^\circ\text{C}) and provided dissolution method development data and discriminating ability data.
L755: The proposed dissolution acceptance criterion is Q(b)(4)% in 20 minutes. Based on the dissolution data provided in quality control (QC) media, all exhibit batches comply with the proposed acceptance criterion. The proposed dissolution method and acceptance criterion are acceptable from biopharmaceutics perspective.

L778: ## 13. Pharmacology Toxicology
L779: ## 13.1. Summary Review of Studies Submitted With the Investigational New Drug Application
L780: The Applicant has conducted a comprehensive program of pharmacology, PK, and toxicology studies to support clinical development and the NDA submission of remibrutinib (referred to during development as LOU064). All pivotal toxicity studies have been submitted and reviewed previously under IND 131325. The impurities as well as some pharmacology studies have been reviewed herein under NDA 218436. The key findings from the nonclinical studies are cited and summarized in this review to support the approval of NDA 218436.
L781: Pharmacological studies demonstrated that remibrutinib inhibited both unphosphorylated and phosphorylated BTK with a &gt;200-fold selectivity for BTK over BTK related enzymes TEC and BMX based on IC_{50} evaluation in enzymatic kinase assays. In cell-based assays, the inhibition of BTK was associated with reduced lymphocyte activation, inhibition of Fc\gamma R and Fc\epsilon R, and reduced secretion of IL-6. Studies also demonstrated remibrutinib inhibition of collagen-induced platelet aggregation. In vivo, remibrutinib showed a response in two models of skin mast cell degranulation induced by Fc\gamma R and Fc\epsilon R mediated hypersensitivity, a mouse reverse passive Arthus model, and a mouse passive cutaneous anaphylaxis model. These models are relevant for CSU.
L782: The Applicant has conducted subchronic and chronic toxicology studies with remibrutinib in both rats and dogs. Target organs of toxicity were identified as the lymph nodes (underdeveloped germinal centers, sinusoidal red blood cells), thyroid gland (follicular hypertrophy), liver (hepatocellular hypertrophy, multinucleated hepatocytes, pigment), pancreas (islet fibrosis, pigment), and blood (prolongation of bleeding, inhibition of platelet aggregation). The nonclinical findings were not considered clinically dose-limiting. The nonclinical general toxicology studies provided sufficient support for the clinical development program.
L783: In animal reproduction studies, remibrutinib was nonteratogenic and did not affect fertility in rats. Pregnant rats dosed with remibrutinib during the period of organogenesis did not show fetal malformations at doses up to 1000 mg/kg/day (approximately 126x the MRHD). In pregnant rabbits, remibrutinib treatment resulted in signs of maternal toxicity (clinical signs of toxicity, weight loss, and decreased food consumption) and adverse developmental outcomes (open/opaque eyes, small jaws, hyperflexion of forelimbs) at doses \ge 300 mg/kg/day (approximately 141x the MRHD). In a pre- and postnatal development study in rats, potentially remibrutinib-related findings were observed at the highest dose of 1000 mg/kg/day dose (approximately 194x the MRHD). Toxicity in two maternal rats resulted in early euthanasia of these animals with one animal exhibiting reduced food consumption, reduced body weight, and poor clinical signs followed by delivery of five stillborn pups and five small, cold, weak pups that were terminated on Postnatal Day 0 to 1. In the second animal, poor clinical signs were observed along with total litter loss. Overall, the 1000 mg/kg/day dose group exhibited a reduced litter size (-11.8\% compared to controls), an increase in the number of stillborn pups (15 versus 1 in the control and 300 mg/kg/day dose groups), and an increase in the number of pup deaths. No adverse remibrutinib-related changes were observed in maternal rats and offspring at doses of \le 300 mg/kg/day (approximately 58x the MRHD).
L784: ## NDA 218436
## RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
L785: Remibrutinib was not mutagenic in in vitro or in vivo assays. A 2-year oral study in Wistar rats and a 6-month oral study in Tg.rasH2 mice were conducted to assess the carcinogenic potential of remibrutinib. No evidence of tumorigenicity was observed in male and female Tg.rasH2 mice at doses up to 1500 mg/kg/day or in rats at exposures approximately 8x and 98x the MRHD in males and female rats, respectively.
L786: To conclude, a complete and adequate nonclinical program was submitted, and NDA 218436 is recommended for approval from the nonclinical perspective.
L787: ## Pharmacology
L788: Remibrutinib is a small molecule inhibitor of BTK that inhibits human BTK with nanomolar potency (IC_{50}: 0.6 to 7.5nM) and was generally more selective for BTK compared to the BTK-related enzymes TEC and BMX (IC_{50}: 124 to 141nM).
L789: In vivo, optimized-for-aging (OFA)-1 and Wistar rats were used to assess BTK target occupancy in the spleen and blood following changes to dose, exposure, and time. Following doses up to 10-mg/kg remibrutinib, these studies demonstrated high occupancy in the spleen and blood, and corresponding blood levels ranging from 2 to 71nM. Blood levels of remibrutinib were maximal by 0.5 hours postdose while BTK occupancy exhibited different kinetics in the blood and spleen with an estimated BTK half-life of 87 hours in the rat blood and 5 to 8 hours in the spleen.
L790: In a mouse reverse passive Arthus model of a local type III hypersensitivity reaction, oral doses up to 100 mg/kg of remibrutinib were administered prior to induction of the Arthus response. In this model, remibrutinib pretreatment resulted in reduced skin swelling. In a BALB/c mouse passive cutaneous anaphylaxis (PCA) model, oral remibrutinib administration at doses up to 100 mg/kg inhibited PCA at all doses while a minimal effect was observed at high-dose IgE sensitization. These results suggest that remibrutinib was effective following low-dose IgE sensitization in this model of anaphylaxis-driven skin edema relevant to urticaria.
L791: In a model of B-cell response to sheep red blood cells (SRBC) in OFA rats, remibrutinib was administered at daily doses up to 3 mg/kg for 3 days following immunization with SRBC. Following dosing, remibrutinib dose dependently decreased the number of SRBC-specific IgM secreting B-cells by 66 to 96%. This model showed correlation between remibrutinib pharmacokinetics, tissue BTK (spleen and blood) occupancy, and a PD effect.
L792: ## Safety Pharmacology
L793: A complete battery of safety pharmacology studies has been conducted with remibrutinib. The remibrutinib IC_{50} at human ether-à-go-go-related gene channels expressed in HEK293 cells was calculated to be 1.4\muM. The effects of remibrutinib on central nervous system function and the respiratory system were examined using a functional observation battery in a single-dose oral gavage study in Wistar rats. There were no effects on autonomic, neurologic, behavioral observations or for respiratory rate, tidal volume, minute volume, or peak inspiratory or expiratory flow observed at up to 1000 mg/kg. The effects of remibrutinib on cardiovascular system function were examined in a single-dose oral gavage telemetry study in Beagle dogs. No effects on body temperatures, heart rates, arterial pressures, or ECG parameters (including PQ, RR, QRS, QT, and QTv) were observed at doses up to 450 mg/kg.
L794: ## **Absorption, Distribution, Metabolism, Excretion**
L795: In rats, remibrutinib was rapidly absorbed, with a bioavailability of 7 to 29% after oral administration. A single oral dose of remibrutinib resulted in broad distribution in all tissues except for the brain, lens of the eye, spinal cord, and white fat, with a T_{max} of 0.5 to 8 hours. Similar distribution was noted in both sexes. Specific retention of total radiolabeled components in the pigmented uveal tract/retina was observed. The highest exposures (based on AUC_{last}) were observed for the kidney medulla, small intestine mucosa, skin, cecum mucosa, kidney cortex, liver, and uveal tract/retina. Following IV or oral administration in rats, remibrutinib appeared to be eliminated via metabolism with &lt;4% of the parent compound detected in excreta and a pattern of metabolites that was qualitatively and quantitatively similar.
L796: In dogs, a single oral dose of remibrutinib resulted in moderate absorption (41.9%) with a bioavailability of 13.5% and a median T_{max} of 2 hours. Remibrutinib was distributed into tissues (volume of distribution at steady state [V_{ss}]=1.26 L/kg) and exhibited high clearance ([CL]=187 L/h/kg). Excretion occurred mainly in the feces, with excretion by urine representing a minor fraction.
L797: Remibrutinib was associated with rapid metabolism in the mouse, rat, dog, and human, with 31 metabolites identified across all species. The major metabolic pathways consisted of N-demethylation, followed by glutathione or cysteine adducts and hydrogenation leading to M11 and M17 formation observed in the mouse, rat, and human, or O-dealkylation leading to M22 observed in all species, and glucuronidation leading to M7 observed in all species. Two metabolites formed by C-hydroxylation (M25) and C-hydroxylation and N-hydroxylation (M27) were only detected in in vitro human hepatocyte incubation with only M25 also observed in vivo at levels of 1.12% of the exposure based on AUC_{0-24h}. There were no major human metabolites identified.
L798: Very low levels of degradation were observed for remibrutinib following incubation in blood or plasma from the rat, dog, or human for up to 2 hours with preferential distribution to the plasma in all tested species and blood distribution appearing to be concentration independent. Remibrutinib highly bound to plasma proteins with no concentration dependence: 89.8%, 92.8%, and 95.4% in the rat, dog, and human, respectively. Formation of covalent drug-protein adducts was observed in human liver microsomes and human hepatocytes. Coincubation with glutathione in human liver microsomes resulted in a 55% decrease in covalent drug-protein adducts, suggesting that glutathione-mediated activation of remibrutinib contributes to the formation of these adducts. Formation of covalent drug-protein adducts was time-dependent in human, dog, and rat blood and plasma, with adduct formation higher in plasma than blood, and with adduct formation in rat and dog plasma higher than that observed in human plasma. Remibrutinib covalently bound plasma proteins without metabolic activation at high levels. Using recombinant enzymes or selective enzymatic inhibitors (ketoconazole and azamulin), CYP3A4 was determined to be the major human liver enzyme implicated in oxidative metabolism of remibrutinib in the liver with potential contributions for CYP1A1 and CYP2C19.
L799: ## **General Toxicology**
L800: As listed in the table below, general toxicity studies with remibrutinib were conducted in rats for up to 26 weeks and in dogs for up to 39 weeks. The pivotal studies have been reviewed previously under IND 131325 and are listed below.

L805: ## **Repeat-Dose Toxicology Studies in Rats**
L806: ## **A 2-Week Rat Study (1470733; 42325 TSR)**
L807: In a 2-week oral repeat-dose toxicity study in Wistar rats (10/sex/group), animals were administered doses of 0 (vehicle), 30, 100, 300, and 1000 mg/kg/day of remibrutinib. Recovery groups (5/sex for the control and 1000 mg/kg/day dose groups) were included to assess reversibility of the findings. Mortality was observed in one female administered 100 mg/kg/day of remibrutinib which was considered potentially related to test article induced prolongation of bleeding during a tail-tip bleeding test. Target tissues were identified as the blood, lymph nodes (decreased paracortex in mesenteric, sinusoidal red blood cells), spleen (decreased periarteriolar lymphoid sheaths and decreases in marginal zone), thyroid (minimal follicular hypertrophy), and liver (increased mitosis, periportal vacuolation, focal necrosis, and inflammatory cell foci). Mild, reversible increases in WBC, lymphocytes (including B-cells and T-cells) were observed in both sexes administered \ge 100 mg/kg/day of remibrutinib. Administration of remibrutinib was also associated with inhibition of collagen-induced platelet aggregation and prolonged bleeding time observed in both sexes at all doses. Due to changes in hemostasis at \ge 30 mg/kg/day, the Applicant did not establish any no-observed-adverse-effect level (NOAEL) in this study; however, given the hemostatic effects are related to the pharmacology of remibrutinib and are monitorable, a NOAEL was considered to be 30 mg/kg/day with an associated Day 14 AUC0-24h of 13000 ng*h/mL in males and 31900 ng*h/mL in females.
L808: ## **A 13-Week Rat Study (1570128; 43328 TCR)**
L809: In a 13-week good laboratory practice (GLP) toxicity study, Wistar rats (10/sex/group) were dosed by oral gavage with 0 (vehicle), 10, 100, and 1000 mg/kg/day remibrutinib. No recovery groups were included in the study. Following administration of remibrutinib, rats exhibited mild immune suppression manifested by decreased circulating B-cells (6 to 32%) in all doses in both sexes and microscopic changes in lymphoid tissues, including decreased cellularity in the mandibular and mesenteric lymph nodes coupled with increased frequency of sinusoidal red blood cells and decreased periarteriolar lymphoid sheaths and reduction in marginal zones in the spleen. These effects were attributed to the pharmacology of the test article and are monitorable in the clinical setting. In addition to the immunosuppression-related findings, the rats dosed with \ge 100 mg/kg/day of remibrutinib exhibited changes in the thyroid (follicular hypertrophy) and liver (hepatocellular hypertrophy). No NOAEL was established by the Applicant in this study; however, given the hemostatic effects are related to the pharmacology of remibrutinib and are
L810: monitorable, a NOAEL was considered to be 1000 mg/kg/day with an associated Day 89 AUC0-24h of 4780 ng*h/mL in males and 52800 ng*h/mL in females.
L811: ## A 26-Week Rat Study (1670372; 45306 TCR)
L812: In a 26-week GLP toxicity study, Wistar rats (20/sex/group) were dosed by oral gavage with 0, 30, 300, and 1000 mg/kg/day remibrutinib. The 300 and/or 1000 mg/kg/day groups showed dose-related increases in liver weights and microscopic changes in the liver, lymph nodes, pancreas, and thyroid glands, with females showing more pronounced effects than males. The following findings were noted in the females: the liver showed minimal hepatocellular hypertrophy, multinucleated hepatocytes, and pigment generally in the 1000 mg/kg/day dose group. The percent of rats with hepatocellular hypertrophy was 10% (2/20) and 65% (13/20) in the 300 and 1000 mg/kg/day dose groups, respectively, accompanied by increases in absolute mean liver weights (by 18% and 51%, respectively). The lymph nodes (both mandibular and mesenteric lymph nodes) showed less developed germinal centers and sinusoidal red blood cells. The pancreas showed minimal islet fibrosis and pigment, which have been reported to be rat-specific effects of BTK inhibitors. The thyroid glands showed follicular hypertrophy. The NOAEL was 30 mg/kg/day with an associated Day 176 AUC0-24h of 5970 ng*h/mL in males and 30900 ng*h/mL in females.
L813: ## Repeat-Dose Toxicology Studies in Dogs
L814: ## A 2-Week Dog Study (1470732; 42326 TSC)
L815: In a 2-week GLP oral repeat-dose toxicity study in beagle dogs, animals (3/sex/group) were administered doses of 0 (vehicle), 50, 200, and 450 mg/kg/day of remibrutinib. No recovery groups were included in the study. Target tissues were identified as the blood and lymph nodes, with alterations in hemostasis including prolongation of bleeding time and inhibition of ADP- and collagen-induced platelet aggregation. Microscopic findings considered potentially related to the test article were limited to sinusoidal red blood cells observed in both the mandibular and mesenteric lymph nodes in both sexes in all dose groups that correlated with macroscopic observations of red discoloration. Exposures of remibrutinib increased in a generally dose-proportional manner from 50 to 200 mg/kg/day with no obvious increase in exposure levels from 200 to 450 mg/kg/day suggesting saturation of the test article. High variability was observed; however, exposures were higher in females than males (by 1.9- to 5.4-fold). The NOAEL was considered the highest dose of 450 mg/kg/day, though higher exposures were noted at 200 mg/kg/day, resulting in a mean AUC0-24h of 10400 ng*h/mL in males and a mean AUC0-24h of 56300 ng*h/mL in females on Day 13.
L816: ## A 13-Week Dog Study (1570127; 43329 TCC)
L817: In a 13-week GLP oral repeat-dose toxicity study in beagle dogs, animals (3/sex/group) were administered doses of 0 (vehicle), 10, 50, and 300 mg/kg/day remibrutinib. No recovery groups were included in the study. Following administration of remibrutinib, dogs exhibited mild immune suppression manifested by decreased circulating B-cells (42 to 80%) in all doses in both sexes and microscopic changes in lymphoid tissues in all doses in both sexes, including increased frequencies of sinusoidal red blood cells in the mandibular and mesenteric lymph nodes and decreased germinal center development in the gut-associated lymphoid tissue. These effects were attributed to the pharmacology of the test article and are monitorable in the clinical
L818: setting. Exposures increased in a less than dose-proportional manner with slightly (&lt;2-fold) increased exposures noted in females at \ge 50 mg/kg/day. The NOAEL was considered the highest dose of 300 mg/kg/day with an associated mean AUC0-24h of 6350 and 9310 ng*h/mL in males and females, respectively, on Days 87 to 89.
L819: ## A 39-Week Dog Study (1670373; 45307 TCC
L820: In a 39-week GLP oral repeat-dose toxicity study in beagle dogs, animals (4/sex/group) were administered doses of 0 (vehicle), 10, 50, and 300 mg/kg/day remibrutinib. No recovery groups were included in the study. Microscopic findings were observed in the mesenteric lymph node (decreased cellularity and decreases in germinal center development) and gut-associated lymphoid tissue (decreases in germinal center development). Red discoloration was observed in the cecum, duodenum, ileum, iliac lymph nodes, and mesenteric lymph nodes in several animals from remibrutinib-treated dose groups and a red mass observed in the gastric lymph node in a single male from the 300-mg/kg/day dose group. In the mandibular and mesenteric lymph nodes, this observation correlated with microscopic findings of minimal to moderate sinusoidal red blood cells in both sexes and at all doses (including controls) with increasing severity generally associating with dose. Increased fibrinogen was observed in some animals at \ge 50 mg/kg/day. In the liver, there was increased incidence and severity of pigment in remibrutinib-treated groups compared to controls with increased intensity of positive Schmorl's stain (indicates the presence of melanin, argentaffin, chromaffin, bile, and formalin pigment) in the 300-mg/kg/day dose group compared to controls, suggestive of lipofuscin, which is considered nonadverse. The NOAEL was considered to be 300 mg/kg/day with an associated combined male and female Cmax and AUC0-24h of 4735 ng/mL and 43550 ng*h/mL, respectively.
L821: ## Toxicokinetics and Exposure Margins
L822: Adequate exposure margins between the nonclinical general toxicology studies and proposed human doses of remibrutinib were obtained throughout the clinical development program.

L830: ## Genetic Toxicology
L831: Remibrutinib was considered negative for genotoxicity in an Ames test, an in vitro micronucleus assay in peripheral human lymphocytes, and an in vivo micronucleus assay in rats.
L832: ## Carcinogenicity
L833: ## **Mouse Carcinogenicity Study (1870307; 8411537)**
L834: A 26-week oral carcinogenicity study of remibrutinib in Tg.rasH2 mice showed no evidence of carcinogenicity in mice (25/sex/dose) dosed by oral gavage with 0 (water), 0 (vehicle), 150, 500, and 1500-mg/kg/day remibrutinib once daily. The Agency concurred with the dose selection for the study on December 2, 2019. Remibrutinib had no effects on survival rates. The remibrutinib treatment groups did not exhibit increases in the incidence of tumor findings. Non-neoplastic findings included decreases in germinal center cellularity in the spleen and mesenteric lymph nodes and findings of hepatocyte cytoplasmic eosinophilic inclusions, karyocytomegaly, and/or multinucleated hepatocytes in the liver. A positive control group (N-methyl-N-nitrosourea; 75 mg/kg/day) showed expected increases in tumor incidences in the hemolymphoreticular tissue, stomach, and skin. Remibrutinib was negative for carcinogenicity under these study conditions.
L835: ## **Rat Carcinogenicity Study (1870308; 8425081)**
L836: A 104-week oral carcinogenicity study of remibrutinib in Wistar rats (50/sex/dose) showed no evidence of carcinogenicity at oral doses of 0 (water), 0 (vehicle), 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day remibrutinib once daily. The Agency concurred with the dose selection for the study on September 23, 2019. Remibrutinib had no effects on survival rates. No remibrutinib-related neoplastic changes were noted up to the highest tested dose of 300 mg/kg/day. Non-neoplastic microscopic findings included findings in the lymphoid tissues (sinusoidal red blood cells, sinusoidal dilatation, focal angiomatous hyperplasia, diffuse reticuloendothelial hypertrophy/hyperplasia, and/or pigmented macrophages), thyroid (follicular cell hyperplasia), and pancreas (islet fibrosis). Remibrutinib was negative for carcinogenicity under these study conditions.

L843: ## Reproductive Toxicology
L844: Remibrutinib did not affect fertility and was nonteratogenic in rats. In rabbits, remibrutinib treatment resulted in maternal toxicity and adverse developmental outcomes at doses \ge 300 mg/kg/day. In the rat pre- and postnatal development study, remibrutinib treatment resulted in increased number of stillborn pups, dead pups, and a smaller litter size at 1000 mg/kg/day.
L845: ## **Fertility Study (1770589)**
L846: In a fertility study, male and female rats (Wistar, 24/sex/dose) were dosed by oral gavage with 0, 100, 300, or 1000 mg/kg/day remibrutinib. Males were dosed for 9 weeks starting from 28 days
L847: prior to mating and through mating. Females were dosed for 14 days prior to mating, through mating, and until Gestation Day (GD) 6. Females were sacrificed on GD 13 for uterine content examinations. No treatment-related findings were observed in any of the fertility parameters evaluated. Therefore, the NOAEL was 1000 mg/kg/day.
L848: ## Embryo-Fetal Development Studies (1670380; 1670382)
L849: In an embryo-fetal developmental study (1670380), pregnant Wistar rats (22/dose) were dosed by oral gavage with 0, 100, 300, or 1000 mg/kg/day remibrutinib during GDs 6 to 17, sacrificed on GD 21, and examined for fetal malformations and variations. No treatment-related effects were observed in any of the key parameters. The NOAEL was 1000 mg/kg/day, corresponding to a C_{max} of 4370 ng/mL and AUC0-24h of 49200 ng*h/mL.
L850: In another embryo-fetal developmental study (1670382), pregnant rabbits (New Zealand White, 20/dose) were dosed by oral gavage with 0, 100, 300, or 450 mg/kg/day remibrutinib during GDs 7 to 20, sacrificed on GD 29, and examined for fetal malformations and variations. Mortality (euthanasia) occurred at \ge 300 mg/kg/day. One doe administered 300 mg/kg/day and the entire group of does administered 450 mg/kg/day were terminated during the period of GD 14 to 22 due to severe toxicities including abortion, clinical signs, weight loss, and decreases in feed consumption. The clinical signs included subdued/decreased activity, cold to touch, irregular respiration, decreased fecal output, pale pinnae, partially closed eyes, and red liquid material. The 450-mg/kg/day dose group was excluded from the analysis of fetal malformations and variations due to severe maternal toxicity. The 300-mg/kg/day dose group showed slight but statistically nonsignificant increases in a few external malformations such as eye opening, small jaws, and hyperflexion of forelimbs. No increases in the incidence of malformations or variations were observed in the 100-mg/kg/day dose group. The NOAEL was 100 mg/kg/day, corresponding to a C_{max} of 2360 ng/mL and AUC0-24h of 8990 ng*h/mL.
L851: ## Pre- and Postnatal Development Study (2070356)
L852: In a pre- and postnatal development study, pregnant rats received daily oral doses of remibrutinib at 0 (vehicle), 100, 300, and 1000 mg/kg/day from GD 6 to Lactation Day 21. Potentially remibrutinib-related findings were observed for the 1000-mg/kg/day dose group. Toxicity in two maternal rats resulted in early euthanasia of these animals, with one animal exhibiting reduced food consumption, reduced body weight, and poor clinical signs followed by delivery of five stillborn pups and five small, cold, weak pups that were terminated on Postnatal Days 0 to 1. In the second animal, poor clinical signs were observed along with total litter loss. Overall, the 1000-mg/kg/day dose group exhibited a reduced litter size (-11.8% compared to controls), an increase in the number of stillborn pups (15 versus 1 in the control and 300-mg/kg/day dose groups), and an increase in the number of pup deaths. No adverse remibrutinib-related changes were observed in maternal rats and offspring at doses of \le 300 mg/kg/day. Therefore, the NOAEL was identified at 300 mg/kg/day.

L862: ## 13.2. Individual Reviews of Studies Submitted
## With the New Drug Application
L863: ## Pharmacology
L864: ## In Vitro Binding and Cellular Activity Study (RD-2014-00811)
L865: Pharmacology studies submitted by the Applicant evaluated the effect of remibrutinib in vitro and in vivo. In these studies, remibrutinib generally demonstrated inhibition of both unphosphorylated and phosphorylated BTK with IC50s that were &gt;200-fold lower than those observed for the related enzymes TEC and BMX (0.6 to 7.5nM versus 124 to 141nM), with no demonstrated inhibition of ITK or TXK. In the same study using a kinase panel, remibrutinib demonstrated inhibition of BTK with an IC50 of 1.1nM and PI4Kb with an IC50 of 3.1µM with no apparent inhibition of other protein and lipid kinases. In an in vitro off-target screening panel, remibrutinib exhibited minimal off-target effects with inhibition of NaV1.5, human BSEP, and the rat transporter VMAT-2 observed with IC50s &gt;3µM.
L866: In Ramos B-cell cultures, remibrutinib inhibited human phosphorylated BTK (pY551), cluster of differentiation (CD69 and CD86, and IL-6 secretion. In a human monocytic cell line, Tohoku Hospital Pediatrics-1 (THP-1) cells, remibrutinib inhibited FcγR signaling, while less potent inhibition was observed in polymorphonuclear leukocytes. This finding is consistent with the relative BTK expression levels in these cell types. Remibrutinib exhibited only minimal effects on proliferation of THP-1 cells (IC50 of 35µM), and in murine Ba/F3 cells including IL-13-independent subclones (IC50 &gt;11µM; except for Ba/F3 transformed by BMX [IC50 of 5.5µM]), and mouse bone marrow cells (IC50 of 1.97µM). Remibrutinib inhibited the mouse and human mixed lymphocyte reaction with IC50s of &lt;4.6 and 3nM, respectively. The epidermal growth factor receptor signaling did show evidence of inhibition (IC50 &gt;60µM) after prolonged in vitro incubation (5 hours).

L871: Remibrutinib also inhibited collagen-induced platelet aggregation at low but not high collagen concentrations, suggesting that BTK inhibition blocks collagen-induced platelet aggregation at low agonist concentrations.
L872: In an evaluation of tissue staining for human BTK using commercial antibodies, moderate to strong signals for BTK were generally observed in B-lymphocytes in the lymph node, spleen, tonsil, and thymic medulla with occasional staining of mast cells and plasma cells. Nonlymphoid tissues included epithelium (breast, renal tubules, respiratory, spleen), hepatocytes, and endometrial glands.
L873: ## **In Vivo PD Effects (BTK Target Occupancy; RD-2014-00816)**
L874: In vivo, OFA-1 and Wistar rats were used to assess BTK target occupancy in the spleen and blood using different doses, exposures, and times. Following doses of 1-, 3-, and 10-mg/kg remibrutinib, these studies demonstrated 75 to 99% occupancy in the spleen and 13 to 96% occupancy in the blood, with corresponding blood levels ranging from 2 to 71nM. Blood levels of remibrutinib were maximal by 0.5 hours postdose while BTK occupancy exhibited different kinetics in the blood and spleen with an estimated BTK half-life (t1/2) of 87 hours in the rat blood and 5 to 8 hours in the spleen. BTK occupancy in the spleen was not affected by the route of administration (subcutaneous or by mouth).
L875: ## **In Vivo Models Relevant to CSU**
L876: In a mouse reverse passive Arthus model of a local type III hypersensitivity reaction (Study RD-2014-00813) using oral doses of 3, 10, 30, and 100 mg/kg of remibrutinib administered prior to induction of the Arthus response, remibrutinib pretreatment resulted in dose-dependent increases in exposure that increased in a less than dose-proportional manner for 3 to 10 mg/kg but in a greater than dose-proportional manner from 10 to 100 mg/kg. Dose-dependent increases in BTK occupancy in the spleen were also evident. Reduced skin swelling in the mouse reverse passive Arthus model was observed with 30- and 100-mg/kg remibrutinib. Inhibition of swelling compared to vehicle was observed when remibrutinib was administered 2 to 16 hours prior to induction of the Arthus response with a maximal response observed at 2 hours and returning to vehicle control levels at 45 hours.
L877: In a BALB/c mouse PCA model (Study RD-2014-00912) where mast cells are activated via the FcεR by IgE and hapten-specific IgE, remibrutinib was administered at doses of 0 (vehicle), 3,
L878: 10, 30, and 100 mg/kg (by mouth) at two time points (4 hours after sensitization and 2 hours prior to hapten challenge). Following low dose IgE sensitization, remibrutinib administration inhibited PCA at all doses while a minimal effect was observed at high dose IgE sensitization for the high dose of 100-mg/kg remibrutinib. However, comparable blood concentrations of remibrutinib that were dose-proportional and comparable ~100% BTK occupancy in the spleen were observed with either approach. These results suggest that remibrutinib was effective following low-dose IgE sensitization in this model of anaphylaxis-driven skin edema relevant to urticaria.
L879: ## **In Vivo Correlation of Remibrutinib Pharmacokinetics, Tissue BTK Occupancy, and Mechanistic Pharmacodynamics**
L880: A model of B-cell response to SRBC in OFA rats (Study RD-2014-00858) was used to assess the correlation of remibrutinib pharmacokinetics, tissue BTK occupancy, and mechanistic PD efficacy. Remibrutinib was administered at daily doses of 0.3, 1, and 3 mg/kg for 3 days following immunization with SRBC. Following dosing, remibrutinib concentrations in the blood increased in a dose-proportional manner and were highest at 0.5 hours postdose with &lt;10% remaining at 5 hours postdose. Dose dependent increases in BTK occupancy in the spleen were evident with increasing doses (31 to 71.6%). Remibrutinib dose-dependently decreased the number of SRBC-specific IgM secreting B-cells by 66 to 96%.

L910: ## 14. Clinical Pharmacology
L911: ## 14.1. In Vitro Studies
L912: Clinical pharmacology-related in vitro studies are summarized in Table 37.
L913: The unbound maximum blood concentration at steady state (C_{max,ss,u}) for remibrutinib at the recommended dosage of 25 mg BID is 0.00595\mu M.
L914: **Table 37. Summary of In Vitro Studies**
L915: Report/Objectives	Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
Absorption and Transporters
DMPK R1500096:
Evaluating the permeability and the effect of efflux-transporters (MDR1, BCRP, and MRP2) on permeability of remibrutinib	
In Caco-2 cell monolayers, P_{app} efflux ratios for [^3H]-digoxin (1\mu M), [^3H]-propranolol (5\mu M), and [^{14}C]-mannitol (5\mu M) were 25.24, 1.77, 0.45, respectively. The P_{app} ratios for remibrutinib at 0.5\mu M and 100\mu M were 17.5 and 1.3, respectively, suggesting saturation of an apically directed transporter-mediated efflux process at higher concentrations of remibrutinib.
At a lower remibrutinib concentration (0.5\mu M), LY335979 (MDR1 [P-gp] inhibitor) significantly decreased P_{app} to 0.8. No significant changes in remibrutinib permeability were observed in the presence of BCRP and MRP2 inhibitors.
These findings suggest that remibrutinib exhibits high passive permeability at higher concentrations, while at lower concentrations, its permeability is reduced due to an apically directed P-gp-mediated efflux process (apparent K_m of 13.8\mu M). The contributions of BCRP and MRP2 to this efflux process appear to be negligible

DMPK R1500097:
Transport study of [^3H]-remibrutinib using cryopreserved human hepatocytes to evaluate the hepatic uptake of remibrutinib by OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1, and OAT2 and the potential impact of lysosomal trapping	
Human hepatocyte uptake of remibrutinib (0.38\mu M) occurs mostly by a passive permeation without modulation by a solute-carrier system. The measured (nonsaturable, passive) membrane permeability for remibrutinib was 47.3 \mu L/min/mg protein and the maximum transporter-mediated membrane permeability for remibrutinib was 0 \mu L/min/10^6 cells.
Inhibitors of OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT family, OCT family, NTCP, and lysosomal trapping did not impact the permeation of remibrutinib into hepatocytes.

Distribution
DMPK R2300145:
Evaluating the binding of [^{14}C]-remibrutinib in vitro	
The protein binding of [^{14}C]-remibrutinib in human plasma at concentrations of 5, 50, 500, and 5000 ng/mL were 98, 95.3, 95.1, and 95.2%, respectively.

DMPK R1400850:
Evaluating the in vitro plasma protein binding of [^3H]-remibrutinib and [^3H]-LTG751 for human.	
Remibrutinib contains a reactive acrylamide moiety by design. LTG751 is an analog of remibrutinib where the reactive acrylamide has been inactivated (double bond saturated).
The plasma protein binding of [^{14}C]-remibrutinib at concentrations of 10, 100, 1000 and 10000 ng/mL was 94.4, 95.2, 95.5, and 96%, respectively.
L916: Report/Objectives	Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
The mean in vitro human blood/plasma distribution of [^{3}\text{H}]-remibrutinib and [^{3}\text{H}]-LTG751 for human were 0.813 (over a concentration range of 100 to 10,000 ng/mL) and 0.858 (over a concentration range of 10 to 1000 ng/mL), respectively. At a concentration of 10 ng/mL, the blood/plasma distribution for remibrutinib was 0.955.

Metabolism
DMPK R1500124:
Evaluation of [^{3}\text{H}]-remibrutinib oxidative metabolite profile in human liver microsomes (HLM), human hepatocytes, liver cytosol, and S9 fractions and using recombinant human enzymes	
CYP3A4 inhibitors ketoconazole and azamulin inhibited the metabolism of remibrutinib up to 96% and 100% in HLM with an \text{IC}_{50} value of 0.04\mu\text{mol/L} and 0.005\mu\text{mol/L}, respectively. The fraction metabolized (f_m) by CYP3A4 was estimated to be 0.95.
CYP3A4 mainly formed metabolites M24 (dihydrodiol), M22 (O-dealkylation; LTP763), and M28 (demethylation). Metabolite M28 was also formed by CYP2C8 (6.3%), CYP2B6 (2.9%), CYP2C9 (1.4%) and CYP3A5 (1.8%). CYP1A1 and CYP2C19 formed a single metabolite (M15; C-hydroxylation) with an abundance >30%.
[^{3}\text{H}]-remibrutinib at 1\mu\text{mol/L} incubated with HLM at 1 mg/mL was extensively metabolized (83%), where M24 (19%), M28 (14%), LTP763 (8%), M23 (7%), M29 (7%) and M30 (6%) were the most abundant metabolites. These metabolites were the predominant oxidative metabolites formed by liver S9 fraction as well. Liver cytosolic enzymes were not found to have a major impact on the metabolic clearance of remibrutinib.
[^{3}\text{H}]-remibrutinib at 10\mu\text{mol/L} incubated in human cryopreserved hepatocytes was metabolized (50%) to M31 (11%), M24 (8.8%), M15 (6.1%), M29 (4%) and LTP763 (3%). The M28 metabolite (the major metabolite in HLM) was no longer detectable in hepatocytes after 3 hours.
CYP3A4 is the predominant enzyme in the hepatic oxidative metabolism of remibrutinib with minor contribution from CYP1A1 and CYP2C19.
