L924: DMPK R1600559: Identifying the human enzymes responsible for the oxidative metabolism of remibrutinib in human liver microsomes and by recombinant enzymes.

Recombinant microsomes expressing CYP3A4 or CYP1A1 showed the highest metabolic activities of [^{3}H]-remibrutinib at a concentration of 3\muM. Metabolic activities were 29.43% (12.24% M28 metabolite) with CYP3A4, and 23.08% (21.92% M15 metabolite) with CYP1A1. The metabolic activity of CYP2C19 was 2.19%. Low metabolic activity was observed with other recombinant microsomes expressing other CYP and flavin-containing monooxygenase (FMO) enzymes. The metabolic activity of HLM with [^{3}H]-remibrutinib 3\muM was 11.06%.
L925: After accounting for the relative abundance in human liver microsomes, CYP3A4/5 is the major enzyme involved in the hepatic oxidative metabolism of [^{3}H]-remibrutinib.
L926: Report/Objectives	Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
In Vitro Drug-Drug Interaction Studies
DMPK R1500083:
Inhibitory effects of remibrutinib on CYP activities in human liver microsomes and measurement of microsomal protein binding (fumic)	
In pooled human liver microsomes, the IC_{50,u} (corrected for microsomal protein binding) for the reversible inhibition of CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, CYP2E1, and CYP3A4/5 was 70.6, >88.3, 35.5, 9.9, 10.9, 14, 68.5, >78.9, and 27.5/26.1 (midazolam/testosterone) \muM, respectively.
The K_{i,u} (corrected for microsomal protein binding) for inhibition of CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A4/5 was 23.7, 5, 7.7, 7.7, and 14.1\muM, respectively.
The fraction unbound to microsomal proteins after incubation with remibrutinib (1 and 20\muM) range between 1.004 to 0.743 with protein concentrations increasing from 0.0039 mg/mL to 1 mg/mL.
Remibrutinib was an inhibitor of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4/5.

DMPK R1400510;
DMPK R1900718:
Examination of time-dependent inhibition of CYP2C9, CYP1A2, CYP2D6, CYP3A4/5, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, and CYP2C19 in HLM	
In human liver microsomes, time-dependent inhibition of CYP3A4/5 by remibrutinib was observed in the presence of NADPH (K_i=8.8\muM, K_{i,u}=6.9\muM, k_{inact}=0.0102 min^{-1}).
No time-dependent inhibition of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19 and CYP2D6 by remibrutinib was observed at concentrations up to 100\muM.
Remibrutinib is a time-dependent inhibitor of CYP3A4/5.

DMPK R1300944:
Evaluating the induction of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C9 and CYP3A4 (activities and mRNA) by remibrutinib	
In primary human hepatocytes, remibrutinib at 10, 20, 30 and 50\muM increased CYP2B6 mRNA expression by 2.2-, 3.2-, 4-, and 6.9-fold, respectively, and enzyme activity by 2.8-, 3.78-, 3.61-, and 5.53-fold, respectively. The EC_{50} value for half-maximal induction was 25\muM for CYP2B6.
Remibrutinib at 1, 5, 10, 20, 30 and 50\muM did not increase CYP2C9 mRNA expression by >2-fold, but increased the enzyme activity by 2.4-, 2.87-, 2.2-, 2.65-, 5.25-, and 3.43-fold, respectively.
Remibrutinib at 5, 10, 20, 30 and 50\muM increased CYP3A4 mRNA expression by 3.4-, 9.7-, 27.7-, 45.6-, 104.6-fold, respectively, and enzyme activity by 1.06-, 1.42-, 1.78-, 2.14-, 2.05-fold, respectively. The EC_{50} was 33\muM for CYP3A4.
The mRNA expression and activity of CYP1A2 were not >2-fold across the studied remibrutinib concentration range (up to 50\muM).
Based on the in vitro data using cryopreserved human hepatocytes from a single donor, remibrutinib may induce CYP2B6, CYP2C9 and CYP3A. remibrutinib is not expected to induce CYP1A2.

DMPK R1600703:
Evaluating the induction of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C9 and CYP3A4 (activities and mRNA) by remibrutinib	
The potential for remibrutinib at concentrations up to 10\muM to induce CYP enzymes was determined in primary human hepatocytes derived from three individual donors. Note that incubation of remibrutinib with hepatocytes for 24 hours showed a time-dependent decrease of remibrutinib concentrations, likely due to metabolism. Thus, induction effects may be underestimated.
L927: Report/Objectives	Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
CYP1A2:
Remibrutinib (\ge 2.5\mu\text{M}) showed dose-dependent induction (>2-fold) of CYP1A2 gene expression in all three donors. The average \text{EC}_{50} and \text{E}_{\text{max}} for fold-induction were 4.55\mu\text{M} and 5.47, respectively.
More than 2-fold increase in enzyme activity in Donor 1 was observed at remibrutinib 10\mu\text{M}. Donor 2 showed no increase in enzyme activity. Dose-dependent induction was observed in Donor 3, with >2-fold induction observed at concentrations \ge 0.63\mu\text{M} with maximum induction of 8.33-fold observed at remibrutinib 5\mu\text{M}.
CYP2B6:
Remibrutinib (\ge 5\mu\text{M}) showed dose-dependent induction (>2-fold) of CYP2B6 gene expression in all three donors. The average \text{EC}_{50} and \text{E}_{\text{max}} for fold-induction were 5.47\mu\text{M} and 5.45, respectively.
Donor 3 showed 2.17-fold (15.9% of the maximal response in the positive control) increase in enzyme activity at remibrutinib 10\mu\text{M}. No induction of enzyme activity was observed for Donors 1 and 2.
CYP2C9:
Remibrutinib (>10\mu\text{M}) showed dose-dependent induction (>2-fold) of CYP2C9 gene expression in all three donors. The average \text{EC}_{50} and \text{E}_{\text{max}} for fold-induction were 5.1\mu\text{M} and 3.02, respectively.
Donor 3 showed 3.08-fold (84% of the maximal response of positive control), 2.12-fold (45.2% of the maximal response of positive control), and 3.13-fold (86% of the maximal response of positive control) increase in enzyme activity at remibrutinib 2.5, 5 and 10\mu\text{M}, respectively. No induction of activity was observed for Donors 1 and 2.
CYP3A4:
Remibrutinib (>0.31\mu\text{M}) showed dose-dependent induction (>2-fold) of CYP3A4 gene expression in all three donors. The average \text{EC}_{50} and \text{E}_{\text{max}} for fold-induction were 5.08\mu\text{M} and 28.08, respectively.
Donor 3 showed 1.98-fold (21.9% of the maximal response in the positive control) increase in enzyme activity at remibrutinib 10\mu\text{M}. No induction of enzyme activity was observed for Donors 1 and 2.
Remibrutinib at concentrations up to 10\mu\text{M} induced CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, and CYP3A4 gene expression. Induction of catalytic activity for CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C9 was only observed in one donor.

DMPK R2301196:
Evaluation of the inhibitory potential of remibrutinib on the human BCRP and MDR1	
The inhibition potential of remibrutinib on BCRP and MDR1 (P-gp) was determined using vesicular transport assays with inside-out membrane vesicles prepared from HEK293 cells overexpressing BCRP or MDR1. Remibrutinib showed significant loss due to nonspecific binding with an average of 31.1% in transport buffer. Nominal concentrations were therefore corrected by a factor of 0.689 at all concentrations.
Remibrutinib inhibited BCRP-mediated (E3S) accumulation by 100% at concentrations \ge 17.2\mu\text{M}. The calculated \text{IC}_{50} was 0.533\mu\text{M}, the calculated \text{K}_i value was 0.467\mu\text{M}.
L928: Report/Objectives	Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
Remibrutinib inhibited MDR1-mediated (N-methyl-quinidine) accumulation by about 91% at the highest tested concentration of 34.5µM. The calculated IC50 was 0.513µM, the calculated Ki value was 0.382µM.
Remibrutinib has the potential to inhibit BCRP and MDR1 efflux transporters

DMPK R1500199: Evaluation of the inhibitory potential of remibrutinib on the human BCRP and P-gp and the inhibition of BCRP by LTG751	
The potential for remibrutinib to inhibit BCRP and P-gp, and potential for LTG751 to inhibit BCRP were evaluated in recombinant MDCKII and LLC-PK1 cell overexpressing BCRP and P-gp, respectively. Note that nominal concentrations were corrected for nonspecific binding to glass and plastic (up to 37.4%).
Remibrutinib inhibited P-gp with IC50=29.4µM, Ki =29.4µM, and a maximal inhibition of 101.7% (relative to positive control) and inhibited BCRP with an IC50=4.3µM and a maximal inhibition of 118.3% (relative to positive control).
LTG751, an analog of remibrutinib in which the reactive acrylamide moiety has been inactivated via saturation of the double bond, inhibited BCRP with an IC50=3µM and a maximal inhibition of 106.6%.
Remibrutinib has the potential to inhibit BCRP and P-gp efflux transporters. Based on inhibition of BCRP by LTG751, BCRP inhibition by remibrutinib is not likely related to the irreversible-binding potential of remibrutinib

DMPK R1500200: Inhibitory effects of remibrutinib and LTG751 on OATP1B1/1B3 transporters	
The potential for remibrutinib and LTG751 to inhibit OATP1B1/3 was determined in HEK293 cells overexpressing OATP1B1/3. The maximum inhibition of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 relative to positive control at the highest remibrutinib concentration (66.6µM, measured) was 99.9% and 100.7%, respectively.
The maximum inhibition relative to positive control of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 at the highest LTG751 concentration (100µM, nominal) was 99.4% and 115.4%, respectively.
The IC50 values of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 inhibition were 2.9µM and 9µM (for remibrutinib) and 5.2 and 8.4µM (for LTG751), respectively.
The Ki values of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 inhibition were 2.8µM and 8.8µM (for remibrutinib) and 5.1 and 8.3µM (for LTG751), respectively.
Remibrutinib and LTG751 have the potential to inhibit hepatic OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 uptake transporters. Based on LTG751 inhibition of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3, inhibition by remibrutinib is not likely related to the irreversible-binding potential of remibrutinib.

DMPK R1500824: Evaluating the inhibitory effects of remibrutinib on OAT1 and OAT3 transporters	
The potential for remibrutinib to inhibit OAT1 and OAT3 was determined in HEK293 cells overexpressing OAT1 and OAT3. The IC50 and Ki values of OAT1 inhibition were 39.4µM and 38.6µM, respectively. The IC50 and Ki values of OAT3 inhibition were 14.1µM and 10.6µM, respectively.
The maximum inhibition of OAT1 and OAT3 relative to positive control at the highest remibrutinib concentration (66.6µM, measured) was 71.2% and 85.3%, respectively.
Remibrutinib has the potential to inhibit renal OAT1 and OAT3 uptake transporters.
L929: Report/Objectives	Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
DMPK R1700782:
Evaluating the inhibitory potential of remibrutinib on BSEP, MRP2, MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT1 and OCT2	
The potential for remibrutinib to inhibit BSEP and MRP2 were determined in vesicular transport inhibition assays with inside-out membrane vesicles prepared from HEK293 cells overexpressing BSEP or MRP2. The potential for remibrutinib to inhibit MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT1, and OCT2 were determined in uptake transporter inhibition assays using HEK293 cells stably expressing the respective uptake transporter.
Relative to control, the maximum inhibition at the highest remibrutinib concentrations was 97%, 29%, 88%, 56%, 97%, and 51% for BSEP, MRP2, MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT1, and OCT2, respectively. The highest nominal remibrutinib concentrations tested in inhibition assays for BSEP, MRP2, MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT1, and OCT2 were 50, 100, 75, 75, 75, and 75\muM, respectively.
The IC50 for transport inhibition was 8.69, 14.4, 68.8, 7.21, and 68.92\muM for BSEP, MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT1, and OCT2, respectively.
The Ki for transport inhibition was 7.8, 13.6, 68.3, 7.19, and 68.7\muM for BSEP, MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT1, and OCT2, respectively.
The IC50 and Ki for MRP2 inhibition were not determined.

DMPK R2000485:
Evaluating the inhibitory potential of remibrutinib on MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 transporters	
Remibrutinib is an inhibitor of BSEP, OCT1, OCT2, MATE1 and MATE2-K
The potential for remibrutinib to inhibit MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1, and OCT2 were determined in uptake transporter inhibition assays using HEK293 (OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1, OCT2) or MDCKII (MATE1, MATE2-K) cells stably expressing the respective uptake transporter.
Nonspecific binding of remibrutinib was 67.4% in HBSS pH7.4 media (correction factor 0.326 was used for nominal concentrations used for OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 assays) and 70% in KH pH 8 media (correction factor of 0.296 was used for nominal concentrations used for MATE1 and MATE2-K assays).
Relative to control, the inhibition at the highest remibrutinib measured concentrations (7.4\muM for MATE1 and MATE2-K and 8.15\muM for the other tested transporters) was 70%, 32%, 41%, 90%, 83%, 44%, 88%, and 44% for MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2, respectively.
The IC50 values for transporter inhibition were 1.71, 1.12, 2.62, and 1.1\muM, and Ki was 1.7, 1.09, 2.36, 1.09\muM for MATE1, OAT3, OATP1B1 and OCT1, respectively.
The IC50 for OAT1, OATP1B3 and OCT2 was estimated to be 9.97, 7.81, and 9.41\muM and Ki was estimated to be 9.87, 7.55, and 9.41\muM. Values were estimated because the maximum inhibition at the highest remibrutinib tested concentration was <50%. The IC50 and Ki for MATE2-K inhibition were not determined.
Remibrutinib has the potential to inhibit MATE1, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1, and OCT2.
L930: Report/Objectives	Summary Findings and Key Conclusions
DMPK R2200876: Investigating the inhibition potential of remibrutinib, on NTCP transporter.	The potential for remibrutinib to inhibit NTCP was determined in uptake inhibition assays using HEK293 cells expressing NTCP. The remibrutinib nominal concentrations were corrected by specific factors based on nonspecific binding assays.
Remibrutinib at concentrations up to 58.7µM inhibited NTCP by 33%. The IC50 and Ki are >58.7µM.
Remibrutinib inhibited NTCP by less than 50% at the highest test concentrations.

L943: ## 14.2.1. Single-Dose, Multiple-Dose, and Food-Effect Studies in Healthy Subjects
L944: ## 14.2.1.1. Study X2101
L945: Study X2101 was a six-part, first-in-human study of remibrutinib using the hard gelatin capsule (HGC) drug product. The overall design of this study is illustrated in Figure 16.
L946: - Part 1 was randomized (6:2, remibrutinib:placebo), double-blind, placebo-controlled to evaluate the pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of single doses of remibrutinib in healthy subjects. Doses of 0.5, 1.5, 5, 15, 30, 60, 100, 200, 400, and 600 mg were evaluated. Blood samples for PK assessment were collected at predose and up to 168 hours postdose.
- Parts 2 and 4 were randomized (6:2, remibrutinib:placebo), double-blind, placebo-controlled to evaluate the pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of multiple doses of remibrutinib in healthy subjects with asymptomatic atopic diathesis. Part 2 was multiple-ascending dose (MAD) given as 13 doses over 12 days and Part 4 was MAD given as 25 doses over 12 days. Dosages in Part 2 were 10, 25, 50, 100, 400, and 600 mg QD. Dosages in Part 4 were 100 and 200 mg BID. Blood PK sampling timepoints for Parts 2 and 4 were at predose (Day 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, and 12) and up to 24 hours after first dose and up to 168 hours after the last dose on Day 12.
L947: - Part 3 was a single-dose, open-label, crossover, food-effect study with 18 days washout period between two single 60-mg doses of remibrutinib in healthy subjects. On Day 1, following an overnight fast of at least 10 hours, subjects were given a high-calorie, high-fat meal (i.e., breakfast consisting of two eggs fried in butter, two strips of bacon, two slices of toast with butter, 4 oz [100 g] of hash brown potatoes, 8 fl. oz [about 240 mL] of whole milk). Subjects were instructed to consume the breakfast within 30 minutes and to administer remibrutinib within 5 minutes after completion of the meal. Blood samples for PK assessment were collected at predose and up to 96 hours postdose.
- Part 5 was a double-blind, single-dose, two-sequence, relative bioavailability study to compare the pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of remibrutinib after administration of a single oral dose of 50 mg of the capsule drug product containing either (b) (4) or (b) (4) drug substance in healthy subjects. Blood samples for PK assessment were collected at predose and up to 48 hours postdose. The relative bioavailability of the (b) (4) drug substance compared to the (b) (4) drug substance is not relevant for the current review. Therefore, Part 5 will not be discussed in this review.
- Part 6 was a double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, 4-week multiple-dose study of remibrutinib 100 mg BID in subjects with atopic dermatitis. As atopic dermatitis is not a proposed indication for remibrutinib, Part 6 will not be discussed in this review.
L948: Figure 16. Study Design, Part 1 to 6, Study X2101
L949: Source: Interim and Full Clinical Study X2101 Reports, Figure 9-1
L950: The Test drug products used in Study X2101 include the following batches: remibrutinib 50 mg (Batch # 1010008635 and 1010015369 for Part 1 to 5; Batch # 2033160 for Part 6), remibrutinib 5 mg (Batch # 1010008926) and remibrutinib 0.5 mg (Batch # 1010008923).
L951: Abbreviations: BID, twice daily; MAD, multiple-ascending dose; QD, once daily; SAD, single-ascending dose
L952: ## Pharmacokinetic Results
L953: ## Part 1: Single-Ascending Dose
L954: Following single-dose administration, remibrutinib exhibited rapid absorption with median T_{max} values ranging from 0.5 to 1.25 hours across all dose levels. At lower doses (&lt;100 mg), the blood concentrations declined below the lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) of 1 ng/mL before entering the terminal phase (Figure 17). At the lowest doses of 0.5, 1.5, and 5 mg, t_{1/2} could not be estimated. The mean t_{1/2} ranged from 0.5 to 3.2 hours across the dose levels of 15 to 200 mg. At higher doses, a longer half-life was observed. Specifically, at 600 mg of remibrutinib, the terminal t_{1/2} extended to 14.1 hours (Table 40). Both the C_{max} and the AUC increased nonproportionally with escalating doses throughout the range from 0.5 mg to 600 mg. For instance, a 20-fold increase in dose from 30 mg to 600 mg resulted in only a 7.6-fold increase in C_{max} and a 15-fold increase in AUC_{last}. Full dose-linearity is not anticipated due to time-dependent clearance, attributed to formation of a covalent bond between remibrutinib and BTK. Based on a linear regression analysis of log-transformed parameters, over the dose range of 30 to 200-mg single dose, the increase in C_{max} was less than dose-proportional (slope 90% CI: 0.68 [0.52, 0.85]), the increase in AUC_{last} and AUC_{0-inf} was approximately dose-proportional and less than dose-proportional, respectively (slope 90% CI: 0.94 [0.78, 1.11] for AUC_{last} and 0.90: [0.75, 1.05] for AUC_{0-inf}; data derived from Table 11-2, Interim clinical study report for Study X2101). The apparent volume of distribution increases with higher doses of remibrutinib, which may be due to saturable covalent binding of remibrutinib to BTK.

L965: ## Part 2: MAD
L966: The summary statistics of the remibrutinib PK parameter values for Part 2 are presented in Table 41. Analogous to Part 1, the terminal elimination phase following the final dose was discernible only at higher doses (i.e., exceeding 100 mg) (Figure 18). Accumulation was observed with an increase observed in both C_{max} and AUC from Day 1 to Day 12. This may be attributed to the covalent binding of remibrutinib to BTK. However, the contribution of target-mediated clearance to the overall clearance of remibrutinib is more pronounced at lower doses. At doses of 400 mg and 600 mg, accumulation becomes negligible. For instance, the exposure difference, as measured by AUC_{0-24h}, was 4.86-fold between Day 1 (AUC_{0-24h}=4.94 ng*h/mL) and Day 12 (AUC_{0-24h}=24 ng*h/mL) at a dosage of 10 mg QD. The accumulation ratio diminishes to 1.14-fold at 600 mg (Day 1 AUC_{0-24h} of 1080 ng*h/mL and Day 12 AUC_{0-24h} of 1230 ng*h/mL), likely due to saturation of target binding. Consequently, remibrutinib exhibits both time and dose-dependent clearance.
L967: Similar to Part 1, C_{max} and AUC increased in a nonproportional manner with escalating doses across the entire range from 10 mg to 600 mg. Based on linear regression analysis with log-transformed parameters, over the dose range of 10 to 100 mg, the increase in C_{max}, AUC_{last}, and AUC_{0-24h} was less than dose-proportional with slope (90% CI) values of 0.64 (0.55, 0.74), 0.81 (0.69, 0.93), and 0.80 (0.68, 0.91), respectively.

L978: ## Part 4
L979: The summary statistics of the remibrutinib pharmacokinetic parameter values for Part 4, encompassing doses of 100 mg and 200 mg administered BID, are presented in **Table 42**. A less-than-dose-proportional increase in both Cmax and AUC was observed when comparing the 100-mg BID and 200-mg BID doses. From Day 1 to Day 12, the accumulation ratios for Cmax were 1.7 and 1.5 at the 100-mg and 200-mg BID dosages, respectively. The accumulation ratios for AUC0-12h were 1.5 and 1.9 for 100 mg and 200 mg, respectively. Notably, at a dose of 100 mg, a similar accumulation ratio of 1.5 was observed for AUCtau (0 to 24 for QD, 0 to 12 for BID) irrespective of whether remibrutinib was administered QD or BID. The data support that
L980: ## NDA 218436
## RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
L981: accumulation of remibrutinib exposure is due to target binding and the contribution of target-mediated clearance to overall clearance.
L982: The renal clearance of remibrutinib constitutes approximately &lt;1% of the total blood clearance, with specific values of 0.22% and 0.18% (calculated as the ratio of renal clearance to steady-state apparent clearance) noted at the 100-mg BID and 200-mg BID dosages on Day 12, respectively.

L1000: ## Part 3: Food Effect
L1001: The blood concentration-time profiles for the treatment arms involving remibrutinib at a dose of 60 mg, under both fasted and fed conditions, are illustrated in Figure 19. The administration of a high-fat breakfast resulted in a 2-hour delay in the absorption of remibrutinib, with the T_{max} increasing from 0.767 hours under fasted conditions to 2.78 hours under fed conditions. Additionally, the AUC_{last} was augmented by 30%, while C_{max} was reduced by 20%. The statistical analysis of the food effect on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib is detailed in Table 43.

L1011: ## Pharmacodynamic Results
L1012: ## **BTK Occupancy in Peripheral Blood**
L1013: ## Part 1: Single-Ascending Dose Regimen
L1014: BTK occupancy exceeding 95% was observed at a dose level of 30 mg and above following a single-dose administration of remibrutinib. This level of occupancy was sustained before beginning to decline 48 hours postadministration (Figure 20).
L1015: ## Part 2: Multiple-Dose QD Regimen
L1016: In the multiple-dose QD regimens, the BTK occupancy on Day 1 was comparable to that observed in the single-ascending dose (SAD) regimen. By Day 12, BTK occupancy exceeded 95% across all tested dose levels, ranging from 10 mg to 400 mg. This occupancy began to decline 48 hours after the final dose on Day 12 (Figure 20).
L1017: ## Part 4: Multiple-Dose BID Regimen
L1018: In Part 4 of the study, near-complete BTK occupancy was achieved at both the 100-mg and 200-mg doses in the multiple-dose BID regimens. This occupancy persisted until 48 hours after the last dose on Day 12 (Figure 20).
L1019: Figure 20. Arithmetic Mean (SD) Percent BTK Occupancy by Remibrutinib in Peripheral Blood in Parts 1, 2, and 4, Study X2101
L1020: Source: Interim Clinical Study X2101 Reports, Figure 11-7, 11-8, 11-9, and 11-10
Blood sampling timepoints: Part 1: at predose and up to 504 hours postdose; Part 2 and 4: at predose (Day 1, 7, and 12) and up to 24 hours after first dose and up to 504 hours after the last dose on Day 12.
Free BTK levels (not bound to remibrutinib) were measured in whole blood by ELISA. For Free BTK, the anticipated LLOQ was 1.45 ng/mL. Concentrations for free BTK below the LLOQ was reported in listings as '&lt;1.45 ng/mL' and imputed in summary statistics as 0.725 ng/mL (i.e., imputed as LLOQ/2).
Abbreviations: BTK, Bruton's tyrosine kinase; ELISA, enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay; hrs, hours; LLOQ, lower limit of quantification; MAD, multiple-ascending dose; QD, once daily; SAD, single-ascending dose; SD, standard deviation
L1021: ## Ex Vivo Activation of CD203c and CD63
L1022: Ex vivo inhibition of basophil activation was assessed by measuring the surface expression of cell-surface markers CD203c and CD63.
L1023: ## Part 1
L1024: The inhibition of CD203c activation exceeded 50% at a dose of 60 mg and above. The peak inhibition of stimulated basophils (79.3% inhibition) was observed 8 hours following administration of 200 mg on Day 1 (Figure 21). A more pronounced and sustained inhibition of CD63 activation was evident at doses \ge 60 mg. At doses of 100 mg and higher, the inhibition of CD63 activation was maintained at or above 88% for up to 48 hours post-treatment (Figure 21).
L1025: ## Parts 2 and 4
L1026: On Day 1, the highest percentage inhibition of CD203c activation was observed 8 hours postdose in the cohorts receiving 400 mg QD and 200 mg BID, with inhibition rates of 84% and 81.76%, respectively. The maximum inhibition of CD203c activation at trough was 63% for the 100-mg BID cohort on Day 12 and 67% for the 200-mg BID cohort on Day 12 (Figure 21). A more pronounced inhibition of CD63 activation was evident at doses \ge50 mg QD, achieving at least 95% inhibition 24 hours after the initiation of treatment (Figure 21). The lowest trough inhibition of CD63 activation was 80% following treatment with 10 mg QD on Day 12.

L1033: ## 6\beta OH-Cortisol/Cortisol Ratio
L1034: The concentration ratios of 6\beta OH-cortisol to cortisol were measured in urine to probe for modulation of intrinsic CYP3A4 activity. There was no dose-dependent or time-dependent increase in the ratio, suggesting that remibrutinib may not modulate CYP3A4 activity (Figure 22).

L1038: ## 14.2.1.2. Study X1101
L1039: Study X1101 was a randomized, placebo-controlled study designed to assess the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of remibrutinib using SAD and MAD regimens in healthy Japanese male subjects. The study was divided into Part A and Part B. Part A comprised five cohorts, each receiving a single dose of remibrutinib at 5 mg, 30 mg, 100 mg, 200 mg, or 400 mg. Part B consisted of a single cohort that was administered remibrutinib at 100 mg BID for 5 days. Remibrutinib was administered as 5-mg and 50-mg HGCs.
L1040: ## **Pharmacokinetic Results**
L1041: ## **Part A: SAD**
L1042: Remibrutinib exhibited rapid absorption, with T_{max} occurring between 0.5 and 1.5 hours across all doses (Figure 23 and Table 44). Based on a linear regression analysis of log-transformed parameters, across the dose range of 30 to 400 mg, C_{max} increased in an approximately dose-proportional manner (slope 90% CI: 0.99 [0.80, 1.18]) and AUC_{last} increased in a greater than dose-proportional manner (slope 90% CI: 1.16 [1.00, 1.32]). The terminal t_{1/2} ranged from 0.841 to 24.3 hours, with longer t_{1/2} observed at higher doses. The apparent terminal elimination phase was not fully established until 12 hours postdose and was only measurable at doses exceeding 100 mg. Urine samples indicated that the amount of remibrutinib excreted in urine over 72 hours
L1043: following a single 100-mg dose was 0.345 mg, with a renal clearance (CLr) of 1.12 L/h (0.28% of total apparent clearance).

L1056: ## Part B: MAD
L1057: Following administration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID for 5 days, accumulation in Cmax and AUC0-12h was observed with accumulation ratios of 1.25 and 1.24, respectively (Table 45 and Figure 24). Steady state was achieved by Day 2. Urine samples indicated that the amount excreted in urine over 12 hours (Ae0-12h) and CLr were 0.356 mg and 0.989 L/hr on Day 1, and 0.512 mg and 1.12 L/hr on Day 5, respectively.

L1071: ## Pharmacodynamic Results
L1072: ## BTK Occupancy in Peripheral Blood
L1073: BTK occupancy exceeding 90% was observed at a dose level of 30 mg and above following a single dose, and this occupancy was maintained for up to 48 hours postdose (Figure 25). Due to technical difficulties, total BTK levels were not analyzed for some subjects in the 200-mg group and for all subjects in the 400-mg group.
L1074: In Part B of the study, free BTK levels were utilized as a surrogate measure for BTK occupancy (Figure 25). Following administration of 100 mg BID, free BTK levels were ablated for up to 48 hours after administration of the last dose.
L1075: **Figure 25. Arithmetic Mean (SD) Percent of BTK Occupancy by LOU064 and Free BTK in Peripheral Blood, Study X1101**
L1076: Source: Study X1101 Clinical Report, Figure 11-5 and 11-6
Abbreviations: BID, twice daily; BTK, Bruton's tyrosine kinase; hr(s), hour(s); LOU064, remibrutinib; SD, standard deviation
L1077: ## Inhibition of B-Cell Activation
L1078: The inhibitory effect of remibrutinib on activation of CD69, a marker found on the surface of B-cells was assessed. In Part A, remibrutinib demonstrated an inhibitory effect of \ge 56\% at a dose of 30 mg, with the maximum inhibition reaching about 66% at a dose of 200 mg at 24 hours postdose. In Part B, the peak inhibitory effect of remibrutinib (i.e., 65%) was observed on Day 1 and was sustained for 24 hours following the final dose administered on Day 5 (Figure 26).
L1079: Figure 26. Arithmetic Mean (SD) Percent Inhibition of CD69 Activation, Study X1101
L1080: Source: Study X1101 Clinical Report, Figure 11-7 and 11-8
Abbreviation: BID, twice daily; LOU064, remibrutinib; SD, standard deviation
L1081: ## 14.2.2. [^{14}\text{C}]-Labelled Absorption, Metabolism, and Excretion Study, Study X2104
L1082: Study X2104 was a single-center, open-label study conducted in seven healthy subjects to evaluate the ADME of remibrutinib under fasted conditions. The study comprised three distinct arms: an oral arm and two IV arms (A and B). As recovery of total radioactivity from the first four subjects in the oral arm was deemed incomplete (~60%), an assessment of exhaled air was introduced for the fifth subject in the oral arm. As recovery of radioactivity did not improve after accounting for exhaled air, an additional cohort of two subjects was enrolled in which radiolabeled remibrutinib was administered IV to ensure receipt of the complete dose.
L1083: - **Oral arm (N=5):** On Day 1, subjects received 100 mg of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib BID as the HGC drug product. On the morning of Day 2, subjects were administered a single oral dose of an oral solution containing 100 mg of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib and 0.074 MBq (2 \mu\text{Ci}) of [^{14}\text{C}]-remibrutinib. To maintain steady-state conditions, the administration of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib 100-mg BID capsules was resumed in the evening of Day 2 and continued until the evening of Day 5.
- **IV Arm A (N=1):** On Day 1, the subject received nonradiolabeled 100 mg of remibrutinib HGC BID. On the morning of Day 2, 1 hour after the oral morning dose of 100 mg of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib, the subject received a single IV microdose of 100 \mu\text{g} and 0.074 MBq (2 \mu\text{Ci}) of [^{14}\text{C}]-remibrutinib. To maintain steady-state conditions, the administration of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib 100-mg BID capsules was resumed in the evening of Day 2 and continued until the evening of Day 5.
- **IV Arm B (N=1):** One subject received nonradiolabeled remibrutinib HGC at a dose of 100 mg BID for 5 consecutive days. On the morning of Day 6, 1 hour after the oral morning dose of 100 mg of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib, the subject received a single IV microdose of 100 \mu\text{g} and 0.074 MBq (2 \mu\text{Ci}) of [^{14}\text{C}]-remibrutinib. To maintain steady-state conditions, the administration of nonradiolabeled remibrutinib 100-mg BID capsules was resumed in the evening of Day 6 and continued until the evening of Day 9.
L1084: ## Schedule of PK Sampling Timepoints
L1085: ## Oral Arm
L1086: - Blood PK samples: Collected at predose and at 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, 36, and 48 hours postradiolabeled dose.
- Urine collection: Conducted at predose and on Days 1, 2, 3 to 5, 6 to 11, 15 to 16, 19 to 20, 23 to 24, and 27 to 28.
- Feces collection: Conducted at predose and on Days 2, 3 to 5, 6 to 11, 15 to 16, 19 to 20, 23 to 24, and 27 to 28.
- Exhaled air collection: Conducted on Days 2 and 3 to 5.
L1087: ## IV Arm A
L1088: - Blood PK samples: Collected at predose and at 5 min, 10 min, 15 min, and 20 min, and 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, and 48 hours post-IV dose.
- Urine and fecal collection: Conducted at predose and on Days 2, 3 to 5, 6 to 19, 23 to 24, 27 to 28, 31 to 32, and 35 to 36. Notably, collections were not performed on Days 12, 14, 16, and 18.
- Exhaled air collection: Conducted on Days 2 and 3 to 5.
L1089: ## IV Arm B
L1090: - Blood PK samples: Collected at predose and at 5 min, 10 min, 15 min, and 20 min, and 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, and 48 hours post-IV dose.
- Urine collection: Conducted at predose and on Days 6, 7 to 9, 10 to 23, 27 to 28, 31 to 32, 35 to 36, and 39 to 40. Notably, collections were not performed on Days 16, 18, 20, and 22.
- Fecal collection: Conducted at predose and on Days 5, 6, 7 to 9, 10 to 23, 27 to 28, 31 to 32, 35 to 36, and 39 to 40.
- Exhaled air collection: Conducted on Days 6 and 7.
L1091: Pharmacokinetic parameters for total radioactivity in plasma and whole blood were derived from the concentration-time data for total radioactivity. Urinary, fecal, and exhaled air recovery, as well as total recovery, were calculated. Concentrations below the LLOQ were reported as “zero,” and missing data points were explicitly labeled as such.
L1092: ## PK Mass Balance Results
L1093: ## Oral Arm
L1094: The cumulative excretions of total radioactivity in urine, feces, and total recovery are presented in Figure 27 and Table 46. The recovery of total radioactivity was incomplete, amounting to only 56.2% of the administered dose. The reasons for incomplete recovery of radioactivity are unclear. Of the excreted radioactivity, 31.8% was recovered in feces, with 24.9% recovered in the feces within the first 72 hours. In urine, 24.4% of the total radioactivity was recovered, with 20.7% recovered in urine within the first 24 hours. The percentage of unchanged remibrutinib
L1095: recovered in urine was 4.1% of the dose, suggesting that a significant portion of the total radioactivity recovered in urine consists of metabolites. Exhaled air was evaluated in one subject. No radioactivity was detected in exhaled air.
L1096: The Applicant attributed the incomplete recovery of radioactivity to technical issues, supported by the following observations:
L1097: - The metabolic profiles in blood, urine, and feces obtained after oral and intravenous administration were comparable.
- The relative contributions of clearance pathways were also comparable between oral and IV administration.
- No long-lasting radioactivity was detected in blood or excreta.
- No volatile radioactivity was detected in exhaled air after oral administration, which would have suggested the formation of volatile metabolites or CO2.
L1098: ## IV Arm
L1099: The recovery of the administered dose of [14C]-remibrutinib was 96.8% in IV Arm A and 97.2% in IV Arm B. Fecal excretion accounted for 67.1% of the dose in IV Arm A, with 57.4% recovered within the first 72 hours. In IV Arm B, fecal excretion was 71.7%, with 65.7% of this amount recovered within the first 48 hours (Figure 27 and Table 46). Urinary excretion constituted 29.7% of the dose in IV Arm A and 25.5% in IV Arm B, the majority of which was recovered within the first 12 to 24 hours. The percentage of unchanged remibrutinib recovered in urine was 2.2% for IV Arm A and 5% for IV Arm B.
L1100: Note that as near-complete recovery of radioactivity was obtained after IV dosing, it is not likely that the missing radioactivity after oral dosing was retained within the body. The data further support that technical issues contributed to incomplete recovery of radioactivity after oral dosing.

The excretion of total radioactivity in urine and feces as presented in the bioanalytical reports, were calculated using the amount of radioactivity measured in Bq divided by the (actual) radioactivity dose in Bq. For the mass balance results reported in the CSR, the percent excreted was derived from the concentrations in ng-eq/mL or ng-eq/g multiplied by the volume (or weight) of the sample, divided by the (actual) administered dose of [^{14}\text{C}]-remibrutinib. Differences in rounding at several steps during these calculations resulted in small differences between the (mean) cumulative excretion results reported in the bioanalytical reports and the CSR.
CV% = coefficient of variation (%) = SD/mean*100

L1107: ## Pharmacokinetics of Total Radioactivity in Plasma and Whole Blood
L1108: ## Oral Arm
L1109: Following administration of [^{14}\text{C}]-remibrutinib, total radioactivity concentrations were quantifiable in plasma for up to 312 to 600 hours and in whole blood for up to 216 to 408 hours postdose. The \text{AUC}_{0-\text{inf}} of total radioactivity in plasma was marginally higher than that in whole blood (14900 \text{h}\cdot\text{ng-eq}/\text{mL} versus 12700 \text{h}\cdot\text{ng-eq}/\text{mL}) (Table 47 and Table 48, as well as Figure 28). The median elimination t_{1/2} of [^{14}\text{C}]-remibrutinib was 250 hours in whole blood and 288 hours in plasma.
L1110: ## IV Arm
L1111: Total radioactivity concentrations were quantifiable in both plasma and whole blood up to 408 hours postdose. In IV Arm A and IV Arm B, the total radioactivity, as measured by \text{AUC}_{0-\text{inf}}, in whole blood and plasma were comparable (\text{AUC}_{0-\text{inf}} 16.9 versus 18.5 \text{h}\cdot\text{ng-eq}/\text{mL}, respectively, in IV Arm A and 13.2 versus 12.8 \text{h}\cdot\text{ng-eq}/\text{mL}, respectively, in IV Arm B), suggesting an equal distribution of total radioactivity between plasma and red blood cells (Table 47, Table 48, and Figure 28). The elimination t_{1/2} of total radioactivity in whole blood was 374 hours in IV Arm A and 435 hours in IV Arm B. The elimination t_{1/2} of total radioactivity in plasma was 291 hours in IV Arm A and 363 hours in IV Arm B.

L1123: ## Pharmacokinetics of Remibrutinib in Blood
L1124: After the administration of remibrutinib at a dose of 100 mg BID, steady-state conditions were achieved by Day 4 with an elimination t1/2 of 3.29 hours in the oral arm, 1.85 hours in IV Arm A and 2.06 hours in IV Arm B. A summary of the pharmacokinetic parameters of remibrutinib at steady state in blood is presented in Table 49.

L1130: ## **Metabolic Profile of Remibrutinib in Human Subjects**
L1131: Metabolite patterns in blood, urine and feces were evaluated as AUC0-12h (IV) or AUC0-24h (oral) in blood, for up to 48 hours in urine, and for up to 96 (IV) or 144 hours (oral) in feces. The pool covering the period from 0 to 48 hours postdose in urine represented 95% of the mean total radioactivity excreted in urine in the oral arm (~24% of the dose) and 98% of that in the IV arms (~28% of the dose). The pool covering the period from 0 to 144 hours postdose in feces in the oral arm represented 94% of the mean total radioactivity excreted in feces (~32% of the dose). The pool covering the period from 24 to 96 hours postdose in feces in the IV arms represented 99% of the mean total radioactivity excreted in feces (~69% of the dose).
L1132: ## **Oral Arm**
L1133: Upon oral administration of 100-mg [14C]-remibrutinib, the parent remibrutinib was the predominant species in blood, constituting 16.7% of the blood 14C-AUC0-24h (Table 50). Metabolic profiling in healthy subjects revealed that remibrutinib is metabolized into 18 distinct metabolites in blood, with none of these metabolites accounting for ≥10% of the total. The most abundant metabolites, accounting for a total of 11.2% of blood AUC0-24h, were a mixture of two diastereomers, M68a and M68b. As the diastereomers are present in a 3:1 ratio, neither metabolite is considered major (i.e., accounts for &gt;10% of total exposure). Remaining metabolites accounted for ≤6.3% of blood AUC0-24h. The unchanged remibrutinib in excreta represented 1.86% of the administered dose.
L1134: ## NDA 218436
## RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
L1135: In urine, a total of 31 metabolites were identified, with metabolite M24 being the most abundant, comprising 3.49% of the dose (refer to Table 6-7, Study X2104 clinical study report Amendment 3). The amount of unchanged remibrutinib excreted in urine was 0.65% of the dose, suggesting negligible renal excretion of remibrutinib.
L1136: In feces, a total of 21 metabolites were detected, with M24 being the most prevalent, representing 7.22% of the dose (refer to Table 6-7, Study X2104 clinical study report Amendment 3). The amount of unchanged remibrutinib in feces was 1.21% of the dose, suggesting near complete absorption of remibrutinib (F_a &gt; 98\%, assuming the compound is stable in the intestine) and extensive metabolism.

L1147: ## IV Arm
L1148: Following intravenous administration of 100-\mug [^{14}C]-remibrutinib, the parent compound remibrutinib was the most abundant component in blood, constituting 21.7% of the blood ^{14}C-AUC0-12h. Thirteen metabolites were identified in the blood, with none representing \ge10% of the total radioactivity (Table 51). Notably, metabolites M68a and M68b were detected and accounted for 6.8% of total radioactive components.
L1149: A total of 27 metabolites were detected in urine, with none exceeding 10% of the total radioactivity (refer to Table 6-8, Study X2104 clinical study report Amendment 3). The amount of unchanged remibrutinib excreted in urine was 2.93% of the administered dose.
L1150: In feces, a total of 21 metabolites were characterized, with M24 being the most prevalent, accounting for 16.9% of the dose. The remaining metabolites in feces each represented less than 10% of the dose (refer to Table 6-8, Study X2104 clinical study report Amendment 3). Unchanged remibrutinib was not detected in feces, indicating that remibrutinib is primarily eliminated through metabolic pathways.

L1166: ## 14.2.3. Relative Bioavailability Study Comparing HGC and FCT Drug Products, Study X2105
L1167: Study X2105 was a randomized, open-label, three-period study designed to evaluate the relative bioavailability of two formulations of remibrutinib: a FCT and a HGC (Figure 29). The HGC formulation was utilized in phase 1 and phase 2 clinical studies of remibrutinib. The selected FCT variant (variant selected [VS]) was employed in phase 3 trials and was compared against the HGC formulation. The FCT (VS) contains remibrutinib that was [REDACTED] (b) (4) range as that in the HGC. Additionally, this study assessed the bioavailability of FCT (VS), with a particle size of approximately (b) (4)nM, in comparison to an alternative FCT variant (variant alternative [VA]), with a particle size of approximately (b) (4)nM. This comparison aimed to determine whether minor differences in the manufacturing process, specifically [REDACTED] (b) (4), could influence the absorption rate and extent of remibrutinib.
L1168: All three remibrutinib formulations were administered to healthy adult subjects (aged 18 to 55 years) in the fasted state as a single oral dose of 100 mg (2x50 mg). Subjects were randomized 1:1 to one of two treatment sequences that varied the order of administering remibrutinib HGC and FCT (VS). Pharmacokinetic samples were collected in each period at predose and at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 8, 12, 24, 36, and 48 hours postdose. Bioequivalence between FCT (VS) and HGC remibrutinib 100 mg was established if the least-squares geometric mean ratio and its 90% CI fell within the predefined bioequivalence range of 80% to 125%. A washout period of 7 to 14 days separated each treatment period.

L1170: Source: Study X2105 Clinical Report, Figure 9-1

L1174: B: single morning oral dose of 100-mg remibrutinib HGC
L1175: C: single morning oral dose of 100-mg remibrutinib FCT (VA)
L1176: Abbreviations: EOS, end of study; FCT, film-coated tablet; HGC, hard gelatin capsule; N, number of subjects; VA, variant alternative; VS, variant selected

L1193: Statistical analysis of the pharmacokinetic parameters of remibrutinib revealed that the Cmax, AUClast, and AUC0-inf of the remibrutinib FCT (VS) formulation were 28%, 16%, and 20% lower, respectively, compared to those of the remibrutinib HGC formulation (Table 54 and Table 55). Additionally, the AUClast and AUC0-inf of the remibrutinib FCT (VS) formulation were 26.8% and 7.2% higher, respectively, while the Cmax was 4.6% lower compared to the remibrutinib FCT (VA) formulation (Table 54 and Table 55). Notably, the elimination phase was determined for only six subjects who received the FCT (VA) formulation. It was also noted that for nine subjects who received the FCT (VA) formulation, the 48-hour postdose PK sample was missing as blood collection tubes were not available. As a result, the AUClast determined for the FCT (VA) formulation may have been underestimated, especially as blood concentrations at 48 hours postdose were quantifiable in the seven subjects from whom samples were collected. No change in median Tmax was observed between the three formulations (about 1 hour for all formulations).

L1207: Overall, the systemic exposure to remibrutinib following oral administration of 100 mg of the FCT (VS) formulation was lower than that following administration of the HGC formulation. However, high variability in the PK parameters was observed. The AUC of the FCT (VS) formulation was greater than that observed with the FCT (VA) formulation. Results should be interpreted with caution due to missing samples among subjects who received the FCT (VA) formulation and inability to characterize the terminal elimination phase in most subjects. Although the magnitude of effect is unclear, differences in particle size due to changes in the manufacturing process do not appear to yield substantial differences in the systemic exposure.
L1208: ## 14.2.4. Food-Effect Study Following Multiple Doses of Remibrutinib, Study A02104
L1209: Study A02104 was a randomized, open-label, two-cohort, two-period, two-sequence, crossover study designed to evaluate the effect of food on the steady-state pharmacokinetics of multiple
L1210: ## NDA 218436
## RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
L1211: doses of remibrutinib FCT 25 mg and 100 mg in healthy Chinese adult subjects. Although the exclusion criteria did not restrict the gender of enrolled subjects, only male subjects were enrolled. The study design is illustrated in Figure 31. Subjects were randomized to one of two cohorts divided by dose (i.e., 25 mg or 100 mg). Within each cohort, subjects were randomized to one of two treatment sequences that varied the timing of prandial states. Remibrutinib was administered BID from Days 1 to 5. On Day 6, only the morning dose was administered. The test drug was provided as remibrutinib FCT 25 mg and 100 mg.
L1212: In the fed state, subjects received a high-fat, high-calorie breakfast (800 to 1000 calories with 150, 250, and 500 to 600 calories from protein, carbohydrate, and fat, respectively). Remibrutinib was administered within 5 minutes after completion of the meal. For drug administration on Day 4 under fed conditions, only the morning dose of remibrutinib FCT 25 mg and 100 mg was taken after a standard high-fat breakfast. The evening dose was taken following a fast of at least 2 hours. For fasted and fed administration, subjects fasted for at least 10 hours before the morning dose (or the high-fat breakfast), and for at least 2 hours before the evening dose.
L1213: PK samples were collected after the Day 4 morning dose and the Day 6 dose, with PK sampling conducted up to 24 hours postdose.
L1214: **Figure 31. Study Design, Study A02104**
L1215: Source: Study A02104 Clinical Report, Figure 9-1
L1216: Per Study A02104 protocol, the breakfast was high-fat (approximately 50% of total caloric content of the meal) and high-calorie (approximately 800 to 1000 calories), derive approximately 150, 250, and 500 to 600 calories from protein, carbohydrate, and fat, respectively.
L1217: Abbreviations: b.i.d., twice daily; D, day; EOS, end of study; N, number of subjects; PK, pharmacokinetic; q.d., once daily

L1235: ## 14.2.5. Hepatic Impairment Study A2101
L1236: Study A2101 was an open-label study designed to evaluate the pharmacokinetics, safety, and tolerability of remibrutinib administered at a dose of 25 mg twice daily in adult subjects with mild (Child-Pugh Class A, score 5 to 6), moderate (Child-Pugh Class A, score 7 to 9), and severe (Child-Pugh Class A, score 10 to 15) HI compared to matched healthy adult subjects with normal hepatic function. The study also aimed to assess the plasma protein binding (unbound fraction [fu]) of remibrutinib in subjects with mild, moderate, or severe HI and in matched healthy control subjects to investigate the potential impact of reduced plasma protein binding on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib.
L1237: An exploratory objective of this study was to examine the relationship between remibrutinib PK parameters and baseline measures of impaired hepatic function (e.g., prothrombin time, INR, bilirubin, albumin, AST, and Child-Pugh score, as appropriate) using a linear regression model.
L1238: The study was conducted in two parts:
L1239: - Part 1: Enrolled subjects with mild (Group 1) and moderate (Group 2) HI and matched healthy control subjects with normal hepatic function (Group 4)
- Part 2: Enrolled subjects with severe HI (Group 3) and matched healthy control subjects with normal hepatic function (Group 4)
L1240: Each subject in the healthy control group was matched with respect to gender, age (\pm 10 years), and body weight (\pm 20\%) to at least one and up to three evaluable subjects in the HI groups. Each control subject in Group 4 could be matched to any HI group (Groups 1, 2, and 3) but could not be matched to more than one subject from the same HI group.
L1241: Key inclusion criteria included males and females between 18 and 70 years of age (inclusive). Subjects with an estimated glomerular filtration rate of &lt;45 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2 were excluded from Groups 1, 2, and 3 (i.e., HI groups).
L1242: All subjects received remibrutinib 25 mg (1x25 mg) BID orally under fasting conditions on Day 1 and Day 2, and a morning oral dose of remibrutinib 25 mg on Day 3 to achieve steady state. The PK samples were collected on Day 3 at predose, and 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, 48, and 72 hours postdose. Blood collection for the determination of unbound drug was performed at 1 hour postdose on Day 3.
L1243: The PK parameters C_{\text{max,ss,u}}, AUC_{\text{tau,u}}, and AUC_{\text{last,ss,u}} were calculated using unbound plasma remibrutinib concentrations. The total plasma concentration of remibrutinib was calculated based on the measured blood concentrations of remibrutinib and the blood-to-plasma partitioning ratio of 0.955 determined at clinically relevant concentrations (refer to Section 14.1). The unbound plasma concentrations were calculated from the total plasma concentration multiplied by the fraction unbound determined from samples collected 1 hour postdose on Day 3.
L1244: ## Results
L1245: The study enrolled eight subjects with mild HI, eight subjects with moderate HI, seven subjects with severe HI, and 15 matched controls with normal hepatic function. All subjects completed the study. Among subjects with HI, the median (min, max) Child-Pugh scores were as follows:
L1246: - Mild HI: 5 (5, 6)
- Moderate HI: 7 (7, 8)
- Severe HI: 10 (10, 11)
L1247: ## Whole Blood Remibrutinib Pharmacokinetics
L1248: The blood concentration-time profiles of remibrutinib following the administration of remibrutinib 25 mg BID in subjects with normal hepatic function, and subjects with mild, moderate, or severe HI are illustrated in Figure 33. The blood PK parameters of remibrutinib on Day 3 for all study groups is presented in Table 58. The C_{\text{max,ss}} values were elevated in all HI groups (65, 57, and 60.8 ng/mL for mild, moderate, and severe HI subjects, respectively) compared to the normal hepatic function group (30.1 ng/mL). Notably, C_{\text{max,ss}} values were comparable among the three HI groups. Similarly, the geometric means AUC_{\text{tau}} and AUC_{\text{last}} were higher in all HI groups compared to the normal hepatic function group. Specifically, AUC_{\text{tau}} values were 60.5, 151, 147, and 204 ng\cdoth/mL for subjects with normal hepatic function, and subjects with mild, moderate, and severe HI, respectively (Table 58). The T_{\text{max}}, ranging from 0.42 to 2 hours, was comparable across all hepatic function groups. It is noteworthy that the arithmetic mean predose blood concentrations of remibrutinib on Day 3 were 0.539 ng/mL in subjects with normal hepatic function and 2.41 ng/mL, 2.88 ng/mL, and 3.93 ng/mL in subjects with mild, moderate, and severe HI, respectively. Furthermore, the terminal t_{1/2} appeared to be prolonged, and the apparent oral clearance at steady state (CL_{\text{ss}}/F) and apparent volume of distribution at steady state (V_{\text{ss}}/F) were elevated in the HI groups compared to the normal hepatic function group.

L1272: ## Unbound Remibrutinib Plasma Pharmacokinetics
L1273: The plasma protein binding of remibrutinib was determined by ultrafiltration. Radiolabeled [^{14}C]-remibrutinib was added ex vivo to clinical samples and the concentrations were determined by liquid scintillation counting. The mean f_u of remibrutinib, measured at the 1-hour postdose time point on Day 3, was found to be comparable across subjects with normal hepatic function (f_u=0.0472) and subjects with mild (f_u=0.0465), moderate (f_u=0.0474), and severe (f_u=0.0438) HI. Similar to what was observed based on total blood pharmacokinetics, the PK parameters of unbound remibrutinib in plasma, including C_{max,ss,u}, AUC_{tau,u}, and AUC_{last,ss,u}, were elevated in all HI groups compared to subjects with normal hepatic function (Table 60). Based on unbound plasma remibrutinib concentrations, the geometric mean ratios for mild, moderate, and severe HI compared to healthy controls were as follows: 1.9, 1.63, and 1.76 for C_{max,ss,u}; 2.14, 2.0, and 2.78 for AUC_{tau,u}; and 2.29, 2.2, and 3.09 for AUC_{last,ss,u}, respectively (Table 61).

L1289: ## Relationship Between Remibrutinib Blood PK Parameters and Hepatic Function Parameters
L1290: The associations between blood remibrutinib exposure and hepatic function parameters were explored using a linear regression model to predict the natural logarithm-transformed AUC_{last,ss} based on logarithm-transformed baseline hepatic function measures. The hepatic function measures included baseline prothrombin time, albumin, bilirubin, AST, and INR. The exploratory analysis showed an apparent relationship between remibrutinib AUC_{last,ss} and different hepatic function measures (Figure 34 and Figure 35).

L1297: ## Impact of Concomitant Treatment
L1298: The Applicant restricted the enrollment of subjects on Legalon, a phytopharmaceutical preparation derived from *Silybum marianum* (L.) (an extract of milk thistle). Per the European Medicines Agency Committee on Herbal Medicinal Products, milk thistle fruit can be used to support liver function. Notably, Study A2101 enrolled subjects from a single site in Hungary. Thus, use of milk thistle products among subjects with HI was possible. The restriction on Legalon usage in Study A2101 was implemented following an observation of elevated remibrutinib exposure in subjects who coadministered remibrutinib with Legalon. Specifically, one subject (b) (6) with mild HI, two subjects (b) (6) with moderate HI, and two subjects (b) (6) with severe HI coadministered Legalon. Notably, subject (b) (6) with mild HI showed significantly higher Cmax and AUC of remibrutinib compared to other subjects within the same group. One of the two subjects with moderate HI on Legalon exhibited higher remibrutinib exposure compared to other subjects within the same HI group (Figure 36). It is important to note that the exposure to remibrutinib in the two subjects with severe HI and one subject with moderate HI fell within the range observed in other subjects within their respective HI groups (Figure 36). The Applicant hypothesized that the increased exposure to remibrutinib when coadministered with Legalon could be attributed to the inhibition of CYP3A4 as remibrutinib was identified as a substrate of CYP3A4. Based on literature reports, in vitro studies have indicated that milk thistle may decrease CYP3A4 activity by 50% (Venkataramanan et al. 2000). However, clinical studies reported in the literature indicated that silymarin products do not alter midazolam exposure (Gurley et al. 2006; Kawaguchi-Suzuki et al. 2014).
L1299: **Figure 36. Scattered Plot of Blood AUC_{last,ss}, AUC_{tau}, and C_{max,ss} of Remibrutinib in Healthy Control Subjects and Subjects With Hepatic Impairment, Study A2101**
L1300: Source: Reviewer Analysis using ADPC.xpt data
L1301: Black dots in red squares represent subjects on Legalon.
L1302: Abbreviations: AUC_{last}, area under the concentration-time curve from time zero to the last measurable concentration sampling time; AUC_{last,ss}, area under the concentration-time curve from time zero to the last measurable concentration sampling time at steady state; AUC_{tau}, area under the concentration-time curve over the dosing interval; C_{max,ss}, maximum plasma concentration at steady state; HI, hepatic impairment
L1303: Given the potential for milk thistle products to inhibit CYP3A4 activity, it is possible that the true effect of HI on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib may have been overestimated. Elevated remibrutinib exposure relative to remaining subjects in the respective HI group was only observed in one subject with mild HI and one subject with moderate HI, both of whom were concomitantly taking Legalon. Thus, conclusions for subjects with mild HI and subjects with moderate HI may have been impacted. The magnitude of increase in C_{max}, AUC_{last}, and AUC_{tau} was higher in mild HI versus moderate HI when compared to subjects with normal hepatic function. This observation may be attributed to the higher exposure observed in Subject (b) (6) in the mild HI group. For the remaining subjects on Legalon, although remibrutinib exposure was comparable to that in other subjects with a similar HI severity, it is still possible that PK parameters were elevated. However, given that there is conflicting evidence regarding the impact of milk thistle products on CYP3A4 activity, the impact of HI on remibrutinib exposure will consider data from all subjects irrespective of use of milk thistle products.
L1304: ## 14.2.6. Drug-Drug Interaction
L1305: ## 14.2.6.1. CYP3A4 Inhibitors (Ritonavir and Grapefruit Juice), Study X2103
L1306: In vitro investigations demonstrated that the primary clearance pathway of remibrutinib is oxidative metabolism mediated by CYP3A4. Study X2103 was designed as an open-label, single-sequence, crossover study to assess the impact of ritonavir (RTV), a strong inhibitor of both intestinal and hepatic CYP3A4/P-gp, and grapefruit juice (GFJ), a moderate to strong inhibitor of intestinal CYP3A4, on the exposure to oral remibrutinib in healthy adult subjects under fasted conditions. Secondary objectives of this study included the determination of the fraction of oral remibrutinib that escapes gut metabolism (F_{\text{gut}}).
L1307: A detailed description of the study design is provided in Figure 37.
L1308: ## Period 1 (Reference Period)
L1309: ## Day 1 and 2 (Lead-In)
L1310: Single oral dose of remibrutinib 50 mg was administered on the morning of Day 1 and Day 2, after an overnight fast of at least 10 hours. The remibrutinib doses on Day 1 and Day 2 were given 24 hours apart.
L1311: ## Day 3
L1312: Subjects were given approximately 240 mL of 10% glucose (to mimic GFJ caloric content) after an overnight fast of at least 9 hours. One hour later, a single oral dose of remibrutinib 50 mg was administered, followed by IV bolus administration of 100 µg stable-isotope-labeled (SIL)-remibrutinib 2 hours after the oral dose. Blood samples were collected for up to 24 hours for remibrutinib and SIL-remibrutinib measurements.
L1313: ## Period 2 (GFJ Period)
L1314: ## Day 4 (Baseline and Lead-In)
L1315: Following baseline assessment, a single 50-mg oral remibrutinib dose was administered in the morning of Day 4 after an overnight fast of at least 9 hours. GFJ was given orally to the subjects 15 hours postremibrutinib intake, on the evening of Day 4.
L1316: ## Day 5
L1317: A total of 240-mL GFJ was given in the morning after an overnight fast of at least 9 hours. A single 20-mg oral remibrutinib dose was administered 1 hour later. Blood samples were collected for up to 24 hours after the oral remibrutinib dosing, for remibrutinib measurements.
L1318: ## **Period 3 (RTV Period)**
L1319: ## **Day 6 (Baseline and Lead-In)**
L1320: Following baseline assessment, a single 50-mg oral remibrutinib dose was administered in the morning of Day 6 after an overnight fast of at least 9 hours. This was followed by a single 100-mg oral dose of RTV 15 hours after the oral remibrutinib dose.
L1321: ## **Day 7**
L1322: RTV 100 mg was administered with approximately 240 mL 10% w/v glucose in water on the morning of Day 7 after an overnight fast of at least 9 hours. One hour later, a single 20-mg oral remibrutinib dose was administered. At 2 hours after the oral dose, subjects received IV bolus administration of 100-\mug SIL-remibrutinib. On the evening of Day 7, about 12 hours post the morning RTV dose, another oral RTV 100-mg dose was given. Blood samples were collected up to 48 hours for remibrutinib and SIL-remibrutinib measurements.
L1323: ## **Day 8**
L1324: An oral dose of RTV 100 mg was administered in the morning after an overnight fast of at least 9 hours followed by a second dose of RTV 100 mg 12 hours postmorning RTV dose.
L1325: The remibrutinib dose of 50 mg once daily was given orally in Period 1 and Period 2 to achieve near-complete BTK receptor occupancy and steady-state conditions, thereby minimizing the influence of target-mediated drug disposition on remibrutinib PK measurements. In the first-in-human Study X2101, once-daily dosing of remibrutinib at 25 mg and above resulted in almost complete BTK occupancy beginning on Day 1. This level of occupancy persisted with administration of subsequent daily doses until about 48 hours after the last dose on Day 12 (refer to Section 14.2.1.1). PK results from Study X2101 indicated that remibrutinib accumulates following administration of multiple doses despite a short apparent half-life at doses \le100 mg. The reason for this accumulation is likely due to the influence of target binding on remibrutinib disposition rather than incomplete clearance of remibrutinib prior to administration of the next dose. Given the short apparent half-life, it is not expected that administration of higher doses (i.e., 50 mg) will influence the pharmacokinetics following administration of the 20-mg dose used to evaluate the impact of CYP3A4 inhibition. Therefore, it is reasonable for the Applicant to administer lead-in doses of 50-mg remibrutinib prior to probing the impact of CYP3A4 inhibition on remibrutinib exposure.
L1326: RTV and GFJ are mechanism-based inhibitors of CYP3A4, exhibiting time- and concentration-dependent enzyme inactivation that is irreversible (Bailey et al. 2013; Loos et al. 2022). Consequently, a complete washout (minimum of 2 to 3 weeks) of RTV or GFJ between treatment periods is not feasible, as it would require the full de novo synthesis of the CYP3A enzyme pool. A washout of 24 hours between all periods was selected to separate remibrutinib dosing. The washout period is acceptable for clearance of remibrutinib prior to administration of the next dose. As GFJ is expected to impact only intestinal CYP3A4, while RTV will impact both intestinal and hepatic CYP3A4, the sequence of administration is reasonable. As an additive effect between GFJ and RTV is not expected, full washout to accommodate resynthesis of the CYP3A enzyme pool between the GFJ and RTV treatment periods is not required.
L1327: ## NDA 218436
## RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
L1328: The dose of ritonavir to be used in the study is 100 mg administered twice daily. This dosage is the same as that approved for use in combination with lopinavir for the treatment of HIV-1 infection (Kaletra) and the same as that approved for use in combination with nirmatrelvir for the treatment of mild-to-moderate COVID-19 (Paxlovid). Notably, at this dosage, ritonavir is used to inhibit CYP3A-mediated metabolism of the concomitant treatment.
L1329: Administration of SIL-remibrutinib as an IV bolus dose was intended to support determination of the fractional contribution of CYP3A to overall clearance. The dose of SIL-remibrutinib of 100 µg is 0.5% of the lowest oral remibrutinib dose of 20 mg.
L1330: Remibrutinib for oral administration was supplied as HGCs in 20-mg and 50-mg strengths. Remibrutinib for intravenous administration was supplied in vials containing SIL-remibrutinib (a stable labeled isotope of remibrutinib) 100-µg powder for solution for injection.
L1331: Figure 37. Study Design, Study X2103
L1332: Source: Study X2103 Clinical Report, Figure 9-1
L1333: On the morning of Day 3, subjects were administered 240 mL of 10% w/v glucose in water after at least 9 hours of fasting and oral remibrutinib was administered thereafter 1 hour later.
L1334: Subjects were administered RTV with approximately 240 mL 10%/w/v glucose in water on the morning of Day 7 after approximately 9 hours of overnight fasting.

L1336: ## Results
L1337: ## DDI With Ritonavir and Grapefruit Juice
L1338: The blood concentration-time profiles and pharmacokinetic parameters for remibrutinib following the administration of 20-mg and 50-mg doses with and without 100-mg RTV or 240-mL GFJ, are illustrated in Figure 38 and summarized in Table 62. Dose-normalized PK parameters were used to assess the impact of CYP3A4 inhibitors on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib as 50-mg remibrutinib was administered in Treatment Period 1, while 20-mg remibrutinib was administered in Treatment Periods 2 and 3. Based on Study X2101, following administration of multiple once-daily doses of remibrutinib, exposure increased in a less than dose-proportional manner between doses of 10 mg and 600 mg (refer to Section 14.2.1.1).
L1339: Therefore, it is unclear whether comparing dose-normalized PK parameters will be appropriate. Based on PK data from Study X2101, between dosages of 10 mg QD and 50 mg QD at steady state, median C_{\text{max}} appeared to increase in an approximately dose-proportional manner (17.2 ng/mL and 100 ng/mL, respectively), while median \text{AUC}_{\text{last}} increased in a greater than dose-proportional manner (22.4 ng.h/mL and 179 ng.h/mL, respectively) (refer to Table 41). However, between dosages of 25 mg QD and 50 mg QD, C_{\text{max}} increased in a less than dose-proportional manner, while \text{AUC}_{\text{last}} increased in an approximately dose-proportional manner. On the other hand, remibrutinib C_{\text{max,ss}} and \text{AUC}_{\text{tau}} increase in a dose-proportional manner between dosages of 10 mg BID, 25 mg BID, and 100 mg BID in Study A2201 (see Section 14.2.7). Specifically, the geometric mean C_{\text{max,ss}} values following the administration of 10 mg BID, 25 mg BID, and 100 mg BID were 26.8, 56.6, and 188 ng/mL, respectively while geometric mean \text{AUC}_{\text{tau}} values were 65.1, 162, and 677 ng.h/mL, respectively. Therefore, the use of dose-normalized parameters is acceptable.
L1340: Median (min-max) T_{\text{max}} values of remibrutinib when administered alone was 1 hour (0.52 hour to 2.5 hours), 0.73 hours (0.5 hours to 2 hours) when remibrutinib was coadministered with GFJ, and 0.715 hours (0.5 hours to 1.5 hours) when remibrutinib was coadministered with RTV. The mean elimination t_{1/2} was comparable for remibrutinib alone (6.58 hours) and when coadministered with GFJ (7.13 hours) but was increased when remibrutinib was coadministered with RTV (12.1 hours).
L1341: GFJ elevated the dose-normalized C_{\text{max}}, \text{AUC}_{\text{last}}, and \text{AUC}_{0-\text{inf}} of orally administered remibrutinib by 1.24-, 1.33- and 1.28-fold, respectively. RTV has a greater impact on remibrutinib pharmacokinetics. Dose-normalized C_{\text{max}}, \text{AUC}_{\text{last}}, and \text{AUC}_{0-\text{inf}} of orally administered remibrutinib increased by 3.32-, 4.5-, and 4.31-fold, respectively (Table 63).

L1360: ## Pharmacokinetics of Intravenous SIL-Remibrutinib and Bioavailability of Remibrutinib
L1361: The blood concentration-time profiles and PK parameters of SIL-remibrutinib, following the administration of a 100-µg IV dose with and without ritonavir, are presented in Figure 39 and Table 64. The mean CL, t1/2, and Vc for intravenous remibrutinib were determined to be 70.1 L/h, 0.725 hours, and 72.4 L, respectively.
L1362: The mean dose-normalized pharmacokinetic parameters for SIL-remibrutinib, when administered alone, were as follows: Cmax, 0.0389 ng/mL/mg; AUC0-24h, 14.5 h*ng/mL/mg; AUClast, 14.3 h*ng/mL/mg; and AUC0-inf, 14.7 h*ng/mL/mg. When administered with ritonavir on Day 7, the corresponding values were: Cmax, 0.0365 ng/mL/mg; AUC0-24h, 24.8 h*ng/mL/mg; AUClast, 24.4 h*ng/mL/mg; and AUC0-inf, 25.3 h*ng/mL/mg.
L1363: Based on the dose-normalized AUC0-24h values for SIL-remibrutinib when administered alone and in the presence of ritonavir, the estimated fraction of SIL-remibrutinib metabolized by CYP3A4 (fm) was determined to be 0.42 (i.e., 1 – [14.5/24.8]).

L1373: ## Fraction of Remibrutinib Absorbed From the Gut
L1374: The Fgut was determined using dose-normalized AUC0-24h values. These values were 4.89 h*ng/mL/mg for remibrutinib administered alone and 6.28 h*ng/mL/mg when coadministered with GFJ. The estimated Fgut value of 0.78 indicates a relatively lower contribution of gut CYP3A4 to the metabolism of remibrutinib compared to hepatic CYP3A4.
L1375: ## 14.2.6.2. CYP3A4 Inducers (Carbamazepine), P-gp Substrates (Digoxin), OATP1B1 Substrates (Coproporphyrin), and BCRP/OATP1B1 Substrate (Rosuvastatin), Study A02103
L1376: Study A02103 was an open-label, two-part study to investigate the impact of carbamazepine (a strong CYP3A4 inducer) 300 mg BID on the exposure to remibrutinib 100 mg BID, and the impact of remibrutinib 100 mg BID on the exposure to digoxin (P-gp substrate) 0.25-mg single-dose tablet and rosuvastatin (BCRP and OATP1B substrate) 10-mg single-dose tablet in healthy adult subjects.
L1377: ## Part 1
L1378: Part 1 was a two-period, single-sequence study in which remibrutinib 100 mg (2x50 mg) BID was administered alone (Period 1, Days 1 to 3), followed by coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg (2x50 mg) BID (Period 2, Days 23 to 25) and the CYP3A4 inducer, carbamazepine (Period 2, Days 4 to 25). Remibrutinib was administered BID on Days 1, 2, 23, and 24, and in
L1379: ## NDA 218436
## RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
L1380: the morning only on Days 3 and 25. Remibrutinib was administered in Period 1 and 2 following an overnight fast of at least 10 hours. On Days 1, 2, 23, and 24, subjects remained fasted 1 hour postmorning dose and kept fasted for at least 1 hour prior to evening dose. On Days 3 and 25, subjects remained fasted until 4 hours postmorning dose.
L1381: During the carbamazepine up-titration, subjects received oral doses of carbamazepine 100 mg BID from Day 4 to Day 6, followed by carbamazepine 200 mg BID from Day 7 to Day 9, and carbamazepine target maintenance dose of 300 mg BID from Day 10 to Day 25 (on Day 25, morning dose only).
L1382: On Days 3 and 25, PK samples were collected up to 12 hours either alone (Day 3) or under CYP3A4 induction by carbamazepine (Day 25). Remibrutinib PK blood concentration was also measured on Day 24 at 1 hour postdose.
L1383: A comprehensive description of the study design and pharmacokinetic sampling times for Part 1 is illustrated in Figure 40.
L1384: **Figure 40. Study Design of Part 1, Study A02103**
L1385: Source: Study A02103 Clinical Report, Figure 9-1
L1386: Remibrutinib was provided in 50 mg strength hard gelatin capsule (Batch # 2044228).
L1387: Day 3: remibrutinib PK samples were obtained at predose, 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, and 12 hours postdose. Day 24: remibrutinib PK sample was obtained 1 hour postdose. Day 25: remibrutinib PK samples were obtained at predose and 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12 hours postdose.
L1388: Abbreviations: bid, twice daily; D, day; EOS, end of study; EOT, end of treatment; h, hour; PK, pharmacokinetic
L1389: Remibrutinib was administered as 50-mg HGCs. Carbamazepine was administered as 100, 200, or 300 mg extended-release capsules. The maintenance dose of carbamazepine of 300 mg BID is within the range of approved doses per the approved labeling for carbamazepine (Carbatrol). Administration of carbamazepine for 14 days prior to coadministration with remibrutinib is acceptable to ensure sufficient time for CYP3A4 induction.
L1390: ## Part 2
L1391: Part 2 was a two-period, single-sequence study in which a drug cocktail containing digoxin and rosuvastatin was administered alone (Period 1, Day 1), followed by coadministration of the same cocktail (Period 2, Day 14) with remibrutinib (Period 2, Days 11 to 24). On Day 1, subjects
L1392: received a single dose of digoxin (0.25-mg tablet) and rosuvastatin (10-mg tablet) after an overnight fast of at least 10 hours and remained fasted until 4 hours postdose. From Day 11 to Day 24, subjects were administered oral doses of remibrutinib 100 mg BID (only the morning dose was administered on Day 24). On Day 14, subjects received a single dose of digoxin (0.25 mg) and rosuvastatin (10 mg) after an overnight fast of 10 hours and remained fasted until 4 hours postdose. A comprehensive description of the study design and PK sampling times for Part 2 is illustrated in Figure 41.
L1393: To differentiate between BCRP and OATP1B1 inhibition, the endogenous OATP1B1 substrate coproporphyrin I was measured. Additionally, the renal clearance of rosuvastatin, a process mediated by OAT3 at the renal tubule level, was monitored to assess the inhibitory potential of remibrutinib on OAT3 transport.

L1412: ## Part 1: Effect of Carbamazepine (Strong CYP3A4 Inducer) on Remibrutinib
L1413: The coadministration of remibrutinib at a dose of 100 mg BID with carbamazepine resulted in significant reductions in the PK parameters of remibrutinib. Specifically, C_{max,ss}, AUC_{\tau}, and AUC_{last,ss} were reduced by 74%, 77%, and 78%, respectively. Additionally, carbamazepine decreased the CL_{ss}/F and V_{ss}/F of remibrutinib from 1540 L/h to 362 L/h and from 2720 L to 1100 L, respectively (Table 66 and Figure 42). Notably, there was no significant impact on the T_{max} of remibrutinib.

L1447: A summary of the PK data for rosuvastatin in urine, both in the presence and absence of remibrutinib, is presented in Table 69. L1453: ## Part 2: Effect of Remibrutinib on Plasma Coproporphyrin I (OATP1B1 Substrate)
L1454: Rosuvastatin was employed as a substrate for BCRP and OATP1B to investigate potential drug interactions. To distinguish between the inhibition of OATP1B and BCRP, changes in coproporphyrin I, a potential endogenous biomarker of OATP1B1 activity, were measured in the absence and presence of remibrutinib. The plasma concentration-time profiles and PK parameters of coproporphyrin I following the administration of digoxin (0.25 mg) and rosuvastatin (10 mg) with remibrutinib (100 mg BID) were compared to those following the administration of digoxin (0.25 mg) and rosuvastatin (10 mg) alone. These data are presented in Figure 45 and Table 70. Overall, Cmax of uncorrected coproporphyrin I increased by 2%, while AUClast decreased by 2% in the presence of remibrutinib (Table 65). These findings suggest that remibrutinib may not have the potential to inhibit the hepatic uptake transporter OATP1B1.

L1465: ## 14.2.6.3. CYP3A4 Substrates (Oral Contraceptives and Midazolam), CYP1A2 Substrates (Caffeine) and CYP2C9 Substrates (Tolbutamide), Study X2102
L1466: In vitro studies have demonstrated that remibrutinib possesses the potential to both inhibit and induce CYP3A4, CYP1A2, and CYP2C9. Study X2102 was designed as an open-label, three-period, single-sequence, crossover study in healthy female subjects of nonchild-bearing potential to investigate the impact of remibrutinib 100 mg (administered as 1x100-mg HGC) BID for 18 days on the exposure to a single dose of an OC containing EE and LVG, which are substrates of CYP3A and CYP2C. Additionally, the study examined the impact of remibrutinib on single oral doses of midazolam (CYP3A4 substrate), caffeine (CYP1A2 substrate), and tolbutamide (CYP2C9 substrate).
L1467: The study design is outlined as follows (Figure 46).
L1468: ## Period 1 (Day 1)
L1469: Subjects were administered a single dose of a probe cocktail containing 2 mg of midazolam, 200 mg of caffeine, and 500 mg of tolbutamide. This was followed by a washout period of at least 72 hours and not exceeding 7 days.
L1470: ## **Period 2 (Day 9)**
L1471: Subjects received a single oral dose of a combined OC containing 30 µg of EE and 150 µg of LVG. This was followed by a washout period of at least 5 days and not exceeding 7 days.
L1472: ## **Period 3**
L1473: Subjects were administered daily doses of remibrutinib 100 mg BID for 18 days (Day 16 to Day 33). On Day 18 and Day 26, subjects received a single dose of the probe cocktail containing 2 mg of midazolam, 200 mg of caffeine, and 500 mg of tolbutamide approximately 30 minutes after the morning dose of remibrutinib. On Day 30, subjects received a single oral dose of the combined OC (EE 30 µg and LVG 150 µg) approximately 30 minutes after the morning dose of remibrutinib.
L1474: The Applicant assumed that the within-subject variability in the PK parameters for EE and LVG would be lower than the between-subject variability. Consequently, a lower sample size was used for this single-sequence crossover design relative to the sample size that may have been needed for a parallel group design.
L1475: Blood sampling timepoints were collected as follows:
L1476: - Midazolam: Days 1, 18, and 26 (at predose and 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, and 24 hours postdose)
- Tolbutamide: Days 1, 18, and 26 (at predose and 24 hours postdose)
- Caffeine: Days 1, 18, and 26 (at predose and 6 hours postdose)
- EE/LVG: Days 9 and 30 (at predose and 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.5, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, 48, 72, and 96 hours postdose)
- Remibrutinib: Days 16 and 17 (at predose and 6.5 hours postdose); Days 18 and 26 (at predose and 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, and 24 hours postdose); Days 22 and 25 (at 6.5 hours postdose); Day 30 (at predose and 6 hours postdose)
L1477: The Applicant utilized tolbutamide C24h and caffeine C6h to assess drug interactions between remibrutinib and tolbutamide and caffeine. The Applicant's rationale for measuring tolbutamide and caffeine concentrations at a single time point is based on prior studies in the literature, which have suggested that tolbutamide C24h levels may reflect CYP2C9 activity (Fuhr et al. 1996; Jetter et al. 2004). Similarly, caffeine C6h levels may reflect CYP1A2 activity (Fuhr et al. 1996). Additionally, the paraxanthine/caffeine C6h ratio may also reflect CYP1A2 activity (Fuhr et al. 1996).
