L1493: ## Impact of Remibrutinib on OC
L1494: The PK parameters of EE and LVG following a single dose of OC administered with and without remibrutinib 100 mg twice daily are summarized in Table 72 and Table 73, respectively. The coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID with a single dose of OC resulted in a 1.28-fold increase in C_{max}, a 1.16-fold increase in AUC_{0-24h}, and a 1.18-fold increase in AUC_{0-48h} for EE. For LVG, the increases were 1.36-fold in C_{max}, 1.39-fold in AUC_{0-inf}, 1.39-fold in AUC_{0-24h}, and 1.41-fold for AUC_{0-48h} (Table 71).
L1495: Despite these increases, the interaction between remibrutinib and OC does not indicate a clinically significant impact on the safety and efficacy of OC when coadministered with remibrutinib.

L1514: ## Impact of Remibrutinib on Midazolam (CYP3A4 Substrate)
L1515: The PK parameters of midazolam following the administration of a single dose of a probe cocktail, with and without coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID, are summarized in **Table 74**. On Day 18, the coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID with a single dose of the probe cocktail, which included midazolam 2 mg, resulted in a 1.36-fold increase in Cmax, a 1.45-fold increase in AUC0-inf, and a 1.43-fold increase in AUC0-24h for midazolam compared to when midazolam was administered alone. Similarly, on Day 26, the coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID with a single dose of the probe cocktail led to a 1.27-fold increase in Cmax, a 1.43-fold increase in AUC0-inf, and a 1.43-fold increase in AUC0-24h for midazolam compared to when midazolam was administered alone.
L1516: In vitro studies have demonstrated that remibrutinib acts as a reversible and time-dependent inhibitor and inducer of CYP3A4. Consequently, the net effect of remibrutinib on CYP3A4 function may exhibit time-dependent characteristics. For example, reversible inhibition is likely to be more pronounced at the initiation of treatment, while the induction effect may be most evident following multiple days of treatment. Based on the observed effects of remibrutinib on midazolam exposure on Days 18 (3 days following the administration of remibrutinib) and 26 (11 days following administration or remibrutinib), remibrutinib has low potential to inhibit or induce CYP3A4. Time-dependent changes in CYP3A4 activity were not observed as midazolam exposure was comparable on Days 18 and 26.

L1526: ## Impact of Remibrutinib on Caffeine (CYP1A2 Substrate)
L1527: The C6h of caffeine and its metabolite paraxanthine showed no significant difference when the probe cocktail was administered with or without remibrutinib 100 mg BID (Table 75). The coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID with 200 mg of caffeine, resulted in a 3% and 12% decrease in the paraxanthine/caffeine C6h ratio on Days 18 and 26, respectively (Table 71). These findings suggest that multiple oral doses of remibrutinib 100 mg are unlikely to impact the exposure to a single dose of caffeine.
L1528: Based on the observed effects of remibrutinib on the paraxanthine/caffeine C6h ratio on Days 18 and 26 (3 and 11 days, respectively, after the initiation of remibrutinib dosing), remibrutinib has low potential to inhibit or induce CYP1A2.

L1534: ## Impact of Remibrutinib on Tolbutamide (CYP2C9 Substrate)
L1535: The C24h of tolbutamide of the probe cocktail showed no significant differences when tolbutamide was administered with and without remibrutinib 100 mg BID (Table 76). The coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID with a single dose of tolbutamide 500 mg resulted in 1.27- and 1.3-fold increases in tolbutamide C24h on Days 18 and 26, respectively (Table 71).
L1536: While PK parameters such as C_{max} and AUC are commonly used to evaluate drug interactions in clinical studies, the Applicant utilized tolbutamide C24h to assess the drug interaction between remibrutinib and tolbutamide. Studies in the literature suggest that tolbutamide C24h may reflect CYP2C9 activity (Fuhr et al. 1996; Jetter et al. 2004). Jetter et al. (2004), proposed using the natural logarithm of tolbutamide C24h as a metric for CYP2C9 activity because it is strongly correlated with clearance. Tolbutamide C24h was found to be able to differentiate between CYP2C9 *1/*1 and *1/*2 genotypes from *1/*3 and *2/*2 genotypes. The natural logarithm of tolbutamide C24h for CYP2C9 *1/*3 and *2/*2 genotypes was approximately 2-fold higher than that for CYP2C9 *1/*1. However, there was no significant difference between CYP2C9 *1/*1 and *1/*3 or *2/*2 genotypes based on C_{max}, and only a 1.45-fold difference based on AUC_{0-inf}. Consequently, tolbutamide C24h may overestimate the magnitude of drug interaction between remibrutinib and tolbutamide compared to that determined when using C_{max} and AUC. As multiple oral doses of remibrutinib 100 mg yielded an approximate 30% increase in tolbutamide C24h, a lower magnitude may have been observed based on C_{max} and AUC. This is further supported by in vitro data. Although remibrutinib was identified as a CYP2C9 inhibitor in vitro, an in vivo DDI risk is not predicted at clinical concentrations following administration of the recommended dose.
L1537: Based on the observed effects of remibrutinib on tolbutamide exposure (C24h) on Days 18 and 26 (3 and 11 days, respectively, following the initiation of remibrutinib treatment), remibrutinib has low potential to inhibit or induce CYP2C9.

L1547: ## 14.2.7. Dose-Ranging in Subjects With CSU, Study A2201
L1548: Study A2201 was a phase 2b, randomized, double-blind, parallel-group, placebo-controlled study designed to evaluate the efficacy, safety, and tolerability of six different dosing regimens of oral remibrutinib in adult subjects with CSU that remained inadequately controlled despite treatment with second-generation H1-antihistamines. The study design and treatment arms are illustrated in Figure 48. Subjects were randomized equally to one of six remibrutinib treatment arms or placebo for 12 weeks. Dosages of remibrutinib ranged from 10 mg QD up to 100 mg BID. Remibrutinib was administered in the form of hard gelatin capsules, available in strengths of 10 mg, 25 mg, and 50 mg. Randomization was stratified by prior exposure to anti-IgE biologics (including omalizumab and ligelizumab), limited to 40% of the total study population, and by geographical region. The primary objective of the study was to characterize the dose-response relationship of remibrutinib administered once or twice daily in subjects with CSU with respect to change from baseline in UAS7 at Week 4.
L1549: **Figure 48. Study Design, Study A2201**
L1550: Source: Study A2201 Clinical Report, Figure 9-1
L1551: 1) Background therapy was a second generation H1-antihistamine at a locally approved licensed posology that was administered every day with a stable treatment regimen throughout the study (from Day -14 until Day 113).
L1552: 2) Rescue therapy was a second generation H1-antihistamine at a locally approved licensed posology that was eliminated primarily via renal excretion (e.g., cetirizine, levocetirizine or bilastine). The rescue H1-antihistamine had to be different from the background H1-antihistamine and could be given to treat unbearable symptoms (itch) of CSU on a day-to-day basis throughout the study (from Day -14 until Day 113).
L1553: 3) H1-antihistamines used for background therapy and rescue therapy was not changed throughout the study. remibrutinib 10 mg (Batch #1010020203, 1010018765, and 1010024908), remibrutinib 25 mg (Batch #1010020204, 1010018766, and 1010024915), and remibrutinib 50 mg (Batch #1010020206, 1010020205, 1010018767, 1010019287, 1010024299, 1010024909, 1010024910) were used in Study A2201.
L1554: Abbreviations: BSL, baseline; CSU, chronic spontaneous urticaria; LOU064, remibrutinib; n, number of subjects; qd, once daily
L1555: ## Efficacy and Dose-Response
L1556: ## Primary Endpoint (UAS7 Score Change From Baseline at Week 4)
L1557: The primary objective of this study was to establish a dose-response relationship and characterize the dose-response curve for remibrutinib administered either QD or BID at doses of 10 mg, 35 mg, and 100 mg QD, and 10 mg, 25 mg, and 100 mg BID, in comparison to placebo. The primary endpoint was the change from baseline in UAS7 at Week 4.
L1558: At the studied doses, a dose-response relationship was not observed. Based on the analysis of changes in UAS7 scores from baseline at Week 4, all remibrutinib treatment arms demonstrated greater reductions compared to the placebo (as detailed in and Table 77). The most substantial LS mean treatment difference (90% CI; p-value) relative to placebo was observed in the remibrutinib 25-mg BID arm (-14.58, 90% CI: -18.43, -10.73; nominal p&lt;0.0001). Notably, the LS means of treatment differences compared to placebo for the remibrutinib 10 mg QD and 30 mg QD arms were similar to those of the remibrutinib 25-mg BID arm. Specifically, the LS mean treatment difference for the remibrutinib 10 mg QD arm was -13.66 (90% CI: -17.51, -9.81; nominal p&lt;0.0001), and for the remibrutinib 30 mg QD arm, it was -13.64 (90% CI: -17.49, -9.78; nominal p&lt;0.0001). These results indicate a flat dose-response relationship at dosages of 10 mg QD and above.

L1582: ## 14.2.8. Phase 3, Remibrutinib (25 mg BID) in Adult Subjects With CSU, Trial A2301
L1583: Trial A2301 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study designed to establish the efficacy, safety, and tolerability of remibrutinib 25 mg administered twice daily in adult subjects with CSU inadequately controlled by H1-antihistamines, in comparison to a placebo. For full details of the study design and efficacy results, refer to Section 6.
L1584: Sparse pharmacokinetic blood samples were collected at Week 2, Week 12, and Week 52, with sampling limited to two time points at each visit to characterize peak exposures of remibrutinib (45 minutes and 90 minutes postdose).
L1585: ## **Pharmacokinetic Results**
L1586: In the remibrutinib arm, the geometric mean coefficient of variation (CV%) blood concentrations of remibrutinib at 45 minutes and 90 minutes postdose were as follows:
L1587: - Week 2: 41 (142) ng/mL (N=266) and 36.4 (97) ng/mL (N=264)
- Week 12: 39.4 (145) ng/mL (N=252) and 35.5 (97) ng/mL (N=253)
- Week 52: 34.1 (149) ng/mL (N=219) and 32.4 (113) ng/mL (N=218), respectively
L1588: Remibrutinib concentrations remained consistent across all visits.
L1589: ## 14.2.9. Phase 3, Remibrutinib (25 mg BID) in Adult Subjects With CSU, Trial A2302
L1590: Trial A2302 mirrors the design and methodology of Trial A2301, with the additional inclusion of a subpopulation of Chinese subjects to assess the exposure to remibrutinib. For full details on the study design and efficacy results, refer to Section 6.
L1591: ## Pharmacokinetic Results
L1592: The pharmacokinetic analysis revealed that remibrutinib concentrations remained consistent, across all visits, mirroring the findings from Trial A2301.
L1593: Sparse pharmacokinetic blood samples were collected at Week 2, Week 12, and Week 52, with sampling limited to two time points at each visit to characterize peak exposures of remibrutinib (45 minutes and 90 minutes postdose). The geometric mean (CV%) blood concentrations of remibrutinib at 45 minutes and 90 minutes postdose were as follows:
L1594: - Week 2: 41.3 (113) ng/mL (N=251) and 39 (91) ng/mL (N=250)
- Week 12: 37.9 (154) ng/mL (N=237) and 38 (101) ng/mL (N=232)
- Week 52: 37.2 (144) ng/mL (N=191) and 36 (76) ng/mL (N=193), respectively
L1595: Overall, the exposure to remibrutinib is comparable between Trials A2301 and A2302 with high variability in PK data. Note that the primary difference between Trials A2301 and A2302 is the inclusion of Chinese subjects with CSU in Trial A2302.
L1596: ## 14.2.10. Phase 3, Remibrutinib (25 mg BID) in Adult Japanese Subjects With CSU, Study A1301
L1597: Study A1301 was an open-label study designed to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and efficacy of remibrutinib administered at a dose of 25 mg twice daily in adult Japanese subjects with CSU who were inadequately controlled by H1-antihistamine therapy. The pharmacokinetic sampling methodology employed in this study was the same as that used in Trials A2301 and A2302 (i.e., at 45 and 90 minutes postdose at Weeks 2, 12, and 52).
L1598: ## Pharmacokinetic Results
L1599: The geometric mean (CV%) blood concentrations of remibrutinib at 45 minutes and 90 minutes postdose were as follows:
L1600: - Week 2: 53.9 (96) ng/mL (N=70) and 44.6 (64) ng/mL (N=69)
- Week 12: 50.8 (110) ng/mL (N=71) and 47.2 (60) ng/mL (N=71)
- Week 52: 43.5 (150) ng/mL (N=68) and 45.7 (62) ng/mL (N=68), respectively
L1601: In comparison to Trials A2301 and A2302, the exposure to remibrutinib in Japanese subjects with CSU in Study A1301 is slightly higher. However, a definitive conclusion on exposure comparison can't be made due to the observed high variability in PK data.

L1609: ## 14.3.1.1. Quantification of Remibrutinib in Human Blood
L1610: A liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry (LC-MS/MS) bioanalytical assay was developed and validated for the quantification of remibrutinib concentrations in human blood including methods DMPK R1600407, DMPK R1800081, and DMPK R1900112a. Method DMPK R1800080 was developed and validated for the quantification of remibrutinib-[^{13}\text{C}_3, ^{15}\text{N}] (i.e., SIL-remibrutinib) concentrations in human blood from Study X2103 (refer to Section 14.2.6.1). A summary of the method validations for remibrutinib in blood is presented in Table 80. The method validation for remibrutinib-[^{13}\text{C}_3, ^{15}\text{N}] in human blood is shown in Table 81. The methods were deemed acceptable for the determination of remibrutinib in human blood samples.
L1611: For most studies (except for Studies X2104 and X2105), the number of samples used for incurred sample reanalysis (ISR) fell below the number recommended in the guidance for industry *M10 Bioanalytical Method Validation and Study Sample Analysis* (November 2022) (i.e., 10% of the first 1000 samples plus 5% of the number of samples that exceed 1000). Nevertheless, across studies, the ISR pass rate was at least 86%. In addition, for all trials with samples analyzed using method 1800081, the total number of samples used for ISR and their pass rates are adequate to meet the guidance-recommended criteria (refer to Table 2.2a in the Applicant's Information Request Response dated June 10, 2025). Given that Methods 1800081 and 1600407 were developed at the same laboratory (i.e., [REDACTED] (b) (4)) and are based on the same assay principle (i.e., LC-MS/MS), the available data are adequate to establish reliability of the bioanalytical methods.
L1612: ## 14.3.1.2. Quantification of Remibrutinib in Human Urine
L1613: Method DMPK R1600408 was developed and validated utilizing LC-MS/MS at the [REDACTED] (b) (4) bioanalytical site in [REDACTED] (b) (4) for the quantification of remibrutinib concentrations in human urine in Studies X2101, X1101, and X2104. A summary of the method validation for remibrutinib in urine is presented in Table 81. The method was deemed acceptable for the determination of remibrutinib in human urine samples.

L1632: ## 14.3.3. Quantitation of Radiation From [^{14}\text{C}]-Remibrutinib in Human Blood, Plasma, Urine, and Feces
L1633: ## **Plasma and Whole Blood**
L1634: The concentrations of total radioactivity in whole blood, plasma, urine, fecal and expired air samples obtained from Study X2104 were determined using accelerator mass spectrometry (AMS). Initially, urine and feces samples were analyzed using liquid scintillation counting (LSC); when the radioactivity concentrations by LSC dropped below the LLOQ, AMS was used to measure total radioactivity in urine and fecal samples.
L1635: A reference standard sucrose-8542 (C_{12}H_{22}O_{11}) with a certified ^{14}C/^{12}C isotope ratio was used as system suitability sample with ^{14}C/^{12}C ratio = 1.5061 \times 10^{-12} dpm/g, C = 20.42, and Bq/g C = 0.3403786. The sucrose was always analyzed at the start of each run of samples (in triplicate). When the measured ^{14}C/^{12}C ratio (average of at least 2) deviated &gt;15% from the certified ratio, the run was rejected, and the samples were reprocessed and reanalyzed.
L1636: Two AMS QC samples were included in triplicate in each analysis. These QC samples were blank pooled plasma spiked with a known amount of ^{14}C-paracetamol. Thereafter, the ^{14}C/^{12}C ratio (nominal ratio) was determined using AMS analysis. The average ^{14}C/^{12}C ratio of the QC samples in an analytical run must be within 85 to 115% of the nominal ratio. In addition, the CV should be \le 15\%. The QC results fulfilled the requirements for all analytical runs.
L1637: All homogenized feces samples were analyzed in triplicate; if the CV is &gt;30%, the sample was reanalyzed. All plasma, blood and urine samples were analyzed in duplicate; if the CV is &gt;20%, the sample is reanalyzed. Thirty-seven samples (one urine, three plasma and thirty-three blood samples of the oral arm and two blood samples of the intravenous arm) with CV% &gt;20% were reanalyzed. For a number of blood samples, the CV remained &gt;20% after multiple reanalyses and were reported, as agreed as not reportable. Overall, the AMS analytical runs met the acceptance criteria.
L1638: ## Urine and Feces
L1639: The [^{14}C] radioactivity concentrations in urine, feces, and vomitus (if applicable) were determined using LSC (for feces and urine) or AMS (when radioactivity concentrations by LSC dropped below the LLOQ). All samples and stock solutions and dilutions thereof were stored at a nominal -20^{\circ}C.
L1640: All QC samples (50, 5000, and 40000 dpm/g) in human urine were prepared by spiking human urine with a reference standard [1-methyl-^{14}C]-caffeine. QC samples (100, 6400, and 128000 dpm/g) in human feces were prepared by spiking human feces homogenate with the reference standard. For each run, the absolute bias had to be \le 10\% of the nominal value (\le 15\% at the lowest level using the low-level count mode, LLCM) for at least four out of six QC samples. At least one QC result had to be accepted for each QC level. The validated range for the analysis of urine and feces samples using LSC is 10 to 1000000 dpm/mL and 40 to 1000000 dpm/g, respectively.
L1641: Measurements of urine samples were performed in duplicate and feces samples were analyzed in triplicate. The samples were placed in the LSC for at least 30 minutes before counting. The CV over the duplicate/triplicate analysis had to be \le 15\% for samples with an activity level &gt;250 dpm/g or \le 20\% for samples with an activity level \le 250 dpm/g. When the criteria for CV over the duplicate/triplicate analysis were not reached, the duplicate/triplicate results were rejected, and the samples were reanalyzed in duplicate. These criteria did not apply for study samples with results below the LLOQ, as these samples were analyzed using AMS.
L1642: The method was acceptable for assessment of [^{14}C]-remibrutinib in human urine and feces in clinical samples obtained from Study X2104.
L1643: ## 14.3.4. Quantification of Other Species in DDI Studies
L1644: In drug-drug interaction trials, a number of coadministered drugs or endogenous biomarkers (i.e., coproporphyrin I) were analyzed. These compounds include tolbutamide, ethinylestradiol, levonorgestrel, midazolam, 1-hydroxymidazolam, caffeine, and paraxanthine in Study X2102; and coproporphyrin I, rosuvastatin, and digoxin in Study A02103. The bioanalytical methods and method performance for the coadministered drugs in the DDI studies are summarized in Table 83. The bioanalytical method was adequately validated and met the acceptance criteria suggested in the FDA guidance for industry *M10 Bioanalytical Method Validation and Study Sample Analysis* (November 2022).

L1663: ## 14.5.1.2. Introduction
L1664: The primary objectives of the Applicant's analysis were to:
L1665: - To characterize the remibrutinib pharmacokinetics in subjects with CSU for total daily dose between 10 mg and 200 mg using pooled concentration data at steady state from healthy volunteers and adult subjects with CSU.
- To assess the effects of baseline covariates (age, weight, race/region, creatinine clearance) and usage of PPIs/antacids on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib in the studied population.
L1666: ## 14.5.1.3. Model Development
L1667: ## 14.5.1.3.1. Data
L1668: The PK analyses for remibrutinib were based on PK data from three phase 1 studies in healthy subjects, one phase 2 study in subjects with CSU, and three phase 3 studies in subjects with CSU. Brief descriptions of the studies included are presented in Table 85.
L1669: The overall percent of BLQ samples for remibrutinib was 12.6%. These observations were included in the population PK analysis as interval-censored. The final NONMEM data file for remibrutinib model development contained 7088 observations from 1152 subjects. Table 86 provides summary statistics of the baseline demographic covariates in the PK analysis dataset.

L1706: ## 14.5.1.3.2. Base Model
L1707: The population PK model was developed using a nonlinear mixed-effects modeling approach with the stochastic approximation expectation-maximization algorithm implemented in MONOLIX (version 2021R2).
L1708: A population PK model for remibrutinib was previously developed based on pooled steady-state data of Studies X2101 and A2201 with total daily dose between 10 mg and 200 mg. The model was described as a two-compartment, first-order absorption model with a lag time and linear elimination. The previous structural popPK model was used in the current popPK model based on the new pooled dataset and with full reevaluation of the correlation matrix.
L1709: Interindividual variability was modelled assuming a log-normal distribution for patient level random effects. Residual variability was described using proportional error model. The quality of the model fit was assessed by diagnostic plots.
L1710: ## 14.5.1.3.3. Covariate Analysis
L1711: The effect of body size covariates (e.g., body weight, body mass index, FFM, and FM) on apparent clearance (CL/F) or apparent central volume of distribution (V_1/F) was evaluated as part of the base model. Weight with both estimated allometric scaling and fixed allometric scaling of 0.75 for clearances and 1 for volumes was tested. The impact of other prespecified
L1712: covariates were investigated: age (on CL/F), gender (on CL/F), race/region (on CL/F), renal impairment (assessed as creatinine clearance on CL/F), and usage of PPI/antacids (on CL/F, V1/F, apparent intercompartmental clearance and apparent peripheral volume of distribution [V2/F]). The significance of the covariates after inclusion of the body size effect was assessed using a full covariate modeling approach. The impact of usage of PPI/antacids was explored graphically as inclusion of the PPI/antacid usage as time-dependent covariate led to unstable models. Hepatic impairment was not assessed as a covariate in the model. According to Applicant's response to Information Request, subjects with hepatic impairment were not enrolled in the studies included in the population PK analysis. In the full modeling dataset, a small fraction of subjects showed mildly elevated levels of ALT (n=94, 8%), AST (n=34, 3%) or INR (n=34, 3%) in the overall dataset, and only one subject had total bilirubin \ge 1.5 \times ULN.
L1713: Primary covariates evaluated in the development of PK model was included in Table 86.
L1714: ## 14.5.1.4. Final Model
L1715: A two-compartment model with first-order absorption with fixed lag time and constant clearance described the PK data of remibrutinib (Figure 52, Figure 53). Statistically significant covariate effects on PK model parameters were the followings: FFM and FM on CL/F, race effect on CL/F, and FM effect on V1/F. Age, gender, and mild renal impairment are not significant covariates. Based on the graphical exploration of observed data, the use of PPI/antacids does not appear to have effect on remibrutinib exposure (Figure 51).
L1716: The parameter estimates for the final covariate model are listed in Table 87. The RSE for all parameters was &lt;40%. The intersubject variability for PK parameters was generally high especially for V1/F, the apparent intercompartmental clearance, and V2/F, which ranges from 127% to 4386%. Moderate to high eta-shrinkage was observed for PK parameters. According to the Applicant, the high intersubject variability is due to large variability observed in PK concentrations.
L1717: The effects of FFM, FM, Mainland Chinese subjects, Japanese subjects, and other Asian subjects, on the AUC of remibrutinib are explored in Table 88. The effects of evaluated intrinsic factors (FFM, FM, Mainland Chinese subjects, Japanese subjects, and other Asian subjects) are generally small (20 to 30%). According to the Applicant, potential explanation for the effect of FFM and FM on remibrutinib may be remibrutinib is a lipophilic compound (LogP ~3), which can distribute into human fat tissue and result in body composition as an important factor for drug distribution and metabolism. The effect of body weight was small based on the results of simulated AUC in subjects with weight of 49 kg (5th percentile) and 109 kg (95th percentile) assuming the ratio of FFM to FM of 2:1. As the FDA requested, the Applicant performed additional simulation to assess the effect of body weight on remibrutinib exposure by using resampled demographic characteristics from the population PK data, which keeps the natural correlations among body fat percentage, body weight, and gender and variability in body composition. The results confirmed that the impact of body weight on remibrutinib exposures is not clinically relevant (Table 89). Given the high intersubject variability of remibrutinib pharmacokinetics, the effect of explored intrinsic factors is unlikely to be clinically meaningful.

L1737: ## 14.5.2. Exposure-Response Analysis
L1738: ## 14.5.2.1. Review Summary
L1739: There was a positive E-R relationship for both continuous and categorical efficacy endpoints (UAS7, ISS7, and HSS7 response) with plateau of response achieved at the proposed remibrutinib dose regimen.
L1740: The E-R analysis for safety did not identify a significant relationship between remibrutinib exposure and AEs and AEs of special interest. The E-R analysis for remibrutinib and safety laboratory and vital sign parameters is considered exploratory due to the limited range of exposure from one dose level. Among the subjects with chronic spontaneous urticaria who received remibrutinib 25 mg twice daily, there was no clinically relevant association between remibrutinib exposure and changes in laboratory parameters of blood chemistry, selected blood cell counts, immunoglobulin concentrations, selected coagulation panel parameters, diastolic and systolic blood pressure (BP).
L1741: ## 14.5.2.2. Introductions
L1742: The primary objectives of the Applicant's E-R analysis were to:
L1743: - To explore the exposure-response relationship between remibrutinib concentrations and UAS7, ISS7, and HSS7 in adult subjects with chronic spontaneous urticaria at selected time points.
- To explore the effect of selected intrinsic and extrinsic factors on clinical outcome (UAS7, ISS7, and HSS7 response) in adult subjects with CSU.
L1744: - To develop nonlinear mixed effects models characterizing the exposure-response relationship to remibrutinib for efficacy and describing longitudinal changes in UAS7 (and possibly ISS7/HSS7) in adult subjects with CSU.
- To explore the exposure-response relationship between remibrutinib concentrations and safety in adult subjects with CSU based on the selected adverse events and changes in selected laboratory and vital sign parameters.
L1745: PK exposure metrics including average concentration at steady state (C_{\text{avg,ss}}), and individual empirical Bayes estimates were predicted based on post hoc parameter estimates from the final population PK model. Of note, 18% of subjects included in the current analyses did not have any PK measurements and C_{\text{avg,ss}} in those individuals were imputed based on population CL/F estimate adjusted for individual covariate values. A sensitivity analysis for the key exposure-efficacy and exposure-safety was conducted by excluding individuals with missing PK measurements, and the interpretation of its results was in line with the analyses presented in this report.
L1746: ## 14.5.2.3. E-R Analysis for Efficacy
L1747: The efficacy E-R analysis was based on data from one phase 2 (A2201) and two phase 3 trials (A2301 and A2302). The primary efficacy endpoints explored in the analysis include the followings: absolute change from baseline in HSS7, absolute change from baseline in ISS7, absolute change from baseline in UAS7, achievement of complete absence of itch and hives (\text{UAS7}=0), and achievement of disease activity control (\text{UAS7} \le 6).
L1748: The analysis data included 45230 measurements for each efficacy endpoint from 1221 subjects. MONOLIX (version 2021R2) and R software (version 4.1.0) were used for the E-R analysis for efficacy.
L1749: For continuous efficacy endpoints (changes from baseline in UAS7, ISS7, and HSS7), the results from a nonlinear model with sigmoidal E_{\text{max}} function of exposure metrics and baseline value as a covariate suggested a positive E-R relationship. The plateau of response was achieved at low remibrutinib exposures, and the remibrutinib dose regimen investigated in phase 3 studies (25 mg BID) provided PK exposures in the plateau range of the E-R relationship (Figure 54). Similarly, for binomial efficacy variables, the results from a logistic regression model with sigmoidal E_{\text{max}} function suggested that the remibrutinib dose regimen investigated in phase 3 studies (25 mg BID) provided PK exposures in the plateau range of the exposure-response relationship (Figure 55). Longitudinal nonlinear mixed effects exposure-response model for UAS7 was attempted but not developed due to issues with model convergence and identifiability of parameters estimates.

L1764: ## 14.5.2.4. E-R Analysis for Safety
L1765: The safety E-R analysis was based on data from one phase 2 (A2201) and two phase 3 trials (A2301 and A2302). The safety endpoints included AEs monitoring, physical examinations, monitoring of laboratory markers in blood and urine, and vital signs. The AEs of special interest included infections, bleeding, and cytopenias. The laboratory and vital sign parameters included laboratory parameters of blood chemistry panel, blood cell counts, immunoglobulin concentrations, selected parameters of coagulation, diastolic BP, and systolic BP. R software (version 4.1.0) were used for the E-R analysis for safety.
L1766: For AEs, three data pools were created based on differences in the design of the analyzed studies, in particular duration of treatment and the placebo-controlled period. Logistic regression with log-transformed C_{\text{avg,ss}} were conducted to test the influence of exposure on AEs in subjects on active treatment in the three pooled safety datasets. Individual remibrutinib C_{\text{avg,ss}} were comparable between subjects with and without occurrence of any AEs across all three E-R safety data pools. Logistic regression fitted to all three E-R safety data pools did not show a statistically significant relationship for occurrence of any AEs with increasing remibrutinib C_{\text{avg,ss}}. Similar results were observed for occurrence of SAEs which had rates below 5%. Logistic regression fitted to all three E-R safety data pools did not show a statistically significant relationship for occurrence of AESIs (bleeding, cytopenia, and infection) with increasing remibrutinib C_{\text{avg,ss}} (Figure 56).
L1767: For laboratory and vital-sign parameters, two data pools from Trials A2301 and A2302 with one included placebo group and one excluded placebo group were explored. Exploratory and MMRM analyses indicated no clear evidence of a relationship between remibrutinib PK exposures (C_{\text{avg,ss}}) and changes in laboratory parameters of blood chemistry panel (ALT, amylase, alkaline phosphatase, AST, bilirubin, creatine kinase, creatinine, gamma-glutamyl transferase, and lipase), selected blood cell counts (erythrocytes, leukocytes, lymphocytes, neutrophils, and platelets), IgE concentrations, selected laboratory parameters from coagulation panel (activated partial thromboplastin time, prothrombin INR, and prothrombin time), diastolic and systolic BP in Trials A2301 and A2302.
L1768: The absolute bilirubin levels, erythrocyte counts, creatinine and creatine kinase levels were higher in subjects with low remibrutinib C_{\text{avg,ss}} (in 1st quartile of distribution) than in subjects with higher remibrutinib C_{\text{avg,ss}} (2nd to 4th quartile) or placebo. According to the Applicant, this could be attributed to confounding factors of body size (e.g., fat mass or fat free mass) which affect both remibrutinib pharmacokinetics and listed safety parameters.
L1769: The trend of elevations in median lymphocyte counts and decrease in platelet counts with increasing remibrutinib exposure is of limited clinical relevance given the transient character and small magnitude of change with median levels remaining within limits of normal, and no signal for AEs of lymphocytosis and bleeding AESIs. Similarly, the trend of elevation in median bilirubin concentrations, and decrease in creatinine concentrations and neutrophil counts with increasing remibrutinib exposure is of no clinical relevance given the marginal magnitude of these changes. The observed trends for positive correlation between C_{\text{avg,ss}} and change from baseline in IgA and IgG concentrations, and a negative correlation with change from baseline in IgM concentration is of limited clinical relevance, given small magnitude of observed changes, inconsistent direction of the remibrutinib C_{\text{avg,ss}} effect on those globulins and no increase in infection rates in remibrutinib arms in Trials A2301 and A2302.

L1779: ## 14.5.3. PBPK Modeling
L1780: ## Executive Summary
L1781: The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant's PBPK modeling analyses to predict the following:
L1782: - The effect of strong (ketoconazole, itraconazole, and ritonavir), moderate (fluconazole, erythromycin), and weak (fluvoxamine) CYP3A inhibitors on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib at doses of 25 mg BID.
- The effect of strong (carbamazepine and rifampicin), moderate (efavirenz), and weak (dexamethasone) CYP3A4 inducers on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib at doses of 25 mg BID.
- The effect of multiple doses of remibrutinib 25 mg BID on the pharmacokinetics of substrates of CYP3A (midazolam), P-gp (digoxin), and BCRP (rosuvastatin).
L1783: The Divisions of Inflammation and Immune Pharmacology and Pharmacometrics have reviewed the PBPK submission (Report DMPK R2301014, modeling and simulation files, and response to Information Requests dated March 24, 2025 (supporting document number [SDN] 0007) and March 14, 2025 (SDN 0005)) to conclude the following:
L1784: - Simulations predicted 3.21- to 3.65-fold increase in AUC of remibrutinib when multiple doses of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were coadministered with multiple doses of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors.
- Simulations predicted 1.89- to 2.27-fold increase in AUC of remibrutinib when multiple doses of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were coadministered with multiple doses of moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors.
- Simulations predicted 74.3% to 90.5% reduction in AUC of remibrutinib when multiple doses of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were coadministered with multiple doses of strong CYP3A4 inducers. Simulations predicted 64% reduction in AUC of remibrutinib when multiple doses of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were coadministered with multiple doses of moderate CYP3A4 inducers.
- Simulations, in combination with clinical DDI results, suggest a low potential for a clinically relevant effect of remibrutinib 25 mg BID on CYP3A4 substrates.
- The magnitude of the interaction effect of remibrutinib 25 mg BID on the pharmacokinetics of substrates of P-gp and BCRP could not be confidently predicted by PBPK.
L1785: ## PBPK Model for Remibrutinib
L1786: ## Background
L1787: Remibrutinib binds to the BTK protein, thereby inhibiting its enzymatic activity. Notably, remibrutinib demonstrates TMDD at lower doses, a characteristic that influences its pharmacokinetic profile and therapeutic efficacy.
L1788: Remibrutinib is predominantly metabolized by CYP3A4, with an estimated fractional contribution of CYP3A4 (f_{mCYP3A4}) of approximately 0.43. Other non-CYP-mediated pathways appear to also contribute to remibrutinib metabolic clearance, while renal clearance is minimal (&lt;2%).
L1789: In vitro studies have demonstrated that remibrutinib acts as a CYP3A-reversible inhibitor (K_{i,u}=14.1\muM) and time-dependent inhibitor (K_{i,u}=6.9\muM, K_{inact}=0.612 1/h). Furthermore, in vitro investigations have revealed that remibrutinib induces CYP3A4.
L1790: Remibrutinib exhibits high passive permeability at concentrations of 50\muM and above, while at lower concentrations, its permeability is diminished due to an apically directed P-gp-mediated efflux process. This efflux is not significantly influenced by BCRP or multidrug resistance-associated protein 2. Additionally, remibrutinib is not a substrate for the hepatic uptake transporters OATP1B1 and OATP1B3. As a precipitant, remibrutinib demonstrates clinically relevant potential to inhibit intestinal P-gp (K_{i,u}=0.382\muM, R=1.02 [hepatic], R=517 [intestinal]), and BCRP (K_{i,u}=0.467\muM, R=1.01 [hepatic], R=423 [intestinal]). The R value denotes the static DDI risk assessment for remibrutinib at a dosage of 25 mg twice daily (see Section 8.2).
L1791: Multiple oral doses of remibrutinib at 100 mg BID did not significantly alter the PK parameters of a single oral dose of combined OCs containing EE 30 µg and LVG 150 µg. Similarly, remibrutinib did not significantly impact the PK parameters of midazolam (a CYP3A4 substrate) or rosuvastatin (a substrate for BCRP, OATP1B1, and OAT3) (see Section 14.2.6.2 and Section 14.2.6.3). The increases in PK parameters for these substrates were less than 1.7-fold when compared to their administration alone. However, coadministration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID with digoxin 0.25 mg resulted in a 2.08-fold increase in C_{max}, a 1.45-fold increase in AUC_{last}, and a 1.4-fold increase AUC_{0-inf} of digoxin (see Section 14.2.6.2). Additionally, remibrutinib did not exhibit the potential to inhibit the hepatic uptake of coproporphyrin I, a biomarker for OATP1B1 substrate.
L1792: Grapefruit juice, a moderate to strong inhibitor of gut CYP3A4, did not increase the exposure to remibrutinib (Study 2103). The F_{gut} was estimated from the dose-normalized AUC_{0-24h} values of 4.89 h*ng/mL/mg and 6.28 h*ng/mL/mg for remibrutinib when administered alone and in the presence of grapefruit juice, respectively. The F_{gut} was estimated to be 0.78, indicating a lesser contribution of gut CYP3A4 in the metabolism of remibrutinib compared to hepatic CYP3A4 (see Section 14.2.6.1).
L1793: Ritonavir, a strong inhibitor of both gut and hepatic CYP3A4/P-gp, significantly increased the dose-normalized C_{max} and AUC_{0-24h} of orally administered remibrutinib by 3.32-fold and 4.27-fold, respectively. Conversely, carbamazepine, a strong inducer of CYP3A4, decreased C_{max,ss}, AUC_{tau,ss}, and AUC_{last,ss} of remibrutinib 100 mg BID by 74%, 77%, and 78%, respectively (see Sections 14.2.6.1 and 14.2.6.2).
L1794: ## Method
L1795: ## Software
L1796: Simcyp Population-based Simulator, version 21, release 1 (V21R1) and Simcyp® In Vitro Data Analysis (SIVA) Toolkit® version 4 (Certara UK Limited, Sheffield, United Kingdom) was used for the simulations.
L1797: ## Model Development
L1798: The input parameters for the remibrutinib PBPK model are detailed in Table 90. The foundational remibrutinib base model was developed utilizing available in vitro absorption, ADME data, along with clinical DDI data with ritonavir (Study X2103). Remibrutinib is formulated as a FCT [REDACTED](b)(4). Thus, the oral absorption of remibrutinib was predicted through a mechanistic approach, considering both the biopharmaceutic properties of the drug substance and formulation effect, using the advanced dissolution, absorption, and metabolism model within the Simcyp Simulator.

L1815: ## Distribution, Metabolism, and Clearance
L1816: The full PBPK model was used to describe the distribution of remibrutinib. The scalar for the tissue-partition coefficient was optimized from 1 to 0.17, resulting in a final V_{ss} of 0.63 L/kg. This value is generally comparable to the V_{ss} of 0.81\pm 0.22 L/kg observed following IV administration in Study X2103.
L1817: CYP3A4 was estimated to contribute to 97.8% of the oxidative hepatic metabolism of remibrutinib based on findings from the study in human liver microsomes (DMPK R1600559). Clinically, the remibrutinib f_mCYP3A4 was determined to be 43% at steady-state exposure based on the findings from the clinical DDI with ritonavir (see Section 14.2.6.1). Additionally, following IV administration in the human ADME study, 40% of the detected drug-related material in the excreta (urine and feces) were identified as oxidative metabolites, while approximately 17% were classified as mixed metabolites from glucuronidation, hydrolysis, or glutathione conjugation with an oxidative metabolism step (unknown as primary and/or secondary step) (see Section 14.2.2).
L1818: The retrograde method was employed to calculate the CYP3A4-mediated intrinsic clearance, utilizing a f_mCYP3A4 value of 0.43 and an estimated IV CL of 72 L/h. This approach predicted a steady-state IV clearance of 70.1 L/h (uninhibited IV CL) in Study X2103. To consider the potential for CYP3A4 saturation, the parameters V_{max}=3.9 pmol/min/mol of CYP3A4 and K_{m,u}=1.5\mu M were used in the model instead of the intrinsic clearance value. The CLr was estimated to be approximately 1.224 L/h based on Study X2101. The remaining clearance attributed to non-CYP3A4 metabolic pathways (hydrolysis and glutathione conjugation) was entered into the model as additional systemic clearance. The disposition scheme of remibrutinib, with estimates for pathways contribution (observed and predicted) at steady state (50-mg daily oral dose) is shown in Figure 57.
L1819: **Figure 57. Observed and Predicted Estimates for the Disposition Pathways of Remibrutinib at Steady State**
L1820: Observed pathway contributions to overall systemic clearance at steady state, white boxes; Resultant PBPK model predicted pathway contributions to overall systemic clearance at steady state, blue boxes
L1821: 1 Oral absorption of remibrutinib was estimated to be approximately 89% based on CLOU064X2103
L1822: 2 Measured bioavailability: 34% CLOU064X2103
L1823: 3 Fractional contribution of CYP enzymes based on clinical DDI data (CLOU064X2103)
L1824: Source: Study 2301014: Final physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) model and simulations for remibrutinib, Figure 3-1
Abbreviations: CYP, cytochrome P450; DDI, drug-drug interaction; PBPK, physiologically based pharmacokinetic
L1825: ## TMDD
L1826: Remibrutinib is an inhibitor of BTK and exhibits TMDD. The PBPK model for remibrutinib was integrated with a PD model (scripted in Lua within the Simcyp Simulator v 21R1) to account for the time-dependent changes in systemic clearance of remibrutinib due to target binding saturation following repeated dosing. This PD model represents the "net effect" of the irreversible binding of remibrutinib to BTK, aggregating binding events and target expression across all tissues within the systemic compartment. This semimechanistic approach describes the increased clearance of remibrutinib on Day 1 due to TMDD and the target saturation observed at steady state and/or at higher remibrutinib doses. Importantly, the PD model was implemented to describe the TMDD process and its effect on the pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib but was not designed to elucidate remibrutinib-BTK binding kinetics across tissues or to be linked to remibrutinib efficacy due to its limited parameters. The PD model is described by the following equations:
L1827: ## **Equation 1. PD Model**
L1828: \frac{dTarget}{dt} = k_{syn} - k_{deg} \cdot Target - k_{irr} \cdot C_{blood} \cdot Target \quad (3)
L1829: \frac{dComplex}{dt} = k_{irr} \cdot C_{blood,free} \cdot Target - k_{deg} \cdot Complex \quad (4)
L1830: \% Target_{occ} = 100 \cdot \frac{Complex}{Target + Complex} \quad (5)
L1831: where Target, Complex and Target_occ represent the concentrations of BTK and BTK-remibrutinib complex as well as the BTK occupancy, respectively; k_{syn} is the zero order target synthesis rate (\mu\text{M}/\text{h}); k_{deg} (\text{h}^{-1}) is the target turnover; k_{irr} is the irreversible binding constant (1/(\mu\text{M} \cdot \text{h})) and C_{blood,free} is the unbound blood concentration of remibrutinib.
L1832: Source: Study 2301014: Final physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) model and simulations for remibrutinib
Abbreviations: BTK, Bruton's tyrosine kinase; C_{blood,free} unbound blood concentration of remibrutinib; dComplex, change in BTK-remibrutinib complex; dt, change in time; dTarget, change in the concentrations of BTK; k_{deg}, target turnover; k_{irr}, irreversible binding constant; k_{syn}, zero order target synthesis rate; Target_occ,
L1833: In response to an FDA request (dated March 12, 2025) to justify the necessity of accounting for the TMDD process in the remibrutinib PBPK model, the Applicant clarified that the exposure differences between Day 1 and Day 12 in Study X2101 were up to 4.86-fold at the dose of 10 mg once daily, which then decreased to 1.14-fold at the higher dose of 600 mg once daily (see Section 14.2.1.1)) due to the time-dependent changes in systemic clearance of remibrutinib by target binding saturation following repeated dosing.
L1834: The FDA also requested the Applicant to demonstrate the performance of the PD model for different sets of PD parameters evaluated within the model. The initial TMDD parameters (degradation constant [k_{deg}], irreversible binding constant [k_{irr}], and synthesis rate constant [k_{syn}]) were manually adjusted in a “top-down” manner to predict remibrutinib pharmacokinetics in Study X2101 after a single dose at 1.5, 5, 15, 30, 60, and 100 mg, and subsequently the PK parameters after 10 and 25 mg once daily. The degradation rate constant (k_{deg}) was maintained at 0.01 \text{ h}^{-1}, consistent with the estimated k_{deg} value of 0.00976 \text{ h}^{-1} obtained from the PK/PD analysis of Study X2101. The PBPK input values for the k_{irr} and k_{syn} of 500 \text{ 1}/(\mu\text{M} \cdot \text{h}) and 0.045 \mu\text{M}/\text{h}, respectively, demonstrated good performance among other evaluated values (data not shown). Of note, parameter estimation or sensitivity analysis functions for the PD parameters were not feasible in the current version of the simulator (Simcyp v 21R1). Therefore, statistical analysis and visual predictive checks were employed to assess the PD model performance.
L1835: Further, the Applicant demonstrated the sensitivity of C_{max} and AUC to target abundance (R_{max} = k_{syn}/k_{deg}) and k_{irr} (=k_{on}) to support the manually optimized k_{irr} and k_{syn} values (Figure 58).
L1836: **Figure 58. Parameter Sensitivity Analysis of R_{max} (=K_{syn}/K_{deg}, K_{deg} = 0.01 h^{-1}) and K_{on} on C_{max} and AUC**
L1837: Source: Response to FDA information request dated March 12, 2025, SDN 003, Module 1.11.3
Abbreviations: AUC, area under the concentration-time curve; C_{max}, maximum plasma concentration; K_{deg}, degradation constant; K_{on}, irreversible binding constant; K_{syn}, synthesis constant; R_{max}, target abundance
L1838: ## Drug Interactions
L1839: The in vitro parameters for CYP enzymes inhibition and induction and transporters inhibition are listed in Table 90.
L1840: ## CYP3A4
L1841: Remibrutinib exhibits characteristics of both a reversible and time-dependent inhibitor and inducer of CYP3A4 in vitro. An initial PBPK model using the in vitro estimates of CYP3A4 interaction parameters did not accurately estimate the observed magnitude of the DDI between remibrutinib (100 mg BID) and midazolam (a CYP3A4 substrate) in Study X2102. Any of the induction parameters (i.e., maximal fold induction of an enzyme or gene expression [Ind_{max}], substrate concentration that induce enzyme by 50% [IndC_{50}]) or time-dependent inhibition (TDI) parameters (i.e., inhibition constant [K_I] and enzyme inactivation constant [k_{inact}]) could be adjusted accordingly to recover a clinical DDI data. Due to identifiability challenges associated with the optimization of TDI and induction parameters, local and global sensitivity analyses (GSA) were then performed to evaluate the impact of individual parameter variations on the predicted DDI and guide the rationale for parameter optimization. The GSA revealed that the most influential parameters affecting the AUC and C_{max} ratios were, in order of significance, K_I (=K_{app}), Ind_{max}, k_{inact}, and IndC_{50} (Figure 59).
L1842: The local sensitivity analysis was subsequently conducted using a range that spanned three times the standard deviation of the in vitro values (0.9 to 12.90\mu M for K_I and 1 to 67.7-fold for Ind_{max}), predicted AUC ratios ranging from 0.7 to 1.6 and C_{max} ratios from 0.7 to 1.48 (Figure 60). Based on these findings, the in vitro K_I value was reduced from 6.9 to 1.38\mu M to best align with the observed AUC and C_{max} ratios of the DDI with midazolam. This optimization was achieved without altering the in vitro estimated values for the reversible inhibition, induction, or the TDI k_{inact}.
L1843: **Figure 59.** Global Sensitivity Analysis of Remibrutinib CYP3A4 In Vitro Interaction (Reversible and Time-Dependent Inhibition, Induction) Properties on the (a) C_{max} and (b) AUC Ratio of the Drug-Drug Interaction Between Midazolam and Remibrutinib 100 mg BID
L1844: Source: Study 2301014: Final physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) model and simulations for remibrutinib, Figure 8-2

L1847: ## Results and Discussion
L1848: ## Remibrutinib PBPK Model Performance for Pharmacokinetics
L1849: The developed PBPK model for remibrutinib effectively captured the PK profiles in healthy subjects under various administration scenarios, including steady-state intravenous dosing (0.1 mg following a 50-mg oral dose), single oral doses (1.5 to 200 mg), and multiple oral doses (10 to 100 mg once daily or 25 to 200 mg twice daily for 12 days) (Figure 61 and Table 93).
L1850: ## NDA 218436
## RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
L1851: Considering the high PK variability reported for remibrutinib, the predicted PK parameters demonstrated an adequate degree of accuracy, with 73% falling within 2-fold of the mean observed values and 50% within 0.7- to 1.4-fold. Additionally, the model accurately predicted the PK profile of remibrutinib at a dose of 25 mg twice daily in subjects with chronic spontaneous urticaria (Figure 62 and Table 93).
L1852: The predicted CL/F of remibrutinib at 100 mg twice daily was 264 L/h, which is comparable to the observed CLss/F of 255 L/h from Study X2101. The model-predicted volume of distribution for remibrutinib at 100 mg twice daily for 12 days was 562 L (calculated from CL =264 L/h and \lambda_{Z} =0.47 h-1). This value is lower than the observed Vss/F of 2040 L in Study X2101 but aligns closely with the observed volume of 576 L from the mass balance Study X2104 when remibrutinib was administered at 100 mg BID for 3 days. The estimated peripheral volume of distribution (V2) from the population pharmacokinetic modeling was 1180 L. Clinical study results indicate that the volume of distribution increases with higher doses of remibrutinib, for example, following the administration of once daily doses of remibrutinib (10 to 600 mg) for 12 days in Study X2101, the Vss/F increased from 554 L at 10-mg dose to 7130 L at 600-mg dose (see Section 14.2.1.1).

L1867: ## PBPK Model Performance for DDI
L1868: DDI simulations were conducted to evaluate the adequacy of the model to recover the clinical DDI data of remibrutinib with the strong CYP3A modulators ritonavir and carbamazepine, confirming the f_{mCY3A4} value (43%) for remibrutinib included in the PBPK model.
L1869: Simulations were conducted for remibrutinib both oral (20 mg) and intravenous (0.1 mg) administration with ritonavir, as observed in the Study X2103. The predicted geometric mean ratios of C_{max} and AUC_{last} for oral remibrutinib 20 mg, when coadministered with ritonavir, were 3.03 and 4.09, respectively. For intravenous remibrutinib, the predicted geometric mean ratios of C_{max} and AUC_{last} were 1.84 and 1.07, respectively. The predicted DDI between ritonavir and remibrutinib was found to be comparable to the observed PK ratios, with a predicted/observed ratio range of 0.91 to 1.21 (Figure 64 and Table 94).
L1870: Similarly, simulations were conducted for remibrutinib 100 mg BID (five oral doses) with carbamazepine, as observed in the Study A02103. The predicted geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC for remibrutinib, when coadministered with carbamazepine, were 0.369 and 0.312, respectively, which were slightly higher than the observed values of 0.26 and 0.23, respectively. The predicted/observed ratio were 1.42 for C_{max} and 1.36 for AUC (Figure 64 and Table 94).
L1871: Overall, the PBPK model of remibrutinib adequately predicted the magnitude of the DDI between remibrutinib 20 mg steady state and ritonavir observed in Studies X2103, and slightly overpredicted the induction effect of carbamazepine on remibrutinib 100 mg steady state observed in Study A02103.
L1872: ## PBPK Model Performance Using PD Model
L1873: The reviewer conducted independent simulations for remibrutinib 10 mg QD, 25 mg QD, and 200 mg QD to assess the impact of integrating the PD model into the PBPK model. The PD model, which accounts for TMDD, demonstrated a notable advantage in accurately describing the observed PK data at lower doses (e.g., 10 mg and 25 mg) compared to the PBPK model without the PD component (Figure 63). However, at a higher dose of 200 mg, the PD model did not provide additional benefits in describing the observed PK data, as the simulated PK profiles were comparable with and without the PD model implementation.
L1874: **Figure 63. Simulated Remibrutinib PK Profiles Following the Administration of Remibrutinib 10, 25, and 200 mg QD With and Without Implementing the PD Model**
L1875: Source: Reviewer-generated figures using remibrutinib model submitted by the Applicant.
Abbreviations: b.i.d., twice daily; hr, hour; PD, pharmacodynamic; PK, pharmacokinetic; QD, once daily
L1876: To further assess the impact of incorporating the PD model into the PBPK model for remibrutinib, the reviewer conducted DDI simulations to predict the influence of the PD model on key model applications, namely the DDI magnitude between remibrutinib administered at 25 mg BID and a moderate CYP3A inhibitor (erythromycin) or inducer (efavirenz). PBPK simulations that included the PD model predicted C_{max} and AUC ratios for the interaction between remibrutinib 25 mg BID and erythromycin 500 mg to be 1.9 (90% CI: 1.84, 1.97) and 2.27 (90% CI: 2.18, 2.36), respectively. The PBPK simulations without the PD model predicted C_{max} and AUC ratios for the same interaction to be 1.83 (90% CI: 1.78, 1.89) and 2.20 (90% CI: 2.12, 2.29), respectively. Similarly, PBPK simulations that included the PD model predicted C_{max} and AUC ratios for the interaction between remibrutinib 25 mg BID and efavirenz 600 mg to be 0.405 (90% CI: 0.384, 0.428) and 0.361 (90% CI: 0.34, -0.384), respectively. PBPK simulations without the PD model predicted C_{max} and AUC ratios for the same interaction to be 0.44 (90% CI: 0.42, 0.46) and 0.39 (90% CI: 0.37, 0.41), respectively. The DDI magnitudes between remibrutinib 25 mg BID and either a moderate CYP3A inhibitor or inducer, with and without the inclusion of the PD model, were found to be comparable. Therefore, it can be concluded that the PD model does not significantly impact the prediction of drug-interaction magnitudes.
L1877: ## PBPK Model Applications
L1878: Remibrutinib PBPK model was used to predict drug interactions for scenarios that were not tested clinically at the proposed therapeutic dose of 25 mg BID.
L1879: ## Effects of CYP3A Inhibitors and Inducers on Remibrutinib
L1880: The net effects of induction, reversible inhibition, time-dependent inhibition, and saturation of CYP3A4 are expected to collectively influence the elimination of remibrutinib. The model predicted a net effect of 10 to 20% reduction in both hepatic and intestinal clearance when the dose is increased from 25 mg BID to 100 mg. Parameter sensitivity analysis has demonstrated that escalating the dose of remibrutinib from 20 mg to 200 mg results in a decrease in the f_mCYP3A4. This decrease leads to a reduction in the magnitude of drug interactions with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors and an increase in the magnitude of drug interactions with strong CYP3A4 inducers (data not shown). Time-dependent inhibition and CYP3A4 saturation were found to contribute to the reduction of both hepatic and intestinal intrinsic clearance by CYP3A4, particularly at doses ranging from 25 mg to 100 mg BID (data not shown). Notably, CYP3A4 saturation is more prevalent in the gut due to higher drug concentrations in this region. Additionally, auto-induction in the gut is more pronounced for remibrutinib at a dose of 100 mg BID compared to 25 mg BID.
L1881: ## *Strong CYP3A Inhibitors*
L1882: DDI simulations were conducted to extrapolate the outcome of the clinical DDI effect of ritonavir with remibrutinib at 20 mg to the proposed therapeutic dose of 25 mg BID, using ritonavir, itraconazole, and ketoconazole as strong CYP3A inhibitors. Following coadministration of ritonavir (100 mg BID for 7 days), itraconazole (200 mg QD for 7 days) or ketoconazole (200 mg BID for 7 days), the predicted geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC_{tau} of remibrutinib 25 mg BID ranged from 2.47 to 2.67 and 3.21 to 3.65, respectively, in healthy subjects (Table 97). These data suggest that a strong CYP3A inhibitor is expected to cause a moderate inhibition effect on the exposure to remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
L1883: ## *Moderate CYP3A Inhibitors*
L1884: DDI simulations were conducted to predict the clinical DDI effect of the moderate CYP3A inhibitors fluconazole and erythromycin on remibrutinib 25 mg BID. The predicted geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC_{tau} of remibrutinib 25 mg BID following oral administration of erythromycin (500 mg four times a day for 7 days) in healthy subjects, were 1.90 and 2.27, respectively (Table 97). Similarly, the predicted geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC_{tau} of remibrutinib 25 mg BID following oral administration of fluconazole (200 mg QD for 7 days) in healthy subjects were 1.67 and 1.89, respectively (Table 97). These data suggest a moderate CYP3A inhibitor is expected to cause a weak to moderate effect on the exposure to remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
L1885: ## *Weak CYP3A4 Inhibitor*
L1886: Following coadministration of the weak CYP3A inhibitor fluvoxamine (50 mg QD for 7 days), the predicted geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC_{tau} of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were 1.04 and 1.04, respectively (Table 97), in healthy subjects. These data suggest that a weak CYP3A inhibitor is not expected to impact the exposure to remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
L1887: ## *Strong CYP3A Inducers*
L1888: DDI simulations were conducted to predict the clinical DDI effect of the strong CYP3A inducers carbamazepine and rifampicin on remibrutinib 25 mg BID. The predicted geometric mean ratios
L1889: for C_{max} and AUC_{tau} of remibrutinib 25 mg BID with carbamazepine (up to 300 mg BID for 22 days) were 0.358 and 0.257, respectively (Table 97). Similarly, the predicted geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC_{tau} of remibrutinib 25 mg BID following oral administration of rifampicin (600 mg QD for 7 days) in healthy subjects were 0.122 and 0.095, respectively (Table 97). These data suggest that a strong CYP3A inducer is expected to cause a strong effect (\ge 80\% decrease) on the exposure to remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
L1890: ## *Moderate CYP3A4 Inducer*
L1891: Following coadministration of the moderate CYP3A inducer efavirenz (600 mg QD for 7 days), the predicted geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC_{tau} of remibrutinib 25 mg BID were 0.405 and 0.361, respectively (Table 97), in healthy subjects. These data suggest that a moderate CYP3A inducer is expected to cause a moderate effect (50 to 80% decrease) on the exposure to remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
L1892: ## *Weak CYP3A4 Inducer*
L1893: Following coadministration of the weak CYP3A4 inducer dexamethasone (8 mg BID for 7 days), the predicted geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC_{tau} of remibrutinib 25 mg BID in healthy subjects were 0.672 and 0.626, respectively (Table 97). These data suggest that a weak CYP3A inducer is expected to cause a weak effect (20 to 50% decrease) on the exposure to remibrutinib 25 mg BID. Moreover, the model predicted no impact of remibrutinib 25 BID on the pharmacokinetics of dexamethasone, as a CYP3A4 substrate (Table 96).
L1894: ## Effect of Remibrutinib on P-gp Substrates (Digoxin)
L1895: Previously, the Applicant has used PBPK modeling and clinical DDI studies of different P-gp precipitants with digoxin aiming to establish a scaling factor (SF) for the in vitro P-gp inhibition constant (IC_{50}/K_i). Based on this analysis results, the Applicant proposed a scaling factor in the range of [REDACTED] for P-gp inhibition constant. The SF P-gp is implemented in the simulations by dividing the fraction unbound in incubation (f_{u,inc}) with the scaling factor as f_{u,inc,scaled} = f_{u,inc}/SF P-gp).
L1896: In remibrutinib PBPK modeling analysis, simulations were conducted to evaluate the ability of the model to recover the clinical DDI effect of remibrutinib 100 mg BID with digoxin (0.25-mg single oral dose) from Study A02103. While using SF P-gp of [REDACTED], the predicted geometric mean ratios of C_{max} and AUC for digoxin following coadministration of remibrutinib in healthy subjects were [REDACTED], respectively. However, the model underpredicted the observed C_{max} and AUC ratios of 2.08 and 1.45, respectively (Figure 64 and Table 95). The prediction errors of the interaction on both the geometric mean C_{max} and AUC ratios were [REDACTED]%. Subsequently, simulations were conducted using the higher SF P-gp of [REDACTED], which did not improve the underprediction of the clinical DDI magnitude (the predicted geometric mean C_{max} and AUC ratios were [REDACTED], respectively). In addition, the reviewer conducted simulations without using SF P-gp and the predicted C_{max} and AUC ratios for digoxin 0.25-mg single oral dose following oral administration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID were [REDACTED], respectively, with prediction errors of [REDACTED]% for C_{max} and [REDACTED]% for AUC.
L1897: DDI simulations were conducted to interpolate the outcome of the clinical DDI effect of remibrutinib 100 mg BID to 25 mg BID, using SF P-gp of [REDACTED]. The predicted geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC_{tau} of digoxin 0.25-mg single oral dose following oral administration of
L1898: remibrutinib 25 mg BID in healthy subjects, were [REDACTED](b) (4), respectively (Table 96). Since the PBPK simulations using remibrutinib 100 mg underpredicted the clinical DDI effect, the PBPK simulations of the interaction magnitude at 25 mg BID is possibly under predicted as well.
L1899: ## Effect of Remibrutinib on Midazolam (Sensitive CYP3A4 Substrate)
L1900: To simulate the results from Study X2102, the predicted geometric mean ratios of C_{max} and AUC for midazolam 2-mg single oral dose on Day 3 and Day 11 following oral administration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID in healthy subjects were 1.29 and 1.35 on Day 3 and 1.35 and 1.46 on Day 11, respectively. These predicted interaction magnitudes were comparable to the observed ratios in Study X2102 with a predicted/observed ratios range of 0.93 to 1.06 (Figure 64 and Table 95). The assessment of DDI between remibrutinib and midazolam on Day 3 and 11 takes in consideration the net effect of remibrutinib on CYP3A4 function which may exhibit a time-dependent characteristics. The predicted reversible inhibition effect is likely more pronounced at the initiation of treatment (Day 3), while the induction effect may be most evident after the cessation of treatment (Day 11).
L1901: The geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC_{\tau} of midazolam 2-mg single oral dose on Day 3 and Day 11 following oral administration of remibrutinib 25 mg BID in healthy subjects were predicted to be 1.18 and 1.20 on Day 3 and 1.22 and 1.25 on Day 11, respectively (Table 96). Based on the predicted effects of remibrutinib 25 mg BID on midazolam exposure on Days 3 and 11, it can be inferred that remibrutinib has a low potential to inhibit or induce CYP3A4.
L1902: ## Effect of Remibrutinib on Ethinylestradiol (Oral Contraceptive, CYP3A4 Substrate)
L1903: To simulate the results from Study X2102, the predicted geometric mean ratios of C_{max} and AUC for ethinylestradiol 0.03-mg single oral dose on Day 11 following oral administration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID in healthy subjects were 1.09 and 1.12, respectively. These predicted interaction magnitudes were comparable to the observed ratios in Study X2102 with a predicted/observed ratio of 0.852 for C_{max} and 0.966 for AUC (Figure 64 and Table 95).
L1904: The predicted geometric mean ratios for C_{max} and AUC_{\tau} of ethinylestradiol 0.03-mg single oral dose on Day 11 following oral administration of remibrutinib 25 mg BID in healthy subjects, were predicted to be 1.07 and 1.08, respectively (Table 96). Therefore, remibrutinib lacks the potential to induce or inhibit CYP3A4.
L1905: ## Effect of Remibrutinib on Rosuvastatin (OATP1B and BCRP Substrate)
L1906: The in vitro inhibition constants for OATP1B and BCRP were not directly applied in the PBPK model of remibrutinib. Instead, the Applicant aimed to establish scaling factors based on their analysis of available clinical drug interactions of various OATP1B/BCRP perpetrators with the biomarker coproporphyrin I and several statins, including rosuvastatin. Based on the analysis results, the Applicant proposed scaling factors of [REDACTED](b) (4) (SF OATP1B = [REDACTED](b) (4)) and [REDACTED](b) (4) (SF BCRP = [REDACTED](b) (4)) for OATP and BCRP, respectively. These scaling factors are implemented in the modeling analysis by dividing the unbound fraction in the inlet (f_{u,inc}) of the precipitant by the respective scaling factor (e.g., f_{u,inc,scaled} = f_{u,inc} / SF).
L1907: In the simulations of remibrutinib, the scaling factor for OATP1B was not utilized because remibrutinib 100 mg BID did not affect the exposure to coproporphyrin I (an endogenous
L1908: OATP1B substrate) in Study A02103 (the observed geometric mean C_{\text{max}} and AUC ratios were 1.02 and 0.98, respectively).
L1909: DDI simulations were conducted to evaluate the model performance to recover the clinical DDI effect of remibrutinib 100 mg with rosuvastatin from Study A02103. Using a SF of (b)^{(4)} or OATP1B and an SF of (b)^{(4)} for BCRP, the geometric mean ratios of C_{\text{max}} and AUC for rosuvastatin 10-mg single oral dose, following the oral administration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID, were predicted to be (b)^{(4)}, respectively. In the clinical setting, the observed geometric mean ratios of C_{\text{max}} and AUC for rosuvastatin 10-mg single oral dose, following the oral administration of remibrutinib 100 mg BID, in healthy subjects were 1.56 and 1.65, respectively. Consequently, the model prediction errors for this drug interaction were (b)^{(4)}% and (b)^{(4)}% for the C_{\text{max}} and AUC ratios, respectively (Figure 64 and Table 95).
L1910: DDI simulations were conducted to interpolate the outcome of the clinical DDI effect of remibrutinib 100 mg to 25 mg BID. The predicted geometric mean ratios for C_{\text{max}} and \text{AUC}_{\text{tau}} of rosuvastatin 10-mg single oral dose following the oral administration of remibrutinib 25 mg BID, while using the same scaling factors (SF OATP1B of (b)^{(4)} and SF BCRP of (b)^{(4)}, were (b)^{(4)}, respectively (Table 96). Since the simulations for remibrutinib 100 mg tended to mis-predict the clinical effect on rosuvastatin, simulation results of the interaction magnitude at 25 mg BID may possibly be mis-predicted as well.

Pharmacokinetic parameters as well as the drug-drug interaction magnitude (AUC and Cmax ratio) are presented as geometric means with 90% confident interval (CI) range in parentheses for the first dose of remibrutinib
L1957: Abbreviations: AUCtau, area under the concentration-time curve over the dosing interval; BID, twice daily; Cmax,ss, maximum blood concentration at steady state; QID, four time a day; QD, once daily
L1958: ## Conclusion
L1959: The pharmacokinetics of remibrutinib are significantly influenced by drugs that modulate CYP3A activity. PBPK simulation studies have predicted clinically significant changes in remibrutinib's C_{max,ss} and AUC_{tau} when coadministered with strong or moderate inhibitors or inducers of CYP3A, which agreed with the observed clinical DDI results of remibrutinib with strong CYP3A modulators.
L1960: When remibrutinib 25 mg BID is coadministered with multiple doses of strong CYP3A inhibitors such as ritonavir, itraconazole, or ketoconazole, the C_{max,ss} and AUC_{tau} are predicted to increase by approximately 2.47- to 2.67-fold and 3.21- to 3.65-fold, respectively. Similarly, moderate CYP3A inhibitors like erythromycin and fluconazole are predicted to elevate the C_{max,ss} and AUC_{tau} by around 1.67- to 1.9-fold and 1.89- to 2.27-fold, respectively. In contrast, weak CYP3A4 inhibitors such as fluvoxamine do not significantly affect the PK parameters of remibrutinib 25 mg BID.
L1961: Conversely, strong CYP3A inducers like carbamazepine and rifampicin can markedly decrease the C_{max,ss} and AUC_{tau} of remibrutinib by approximately 62.4% to 87.8% and 74.3% to 90.5%, respectively. Moderate CYP3A inducers such as efavirenz reduce these PK parameters by about 59.5% and 64%, respectively.
L1962: In clinical Study A02103, remibrutinib 100 mg BID increased the C_{max} and AUC of the P-gp substrate digoxin by 2.08-fold and 1.45-fold, respectively, and the C_{max} and AUC of the BCRP substrate rosuvastatin by 1.56-fold and 1.65-fold, respectively. PBPK simulations tended to mispredict the observed magnitude of the interaction between remibrutinib 100 mg BID and these transporter substrates. Therefore, due to the inherent limitations of model predictions and confidence in the predicted results, the clinical findings from Study A02103, conducted with the 100-mg BID dosage of remibrutinib, should be applicable to subjects receiving the lower dosage of 25 mg BID.
L1963: PBPK simulations and clinical DDI studies have demonstrated the absence of an impact of remibrutinib coadministration on the exposure to CYP3A4 substrates, such as midazolam and oral contraceptives.
L1964: ## 14.6. Pharmacogenetics
L1965: Not applicable.
