L2064: ## **15.1.4. Statistical Analysis Plan, Trials A2301 and A2302**
L2065: Additional details regarding the statistical analysis plan for the individual trials and prespecified pooling of data from the two trials are provided below.
L2066: ## 15.1.4.1. Sample Size Determination, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2067: Based on the primary endpoints analysis of phase 2b Study CLOU064A2201, the Applicant assumed a common standard deviation of 6 and treatment difference between remibrutinib and placebo of 4 in the mean change from baseline of ISS7 at Week 12. A t-test with type I error of one-sided 0.025 had more than 99% power to detect a treatment difference of 4 with a sample size of 270 subjects in the remibrutinib arm and 135 subjects in the placebo arm. Assuming approximately 10% dropout by Week 12, a sample size of 300 subjects in the remibrutinib arm and 150 subjects in the placebo arm was targeted.
L2068: ## 15.1.4.2. Supplementary Analysis at Week 24, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2069: An analysis of the change from baseline in HSS7 and ISS7 at Week 24 was conducted by the Applicant as an exploratory analysis. The analysis used the same methods as the primary analysis at Week 12, with intercurrent events handled similarly. However, the intercurrent event of intake of strongly confounding prohibited medication was defined as biologics treatment at any time before Week 24, cyclosporin after Week 8 to Week 24, or systemic corticosteroids after Week 8 to Week 12. As with the analyses at Week 12, the treatment policy strategy was recommended to the Applicant during the planning and is of primary regulatory interest. The results presented in this review correspond to the Applicant's prespecified intercurrent event handling strategy and comparisons of the analyses of the two estimands are discussed where relevant. Further, oral corticosteroids were allowed as an additional rescue therapy for CSU only after Week 12 on an as-needed basis for unbearable symptoms.
L2070: ## 15.1.4.3. Sensitivity and Supplementary Analyses, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2071: A prespecified supplementary analysis varied the intercurrent event handling strategies of the primary estimand. The change from baseline in ISS7 and HSS7 score up to Week 12 was replaced with zero (i.e., no clinical improvement from baseline) for the intercurrent event of "Intake of strongly confounding prohibited medication (e.g., biologics treatment at any time before Week 12, cyclosporin after Week 8 to Week 12, systemic corticosteroids after Week 8 to Week 12)" handled with composite strategy.
L2072: Subjects who did not meet the inclusion criteria of baseline ISS7 \ge 6 and HSS7 \ge 6 were noted by the review team. A sensitivity analysis was performed for the coprimary endpoints which excluded such subjects and methods were the same as for the primary analysis.
L2073: At the IND stage, the Agency recommended that the Applicant conduct sensitivity analyses to investigate the robustness of the assumption in the primary analysis that data were MAR. Although no such analysis was prespecified by the Applicant prior to submission, the Agency requested a tipping-point analysis be performed in line with the prespecified Agency recommendation. Assumptions were varied on the missing outcomes handled through treatment policy strategy for discontinuations of study treatment prior to Week 12 due to any reasons and
L2074: the missing outcomes handled under jump-to-reference or MAR assumption. The steps were as follows.
L2075: - Step 1: As per the primary analysis, all missing ISS7/HSS7 scores at Week 12 were imputed following the same imputation method specified for primary estimand.
- Step 2: In each imputed dataset i (i = 1, \dots, 100), in the remibrutinib arm, positive shift \delta_j was added to the imputed missing scores and in the placebo arm, negative shift \delta_k was added to the imputed missing scores. This imputed a worse outcome in the remibrutinib arm and a better outcome in the placebo arm. This was done so that the resulting values cannot exceed possible range of variables (minimum 0 and maximum 21). Observed data and the worst scores used under the composite strategy remained. Then the same MMRM as for primary analysis was fitted.
- Step 3: The shift values \delta_j and \delta_k were varied over a plausible range for both arms. For ISS7 and HSS7, \delta_j varied between 0 to 21 (0, 5, 10, 15, 21) for the remibrutinib arm and \delta_k varied between 0 to -21 (0, -5, -10, -15, -21) for the placebo arm.
L2076: ## 15.1.4.4. Subgroup Analyses, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2077: Subgroup analyses performed within study using an MMRM with the same covariates as the primary analysis, as well the subgroup and interaction of subgroup with treatment.
L2078: ## 15.1.4.5. Secondary Analyses, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2079: For the secondary endpoints of proportion of subjects with UAS7 \le6 at Week 2, proportion of subjects with UAS7 \le6 at Week 12, and proportion of subjects with UAS7 =0 at Week 12, the prescribing information presents the risk difference and its 95% confidence interval, rather than the conditional odds ratio as specified in the primary analyses of these endpoints. The Applicant specified the methodology for calculating the risk difference and its confidence interval as follows.
L2080: - A logistic model was fit with maximum likelihood that regresses the outcome on treatment assignments and prespecified baseline covariates, including an intercept term.
- For each subject, the model-based prediction of the probability of response under treatment was computed in both the treatment group and placebo group using each subject's specific baseline covariates.
- The average response under treatment was estimated by averaging (across all subjects in the trial) the probabilities estimated in Step 2.
- For each subject, the model-based prediction of the probability of response under placebo was computed in both the treatment group and placebo group using each subject's specific baseline covariates.
- The average response under placebo was estimated by averaging (across all subjects in the trial) the probabilities estimated in Step 4.
- The estimates of average responses rates in the two treatment groups from Steps 3 and 5 were used to estimate the risk difference.
L2081: The delta method was applied to calculate the standard error of the risk difference. The steps above were done for each imputed data set separately and the results were combined using Rubin's rules, leading to a pooled estimate of the marginal difference and of the standard error. Based on these estimates, a 95% confidence interval for the marginal difference was calculated.
L2082: ## 15.1.4.6. Pooled Subgroup Analyses, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2083: Pooled analyses were prespecified by combining data from Trials A2301 and A2302. For the coprimary endpoints, LS means for within-treatment and treatment-comparison (within subgroups) were evaluated using an MMRM including treatment group, baseline score (respectively HSS7/ISS7) score, study, geographical region, previous exposure to anti-IgE biologics for CSU, week, the interaction of treatment by week, and the interaction of baseline score (respectively HSS7/ISS7) by week as fixed effects, in addition to the respective subgroup variable and interaction between the respective subgroup, treatment and week, with estimand related multiple imputation data.
L2084: For the cases where the computation of the MMRM took extreme amount of time, the ddfm = bw option was considered in the corresponding SAS procedure. When the MMRM with an unstructured covariance matrix did not converge, the compound-symmetry structure was used. When this also failed, the analysis of covariance model was used including treatment group, baseline score (respectively HSS7/ISS7) score, study, geographical region, previous exposure to anti-IgE biologics for CSU, the respective subgroup variable, and interaction of treatment with the respective subgroup variable.
L2085: Rubin's rule was applied to combine the multiple imputed datasets of estimates to produce the overall estimates, standard errors, and confidence intervals. If no data were missing, there was no imputation; the imputed datasets were all identical, and calculations were based on the first imputed dataset only.
L2086: The following subgroups were prespecified:
L2087: - Randomization strata:Previous exposure to anti-IgE biologics (yes/no)
- Geographic region (Latin America, The Caribbean, and Canada; Region Europe; Japan; United States; Asia, Middle East and Africa)
- Age (18-&lt;65, 65-&lt;85, 85+)
- Sex (male, female)
- Race (White, Black or African American, Asian, American Indian or Alaska Native, Other)
- Ethnicity (Hispanic or Latino, Non-Hispanic or Latino)
- Baseline UAS7 score (16-&lt;28 moderate, 28+ severe)
- Previous experience of angioedema (defined based on prior urticaria history with angioedema occurrence; yes/no)
- Baseline chronic urticaria index status (10+ positive, &lt;10 negative)
L2088: - Duration of CSU defined as time since diagnosis of urticaria (defined as informed consent date—diagnosis date +1; \le 1 year, 1 \le 3 years, 3 \le 5 years, &gt; 5 years)
- Baseline total IgE level (\le 43 IU/mL low, &gt; 43 IU/mL normal/high)
- Prior use of second-generation antihistamines
- Diagnosis of concomitant chronic inducible urticaria (yes/no)
L2089: As a post hoc exploratory analysis, the review team investigated a subgroup of subjects with previous exposure to omalizumab with a treatment duration of at least 3 months and discontinued for reasons documented as lack of efficacy, adverse event, or physician decision. Results were generated for the coprimary endpoints following the same methods as the Applicant's prespecified subgroup analyses.
L2090: ## 15.2. Study A2201
L2091: ## 15.2.1. Study Design, Study A2201
L2092: The primary objective was to characterize the remibrutinib dose-response on the presence of hives and itch. Study A2201 was a phase 2 multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, dose-finding study in adults with CSU inadequately controlled by H1-antihistamines (see Sections 6.2 and 14.2.7 for additional details on design and conduct). The study employed a 1:1:1:1:1:1:1 randomization scheme across seven treatment arms, including six different remibrutinib dosing regimens and placebo. Conducted across 82 centers in 17 countries from June 2019 to April 2021, the study enrolled 311 subjects who underwent a 12-week treatment period followed by a 4-week treatment-free follow-up period.
L2093: ## 15.2.2. Eligibility Criteria, Study A2201
L2094: The study population consisted of male and female adults aged \ge 18 years with a confirmed CSU diagnosis for \ge 6 months prior to screening and disease inadequately controlled by second-generation H1-antihistamines, as evidenced by the presence of itch and hives for \ge 6 consecutive weeks despite appropriate antihistamine treatment according to local guidelines. Key inclusion criteria required subjects to have a UAS7 score \ge 16 and HSS7 score \ge 8 during the 7 days prior to randomization, with documented presence of hives within 3 months before randomization. The study excluded subjects with chronic inducible urticaria (having clearly defined triggers), other diseases with urticaria or angioedema symptoms (such as urticaria vasculitis or mastocytosis), other chronic itching skin diseases that could confound study evaluations, significant ECG abnormalities, bleeding disorders, or ongoing chronic/recurrent infectious diseases.
L2095: ## 15.2.3. Study Treatment and Concomitant Medications, Study A2201
L2096: The investigational medical product was formulated as hard gelatin capsules for oral administration in three dosage strengths: 10 mg, 25 mg, and 50 mg. Placebo was provided as matching hard gelatin capsules. Subjects were randomized equally to one of the following treatment groups:
L2097: - Remibrutinib 10 mg once daily
- Remibrutinib 35 mg once daily
- Remibrutinib 100 mg once daily
- Remibrutinib 10 mg twice daily
- Remibrutinib 25 mg twice daily
- Remibrutinib 100 mg twice daily
- Placebo
L2098: ## Permitted Therapies
L2099: Second-generation H1-antihistamines with renal elimination pathways were permitted for subjects experiencing flare-ups of unbearable disease symptoms.
L2100: ## Prohibited Therapies
L2101: - CSU-specific biologics
- Systemic corticosteroids
- Leukotriene antagonists
- Antihistamines (except defined background and rescue medications)
L2102: ## 15.2.4. Study Endpoints, Study A2201
L2103: All subjects were provided with an eDiary that contained the UPDD, AAS, and DLQI. The UAS7 was recorded twice daily, while the AAS was recorded once daily. All PRO instruments were the same as those used in the phase 3 trials (Section 15.1.3).
L2104: ## Safety Assessments
L2105: The primary safety variables were AE, SAEs, study discontinuations, laboratory test results, vital signs, EKG, and physical exams.
L2106: ## **Primary Efficacy Endpoint**
L2107: - Change from baseline in UAS7 at Week 4
L2108: ## **Secondary Efficacy Endpoints**
L2109: - Change from baseline in UAS7 at Week 12
- Change from baseline in UAS7 throughout the study period
- Complete clinical response (UAS7 =0) throughout the study period
- Disease control (UAS7 \le6)
- Cumulative number of weeks with an AAS7 =0 response (absence of angioedema) from baseline through Week 12
- Achievement of DLQI score of 0 or 1 at Week 4 and Week 12, and change from baseline in DLQI scores at these time points
- Concentrations of remibrutinib in blood and calculation of respective PK parameters at Week 4 and Week 12
L2110: ## **15.3. Study A2201E1**
L2111: ## **15.3.1. Study Design, Study A2201E1**
L2112: The primary objective of Study A2201E1 was to assess the long-term safety and tolerability of remibrutinib in subjects with CSU who had participated in Study A2201. Study A2201E1 was an open-label, single-arm, multicenter, long-term safety, and tolerability extension study designed to evaluate the extended use of remibrutinib in subjects with CSU who had previously participated in the core Study A2201. The study was conducted across 72 sites in 15 countries and enrolled 230 subjects between October 2019 and September 2022.
L2113: The study consisted of three distinct periods. First, an observational period where subjects rolling over from the core study with controlled disease (defined as UAS7 &lt;16) were monitored without receiving remibrutinib for up to 12 weeks to assess disease stability. If subjects experienced a relapse (defined as UAS7 \ge16), they would transition to the treatment period. Second, a treatment period where subjects with UAS7 \ge16 either from the core study rollover or from relapse during the observational period received open-label remibrutinib 100 mg twice daily for 52 weeks. Notably, background H1-antihistamine therapy was prohibited for the first 4 weeks of treatment to assess the drug's efficacy as monotherapy. Finally, a treatment-free follow-up period provided post-treatment monitoring for a minimum of 4 weeks, with extended follow-up up to 16 weeks for subjects who achieved disease control (defined as UAS7 \le6) at the end of treatment to monitor for relapse.
L2114: The study allocation was based on disease activity scores at specific timepoints from the preceding core study, with subjects having UAS7 \ge16 at Week 12 or 16 entering the treatment period directly, while those with UAS7 &lt;16 at Week 16 entering the observational period first. This design allowed for evaluation of both the long-term safety profile and the durability of treatment response in subjects with CSU as depicted in Figure 67.
L2115: Figure 67. Study Design Schema, Study A2201E1
L2116: Source: Applicant provided CSR for CLOU0642201A1, p. 25
L2117: Abbreviation: bid, twice daily; LOU064, remibrutinib; UAS7, weekly urticaria activity score
L2118: ## 15.3.2. Eligibility Criteria, Study A2201E1
L2119: Key inclusion criteria consisted of male and female adult subjects with CSU who had received various doses of remibrutinib or placebo in the core study (Study A2201) and who had completed either the Week 12 visit (end of treatment period) OR the Week 16 visit (end of follow-up period) of Study A2201.
L2120: ## 15.3.3. Study Treatment and Concomitant Medications, Study A2201E1
L2121: IMP consisted of open-label 100 mg remibrutinib twice daily. Permitted and prohibited concomitant medications were the same as in Study A2201 (see Section 15.3.3).
L2122: ## 15.3.4. Study Variables and Assessments, Study A2201E1
L2123: The primary study variables focused on safety parameters, specifically the occurrence of TEAEs during the extension study period. Although efficacy assessments included various outcomes based on UAS7 (e.g., change from baseline, proportion achieving UAS7\le6 and UAS7 =0), these results are not presented or discussed in this review because the interpretability of uncontrolled, unblinded efficacy results for a PRO in a condition with relatively high spontaneous remission rate is extremely limited.
L2124: ## 15.4. Study A1301
L2125: ## 15.4.1. Study Design, Study A1301
L2126: The primary objective was to evaluate safety through assessment of TEAEs during the 52-week treatment period. Study A1301 was a multicenter, open-label phase 3 clinical study conducted across 13 centers in Japan from January 2022 to December 2023. The study investigated the safety, tolerability, and efficacy of remibrutinib at a dose of 25 mg twice daily in 71 adult Japanese subjects with CSU who remained inadequately controlled despite treatment with H1-antihistamines at locally approved doses.
L2127: The study design consisted of three periods totaling up to 60 weeks: a screening period of up to 4 weeks, an open-label treatment period of 52 weeks with remibrutinib, and a treatment-free follow-up period of 4 weeks.
L2128: Figure 68. Study Design Schema, Study A1301
L2129: Source: Applicant provided CSR for Study A1301, p. 28
Abbreviations: b.i.d., twice daily; Scr, Screening; W, Week
L2130: ## 15.4.2. Eligibility Criteria, Study A1301
L2131: Eligible subjects were adults aged 18 years or older with CSU for at least 6 months prior to screening. Key eligibility criteria included inadequately controlled disease despite treatment with second-generation H1-antihistamines at locally approved doses defined as having persistent itch and hives for at least 6 consecutive weeks prior to screening despite ongoing antihistamine therapy, a UAS7 score of at least 16, an ISS7 score of at least 6, and an HSS7 score of at least 6. Additionally, subjects were required to have documented evidence of hives within 3 months before baseline and be on a stable, locally approved dose of second-generation H1-antihistamines at the time of study entry.
L2132: ## 15.4.3. Study Treatment and Concomitant Medications, Study A1301
L2133: The IMP consisted of remibrutinib 25-mg film-coated tablets. Subjects were instructed to administer one tablet in the morning and one tablet in the evening.
L2134: NDA 218436
RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
L2135: ## **Permitted Medications**
L2136: - All subjects were required to remain on a stable, locally approved dose of a second-generation H1-antihistamine throughout the entire study duration.
- Oral corticosteroids were allowed as rescue therapy after Week 12.
L2137: ## **Prohibited Medications**
L2138: - Biologics for CSU treatment, including omalizumab and ligelizumab.
- Routine oral corticosteroids (defined as more than three doses over 5 days).
- Injectable corticosteroids.
- Leukotriene antagonists.
- H2-antihistamines, first-generation antihistamines, alternative second-generation antihistamines beyond the designated background medication.
- Immunosuppressive or immunomodulating agents.
- Intravenous immunoglobulins or plasmapheresis.
- Ultraviolet therapy, and any other urticaria-specific treatments including herbal therapies.
- Live attenuated vaccines.
- Strong inhibitors of CYP3A4, moderate and strong inducers of CYP3A4.
- Anticoagulant medications, and antiplatelet medications with the exception of low-dose acetylsalicylic acid (up to 100 mg daily) or clopidogrel (up to 75 mg daily) as monotherapy; dual antiplatelet therapy combinations were not allowed.
L2139: ## **15.4.4. Study Variables and Assessments, Study A1301**
L2140: ## **Safety Assessments**
L2141: The primary safety variables were TEAEs during the 52-week treatment period including number and proportion of subjects with at least one TEAE. Although efficacy assessments included various outcomes based on UAS7 (e.g., change from baseline, proportion achieving UAS7 \le6 and UAS7 =0), AAS7 (cumulative weeks with AAS7 =0), and DLQI (proportion with DLQI =0 to 1), these results are not presented or discussed in this review because the interpretability of uncontrolled, unblinded efficacy results for a PRO in a condition with relatively high spontaneous remission rate is extremely limited.
L2142: ## 15.5. Study A2305
L2143: ## 15.5.1. Study Design, Study A2305
L2144: The primary objective focused on cardiovascular safety assessed by ABPM. Study A2305 was a global, multicenter, open-label phase 3 study evaluating remibrutinib 25 mg twice daily in adult subjects with CSU inadequately controlled by second-generation H1-antihistamines. The study was conducted across 45 centers in 10 countries including Argentina, Canada, France, Germany, South Korea, Singapore, Slovakia, Spain, Turkey, and the United States.
L2145: The study design consisted of three distinct periods: a screening period of up to 4 weeks, a 12-week open-label treatment period where all subjects received remibrutinib 25-mg film-coated tablets administered orally twice daily, and a treatment-free follow-up period of 4 weeks, resulting in a total study duration of up to 20 weeks. The planned sample size was approximately 136 subjects, with 144 subjects ultimately completing the screening and enrollment process.
L2146: The primary outcome evaluated whether the 24-hour weighted average systolic blood pressure as measured by ABPM increased by more than 3 mm Hg at steady state (Week 4) compared to baseline. Secondary outcomes included change in both systolic and diastolic blood pressure during daytime and nighttime periods, as well as overall safety and tolerability. Exploratory outcomes encompassed various efficacy measures related to urticaria disease activity, including changes in urticaria activity scores, quality of life assessments, and pharmacokinetic parameters. Refer to the cardiac safety integrated review team consult memo dated June 13, 2025 (DARRTS Reference ID 5608166) for additional details on the design and results.
L2147: ## 15.6. Study F12101
L2148: ## 15.6.1. Study Design, Study F12101
L2149: The primary objective was to evaluate if immune responses following influenza and PPV-23 vaccinations in healthy subjects with interrupted remibrutinib treatment are noninferior to placebo. Study F12101 was a phase 1, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group study that evaluated the effect of remibrutinib 100 mg twice daily on immune responses to three different types of vaccines in healthy subjects aged 18 to 55 years. Approximately 90 subjects were randomized 1:1:1 into three treatment arms over a 43-day treatment period: Arm A received concomitant remibrutinib treatment (placebo for Days 1 to 7, then remibrutinib Days 8 to 42), Arm B received interrupted remibrutinib treatment (remibrutinib Days 1 to 7, placebo Days 8 to 28, then remibrutinib Days 29 to 42), and Arm C received placebo throughout Days 1 to 42. All subjects received three vaccines simultaneously on Day 15: quadrivalent seasonal influenza vaccine (intramuscular, dominant arm), PPV-23 pneumococcal vaccine (intramuscular, nondominant arm), and KLH neoantigen vaccine (subcutaneous, nondominant lower arm). The vaccines were administered 3 hours after study drug dosing, with subjects domiciled at the clinical site from Days -1 to 1 and Days 14 to 17. The study included a 28-day screening period, followed by the 43-day treatment period, an end-of-study visit on Day 57, and a safety follow-up call on Day 72, for a total study duration of approximately 85 days per subject. The intent of this design was to determine whether temporarily interrupting remibrutinib treatment around
L2150: vaccination could preserve immune responses compared to continuous treatment or placebo control. The primary endpoints were the proportion of 'responders' to T-cell-dependent influenza quadrivalent vaccine and T-cell-independent PPV-23 vaccine in the interrupted remibrutinib group compared to placebo. Response was defined as \ge 4-fold increase in hemagglutinin antibody titers (or \ge 1:40 if baseline &lt;1:10) for influenza, and \ge 2-fold increase in IgG titers for at least 50% of PPV-23 serotypes (\ge 12 out of 23) at 28 days postvaccination. Noninferiority to placebo was tested applying a noninferiority margin of 0.30. The primary analysis was performed on the PD analysis set, which included all randomized subjects who had no relevant protocol deviations and had completed the planned study treatment. Secondary endpoints assessed immune responses with concomitant remibrutinib treatment, KLH vaccine responses with interrupted treatment, and antibody titers/concentrations in the concomitant and interrupted remibrutinib groups relative to placebo over time. For additional details regarding the design of Study F12101, refer to the CBER consult memo (Inter-Center Consult #: 01066955) dated June 2, 2025, in DARRTS (Reference ID 5601342).
L2151: ## 16. Efficacy
L2152: Results of supplementary analyses performed on data from the individual pivotal trials as well as pooled data from the pivotal trials are presented in the sections below.
L2153: ## 16.1. Trials A2301 and A2302
L2154: ## 16.1.1. Results of Analyses at Week 24, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2155: Table 100 and Table 101 summarize the results for Trials A2301 and A2302 for efficacy endpoints of change from baseline to Week 24 in HSS7 and ISS7, respectively. After adjusting for baseline HSS7 score, geographical region, prior exposure to anti-IgE biologics, visit week, treatment by visit interaction, and baseline HSS7 score by visit interaction, the estimated least-squares mean change from baseline in HSS7 score at Week 24 was greater in magnitude in the remibrutinib group (LS means = -10.88 [SE 0.40] and -10.92 [SE 0.41]) compared to placebo (LS means = -7.97 [SE 0.55] and -7.31 [SE 0.56]) in Trials A2301 and A2302, respectively. For HSS7, the estimated difference in LS means relative to placebo was -2.91 (95% CI: -4.16, -1.66) and -3.61 (95% CI: -4.92, -2.29) in Trials A2301 and A2302, respectively. Similarly, after adjusting for similar covariates, the estimated least-squares mean change from baseline in ISS7 score at Week 24 was greater in magnitude in the remibrutinib group (LS means = -9.81 [SE 0.34] and -9.49 [SE 0.35]) compared to placebo (LS means = -8.00 [SE 0.47] and -6.46 [SE 0.48]) in Trials A2301 and A2302, respectively. For ISS7, the estimated difference in LS means relative to placebo was -1.81 (95% CI: -2.88, -0.73) and -3.03 (95% CI: -4.16, -1.90) in Trials A2301 and A2302, respectively. Both endpoints were nominally significant in both trials.
L2156: Up to Week 24, one subject in the remibrutinib 25-mg group experienced the intercurrent event of intake of strongly confounding medication in Trial A2301. In Trial A2302, one subject in the remibrutinib 25-mg group and two subjects in the placebo group experienced the intercurrent event of intake of strongly confounding medication. Results of analyses which correspond to the
L2157: NDA 218436
L2158: RHAPSIDO (remibrutinib, LOU064)
L2159: estimand with treatment policy strategy for all intercurrent events were substantially similar, with p-value&lt;0.001 and small differences in point estimates and confidence intervals which do not impact the interpretation of the results.

L2180: ## 16.1.3. Results of Sensitivity and Supplementary Analyses, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2181: Results for the prespecified supplementary analysis which varied the intercurrent event handling strategies of the primary estimand were similar to and supportive of the primary analysis.
L2182: One subject in the placebo group did not meet inclusion criteria of baseline ISS7 \ge6 and HSS7 \ge6 in Trial A2301. Three subjects in the FAS (two remibrutinib, one placebo) did not meet this inclusion criteria in Trial A2302. Results from the sensitivity analyses which excluded these subjects were consistent with the primary analysis.
L2183: In both Trials A2301 and A2302, the tipping-point analysis for HSS7 did not identify a tipping point across the explored plausible delta values, supporting the robustness of the primary analysis to violations of its missing data assumptions. Tipping points were identified for ISS7 for both trials, as shown in Table 104 and Table 105. The assumptions at which one-sided p-value &gt;0.025 vary from the most extreme assumptions (all subjects with missing data in the placebo group experiencing the best possible score and simultaneously all subjects with missing data in the remibrutinib group experiencing the worst possible score), to somewhat less extreme assumptions. These analyses reasonably support the robustness of the primary analysis with respect to missing data assumptions in both trials.

L2198: ## 16.1.4. Results of Subgroup Analyses, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2199: Subgroup analyses within Trial A2301 and A2302 were generally consistent with the primary analyses results, as well as the results of the pooled analyses. The results of the pooled subgroup analyses provide more precise estimates than estimates within each trial, and pooled results are presented in Section 16.2.1.
L2200: ## 16.2. Pooled Analyses, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2201: ## 16.2.1. Results of Subgroup Analyses, Pooled Analyses, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2202: Results for subgroups for HSS7 (respectively, ISS7) are summarized in Figure 69 and Figure 71 for demographic characteristics and in Figure 70 and Figure 72 for baseline disease characteristics. L2267: Although the incidence of common TEAEs generally appears similar across demographic subgroups, there are some imbalances, such as an apparent increased risk for bleeding-related TEAEs in females (versus males), geriatric subjects \ge 65 or 75 years of age (versus &lt; 65 or 75 years), and subjects of non-White race or Non-Hispanic ethnicity (versus Asian/Black/Multiple or Hispanic ethnicity) who were treated with remibrutinib. Some of the differences are likely artifacts caused by the small number of subjects within particular subgroups; however, the reason for an imbalance between females and males is unclear.
L2268: ## 17.2. Open-Label Period, Trials A2301 and A2302
L2269: The open-label (OL) periods for Trials A2301 and A2302 provided an additional 28 weeks of exposure to subjects who had participated in the double-blind period (see Section 7.5 for additional exposure details).
L2270: During the OL period of Trial A2301, 43.7% of subjects in the remibrutinib-to-remibrutinib group and 46.6% of subjects in the placebo-to-remibrutinib group experienced at least one TEAE. The majority of TEAEs were mild-to-moderate in severity. SAEs occurred in 1.5% and 0.8% of subjects in the respective groups during the OL period.
L2271: The overall TEAE incidence in the OL period of Trial A2302 was 52.9% in the remibrutinib-to-remibrutinib group and 55.0% in the placebo-to-remibrutinib group. The most frequently reported TEAEs (\ge 2\% frequency) included COVID-19 (7.3% and 10.1%, respectively), upper respiratory tract infection (5.0% and 6.2%), nasopharyngitis (3.9% and 2.3%), and suspected
L2272: COVID-19 (2.7% and 3.1%). These findings are consistent with common respiratory infections and may reflect the study conduct period during the COVID-19 pandemic and do not raise any new or unexpected safety signals.
L2273: ## **Bleeding**
L2274: During the OL period of Trials A2301 and A2302, the bleeding risk profile demonstrated specific patterns when analyzed through EAOR. In the remibrutinib group during the entire study period, 11.7% of subjects experienced bleeding AESIs compared to 10.6% during the placebo-controlled period. The EAOR analysis showed a decrease from 43.4 events per 100 patient-years during the placebo-controlled period to 25.4 events per 100 patient-years during the entire study period. For subjects who transitioned from placebo to remibrutinib during the OL phase, 6.9% experienced bleeding AESIs. The median time-to-event onset was 47.5 days with a median event duration of 43.0 days. All events in this population were mild or moderate in severity, with no severe or serious bleeding events reported. The temporal analysis revealed that bleeding AESIs occurred predominantly within the first 4 to 12 weeks of treatment initiation. During the OL extension period, no additional serious bleeding AESIs were reported beyond those occurring in the placebo-controlled phase. The types of bleeding events remained consistent throughout the study duration, consisting primarily of cutaneous bleeding manifestations including petechiae, contusion, and ecchymosis. Most bleeding events resolved during the study period, and most did not require treatment modifications. See Section 7.7.1 for details on the bleeding risk during the placebo-controlled period.
L2275: ## **Infection**
L2276: During the entire study period, 45.7% of subjects in the remibrutinib 25-mg BID group experienced infection AESIs, with the EAOR decreasing to 82.7 events per 100 patient-years compared to the placebo-controlled period, suggesting no increase in infection risk with longer treatment duration. For subjects who transitioned from placebo to remibrutinib, 26.3% experienced infection AESIs with an EAOR of 66.9 per 100 patient-years. The most frequently reported infections were respiratory tract infections, including COVID-19 (10.7% versus 11.4% in remibrutinib versus placebo groups), nasopharyngitis (6.6% versus 4.6%), and upper respiratory tract infections, similar to the placebo-controlled period. No opportunistic infections were reported using the standardized MedDRA query, and bacterial encapsulated infections were not observed. See Section 7.7.2 for details on the infection risk during the placebo-controlled period.
L2277: ## **Cytopenia**
L2278: During the entire study period, 5.9% of subjects in the remibrutinib 25-mg BID group experienced cytopenia AESIs, with the EAOR decreasing from 12.1 per 100 patient-years during the placebo-controlled period to 8.9 per 100 patient-years during the entire study period, suggesting no increase in cytopenia risk with longer treatment duration. For subjects who transitioned from placebo to remibrutinib, 1.5% experienced cytopenia AESIs and the majority did not require treatment modifications. No serious cytopenia AESIs were reported, and none were associated with increased infections or bleeding events. See Section 7.7.3 for details on the cytopenia risk during the placebo-controlled period.
L2279: ## DILI
L2280: Liver enzyme elevations continued to occur at low frequencies with patterns similar to those observed during the placebo-controlled period. In the remibrutinib group during the entire study period, ALT or AST elevations greater than three times the ULN occurred in 1.5% of subjects compared to 1.3% during the placebo-controlled period alone. One additional subject in the remibrutinib group developed an asymptomatic AST elevation between 3x and 5x ULN after completion of the placebo-controlled period (Table 112). For subjects who transitioned from placebo to remibrutinib during the OL phase, liver enzyme elevations occurred in 1.2% of subjects for ALT or AST greater than 3x ULN. This population experienced two asymptomatic ALT elevations between 3x and 5x ULN and one ALT elevation between 10x and 20x ULN combined with AST elevation between 5x and 8x ULN. Notably, the latter case occurred 14 days after the last dose of remibrutinib and was attributed to excess antihistamine use in a subject with underlying fatty liver disease who had previously experienced spontaneous ALT/AST elevation during placebo treatment. Throughout the entire study period, no potential Hy's Law cases were identified. See Section 7.6.1.8 for details on the risk of elevated liver enzymes during the placebo-controlled period.

L2291: ## 17.3. Study A2201
L2292: The results from Study A2201 provide a limited assessment of the dose-response for safety across remibrutinib dosages of 10 mg QD up to 100 mg BID. Similar to the efficacy results from this study, there were no apparent dose-related patterns observed in TEAEs, SAEs, TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuations, laboratory abnormalities, vital signs, or ECG parameters. The overall incidence of TEAEs was higher in the combined remibrutinib groups (58.1%) compared to placebo (42.9%) as shown in Table 113.

L2302: Abbreviations: BID, twice daily; N, number of subjects; n, number of subjects with specific treatment-emergent adverse event; QD, once daily; REMI, remibrutinib; SAF, safety analysis set; TEAE, treatment-emergent adverse event
L2303: Regarding adverse events of special interest, infection rates were comparable between remibrutinib (25.1%) and placebo (21.4%) groups, as were cytopenias (3.0% versus 2.4%). Bleeding events were slightly more frequent in remibrutinib groups (6.7% versus 2.4%) but were mostly mild, with only two moderate cases of gingival bleeding and hematuria, and two bleeding events leading to discontinuation. Overall, there did not appear to be a dose-response pattern for AESIs suggesting the safety profile of remibrutinib was consistent across the tested dose range. Laboratory assessments revealed notable liver enzyme abnormalities in three subjects (all in once-daily arms), but these were isolated elevations that did not meet Hy's Law criteria and did not require treatment discontinuation. Hematology, clinical chemistry, and vital signs showed no significant drug-related safety concerns. During the placebo-controlled period, four female subjects experienced isolated QTcF values in the 10 mg BID, 25 mg BID, and 100 mg BID groups. Notably, no subject exceeded the critical 500 ms threshold, and one case occurred during the extension period, suggesting no treatment relationship. The observed QTcF elevations were transient and were not associated with clinical adverse events nor was there a dose-response relationship.
L2304: ## 17.4. Study A2202E1
L2305: The safety profile of chronic treatment with remibrutinib 100 mg BID was consistent with the preceding 12-week core study and did not reveal any new safety concerns. TEAEs were reported in 139 out of 194 subjects (71.6%), with the majority being mild (35.1%) or moderate (32.5%) in severity. The most commonly reported TEAEs included chronic spontaneous urticaria (11.3%, primarily occurring during the treatment-free follow-up period), COVID-19 (8.2%), headache (6.7%), and eczema (5.2%). Only 23 subjects (11.9%) experienced TEAEs suspected to be related to study treatment, with all individual treatment-related events occurring in two or fewer subjects each.
L2306: Serious adverse events were infrequent, occurring in only six subjects (3.1%) with seven total SAEs reported, and none were considered related to study treatment. COVID-19 pneumonia was the only SAE reported in more than one subject (two subjects, 1.0%). No deaths occurred during the study. Treatment discontinuation due to adverse events was relatively uncommon, affecting 11 subjects (5.7%), with diarrhea being the only AE leading to discontinuation in more than one subject (two subjects, 1.0%).
L2307: Regarding adverse events of special interest, infections were reported in 61 subjects (31.4%), with COVID-19 and nasopharyngitis being the most frequent. Bleeding events occurred in 12 subjects (6.2%), and cytopenias were rare, affecting only 2 subjects (1.0%). Notable changes in vital signs included sitting diastolic blood pressure abnormalities in 20.6% of subjects and sitting systolic blood pressure abnormalities in 19.1%, though subjects with hypertension history were permitted in the study. Laboratory abnormalities and ECG changes, including QTcF prolongation, were infrequent and did not indicate new safety concerns. One pregnancy occurred during the study with a normal course and healthy delivery, with the infant showing normal development at 12-month follow-up.
L2308: ## 17.5. Study A1301
L2309: The safety results from Study A1301 were similar to the findings in Trials A2301 and A2302, revealing no additional safety concerns with remibrutinib 25 mg BID over 52 weeks of treatment. No deaths occurred during the study, and the overall completion rate was high with 68 of 71 subjects (95.8%) completing the full 52-week treatment period.
L2310: Adverse events were reported in 62 subjects (87.3%), but all were either mild (52.1% of subjects) or moderate (35.2% of subjects) in severity, with no severe adverse events reported. The most commonly affected system organ classes were infections and infestations (57.7% of subjects) and skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders (53.5% of subjects), with COVID-19 (19.7%) and headache (12.7%) being the most frequent individual adverse events. Treatment-related adverse events occurred in 19 subjects (26.8%), primarily consisting of diarrhea, petechiae, and purpura, each reported in 3 subjects (4.2%).
L2311: Three SAEs were reported (Meniere's disease, worsening epiretinal membrane, and rhegmatogenous retinal detachment), none of which were considered related to study treatment. Treatment discontinuation due to adverse events occurred in one subject (1.4%) due to worsening atopic dermatitis, which was assessed as unrelated to study treatment. AESIs included infections in 42 subjects (59.2%), bleeding events in 13 subjects (18.3%), and cytopenia in 5 subjects (7.0%), all of which were nonserious and predominantly mild in severity. Laboratory evaluations, vital signs, and ECG parameters showed no clinically relevant abnormalities or drug-related safety concerns, with the majority of laboratory abnormalities being CTCAE Grade 1 or 2 with no clinical consequences.
L2312: ## 17.6. Study A2305
L2313: The safety profile of remibrutinib in this study was based on 33.51 subject-years of cumulative exposure across 144 subjects and demonstrated consistency with the known safety profile for remibrutinib, with no new or unexpected safety signals identified and no deaths reported during the study period. Overall, 86 subjects (59.7%) experienced at least one AE, with the majority being mild (41.7% of subjects) or moderate (16.0% of subjects) in severity, while only three subjects (2.1%) reported severe AEs. The most commonly reported system organ classes were infections and infestations (22.2% of subjects) and skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders (15.3%), with the most frequent individual AEs being headache (9.0%), nasopharyngitis (8.3%), and chronic spontaneous urticaria (6.3%). Treatment-related AEs were reported in 21 subjects (14.6%), with purpura, nausea, headache, and heavy menstrual bleeding being the most common (2.1% each).
L2314: SAEs occurred in four subjects (2.8%), including one case each of acute cholecystitis, dizziness, chronic spontaneous urticaria, and aortic dissection. Two subjects (1.4%) discontinued treatment due to AEs (increased blood pressure and aortic dissection). Infection AESIs were reported in 22.9% of subjects, with all 51 events being nonserious and mild-to-moderate in severity; bleeding AESIs occurred in 8.3% of subjects with all 21 events being nonserious and mild; and cytopenia AESIs were reported in 4.2% of subjects with all 6 events being mild-to-moderate and nonserious. Laboratory analyses, vital signs, and ECG monitoring revealed no clinically relevant abnormalities or drug-related safety concerns, with all newly occurring laboratory abnormalities
L2315: being CTCAE Grade 1 or 2 except for a single Grade 3 triglyceride increase in a subject with preexisting elevated triglycerides at baseline. There were no liver enzyme abnormalities exceeding three times the upper limit of normal and no cases meeting potential Hy's Law criteria.
L2316: The clinical pharmacologist within the Division of Cardiology and Nephrology reviewed the study with regard to hypertension-related adverse events and concluded that the study supported that 25-mg remibrutinib is not associated with clinically significant changes in blood pressure (see review dated June 13, 2025).
L2317: ## 17.7. Study F12101
L2318: The safety profile of remibrutinib 100 mg BID administered to healthy subjects was consistent with prior remibrutinib clinical studies and comparable to placebo, with no new safety signals identified. The overall incidence of adverse events was similar across treatment groups, with 84.2% in the concomitant remibrutinib group, 88.6% in the interrupted remibrutinib group, and 82.4% in the placebo group. The majority of adverse events were mild-to-moderate in severity, with 83.2% classified as Grade 1 and 14.0% as Grade 2.
L2319: No deaths or serious adverse events were reported during the study. Adverse events leading to study treatment discontinuation were more frequent in the concomitant remibrutinib group (13.2%) compared to the interrupted remibrutinib group (2.9%) and placebo group (5.9%), with COVID-19 being the most common reason for discontinuation.
L2320: The overall incidence of infections was numerically higher in the concomitant remibrutinib group (23.7%) compared to the interrupted remibrutinib (14.3%) and placebo (17.6%) groups, though no specific infectious condition drove this difference. Bleeding events were reported in the concomitant remibrutinib group (7.9%) and placebo group (8.8%) but not in the interrupted remibrutinib group; all bleeding events were Grade 1 in severity, and none led to treatment discontinuation. No cytopenia adverse events were reported in any treatment group. Laboratory parameters, vital signs, and ECG remained generally comparable between treatment groups with no clinically meaningful changes observed throughout the study. For additional details regarding the significance of this study, refer to Section 7.7.5.

A Hepatic Impairment (HI) subsection was included because the hepatic impairment study showed an increase in AUC of remibrutinib in patients with mild, moderate, and severe HI. Therefore, the subsection was revised to convey avoid use in patients with any degree of HI.

13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY	
Included results that did not show evidence of carcinogenic potential from a 6-month rash2 mouse study. Remibrutinib was not mutagenic in a bacterial mutagenicity (Ames) assay and was not clastogenic in an in vitro micronucleus assay in human peripheral lymphocytes or in an in vivo blood reticulocyte micronucleus assay in rats. Lastly, remibrutinib did not impact fertility in female or male rats.

L2342: ## 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L2343: ## PMR 1 (4896-1)
L2344: Conduct a 24-week randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group study in pediatric patients aged 12 to less than 18 years with chronic spontaneous urticaria who remain symptomatic despite H1-antihistamine treatment to assess the efficacy, safety, and pharmacokinetic responses to remibrutinib.
L2345: - Final Protocol Submission: September 2022 (complete)
- Study Completion: September 2027
- Final Report Submission: February 2028
L2346: ## PMR 2 (4896-2)
L2347: Conduct a 24-week, open-label, pharmacokinetic study in pediatric patients aged 6 to less than 12 years with chronic spontaneous urticaria who remain symptomatic despite H1-antihistamine treatment.
L2348: - Final Protocol Submission: December 2027
- Study Completion: October 2030
- Final Report Submission: March 2031
L2349: ## **PMR 3 (4896-3)**
L2350: Conduct an additional oral (gavage) juvenile toxicity study in the rat. The age of rats at study start should be equivalent to pediatric patients approximately 6 years old. The study should include ophthalmic examinations performed prestudy, at the end of the dosing period, and at the end of the recovery period. Potential neurobehavioral effects should also be evaluated in animals at the end of the dosing and recovery periods.
L2351: - Draft Protocol Submission: September 2026
- Final Protocol Submission: February 2027
- Study Completion: May 2027
- Final Report Submission: August 2027
L2352: ## **PMR-4 (4896-4)**
L2353: Collect data from a prospective pregnancy exposure registry, preferably a disease-based multiproduct pregnancy registry, using a cohort analysis that compares the maternal, fetal, and infant outcomes of women exposed to remibrutinib during pregnancy with an appropriate comparator population(s). Collect data outside the United States to reach the target sample size, if feasible. The registry will identify and record major and minor congenital malformations, pregnancy complications, spontaneous abortion, stillbirths, neonatal deaths, pregnancy terminations, preterm births, small-for-gestational-age births, and any other adverse outcomes, including postnatal growth and development. These outcomes should be assessed throughout pregnancy. Infant outcomes, including effects on postnatal growth and development, will be assessed through at least the first year of life.
L2354: - Draft Protocol Submission: June 2026
- Final Protocol Submission: June 2027
- Interim Reports:June 2028

- June 2031

- Final Report Submission: December 2038
L2355: ## **PMR-5 (4896-5)**
L2356: Conduct a retrospective pregnancy cohort study using claims or electronic health record data with medical chart validation that is adequately powered to assess major congenital malformations, spontaneous abortions, stillbirths, preterm births, and small-for-gestational-age births in women exposed to remibrutinib during pregnancy compared to appropriate comparator population(s).

L2358: ## **PMR-6 (4896-6)**
L2359: Perform a milk-only lactation study in lactating women who have received remibrutinib to measure concentrations of remibrutinib in breast milk using a validated assay. Assess the effects on the breastfed infant, if available, based on study population.
L2360: - Draft Protocol Submission: March 2026
- Final Protocol Submission: July 2026
- Study Completion: July 2028
- Final Report Submission: September 2028

L2451: ## Summary
L2452: In this submission the sponsor included the report of one animal carcinogenicity study in Crl:WI (Han)rats. This study was intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of LOU064, when administered by oral gavage once daily for 104 weeks.
L2453: ## Rats Study:
L2454: ## Survival analysis:
L2455: - For either male or female rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the water control group and treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the water control group and any of the treated groups.
- For either male or female rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control group and any of the treated groups.
L2456: ## Tumor analysis:

The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant increases in tumor incidence between the water control groups and any of the treated groups.
- For male rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidence across the vehicle control groups and the treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in tumor incidence between the vehicle control groups and any of the treated groups.

L2458: treated groups.

4. For female mice, the trend test showed a statistically significant positive dose response relationships in incidence of endometri adenocarcinoma in uterus (p-value=0.0021&lt;0.005). The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in tumor incidence between the vehicle control groups and any of the treated groups.
L2459: ## 1. Background
L2460: In this submission the sponsor included the report of one animal carcinogenicity study in Crl:WI (Han) rats. This study was intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of LOU064, when administered by oral gavage once daily for 104 weeks.
L2461: In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as the dose increases.
L2462: ## Rat Study
L2463: Two separate experiments were conducted, one in males and one in females. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, one water control, and one vehicle control group. Two hundred and fifty rats of each sex were randomly assigned to the treated groups, water control group, and vehicle control group in equal size of 50 rats per group. The study was designed to continue for up to 104 weeks for both sexes. Rats in water control group received purified water. Rats in vehicle control group received 2% w/w Kolliodon VA64, 0.1% w/w Sodium Lauryl Sulfate in water.
L2464: Table 1: Study Design in Rat Study
L2465: Protocol Group No.	Dose Levels (mg/kg/day)
Male/Female	Identification	Number of Animals Enrolled
Males	Females
1	0	Water	50	50
2	0	Vehicle	50	50
3	30	Low	50	50
4	100	Mid	50	50
5	300	High	50	50
L2466: ## 1.1. Sponsor's analyses
L2467: ## 1.1.1. Survival analysis
L2468: Tests to compare survival were performed using two-sided risk for increasing and decreasing mortality with dose. Tests were performed for dose response (vehicle control and dosed groups only) and for pairwise comparisons of the water control and each dosed group against
L2469: vehicle control using Kaplan-Meier product-limit estimation curves, along with Log-Rank and Wilcoxon tests. These were performed using the LIFETEST procedure in SAS (SAS 2002-2008). The time to death or euthanasia (in weeks, calculation detailed in the following) was the dependent variable. The groups were included as the strata. Animals with a death or euthanasia status recorded as a scheduled euthanasia (interim or terminal) or accidental death were censored in the analysis.
L2470: Sponsor's findings: The sponsor's analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival were 26 (52%), 27 (54%), 25 (50%), 25 (50%) and 23 (46%) male rats, and 28 (56%), 29 (58%), 24 (48%), 26 (52%) and 25 (50%) in female rats in water control, vehicle control, low, mid and high dose groups, respectively.
L2471: The sponsor concluded that oral (gavage) administration of 30, 100, or 300 mg/kg/day LOU064 to Crl:WI (Han) rats for up to 104 weeks resulted in no LOU064-related effects on mortality in males or females.
L2472: ## 1.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L2473: Tests to compare tumor incidence were performed using a one-sided risk for increasing incidence with dose. Tests were performed for dose response (vehicle control and dosed groups only) and for pairwise comparisons of the water control and each dosed group against the vehicle control group. The water and vehicle control groups were compared using a two sided tests.
L2474: For tumors occurring in animals dying spontaneously or euthanized in a moribund condition during the study, the pathologist classified the context of observation as one of the following.
L2475: **Fatal:** The tumor was a factor in the demise of the animal.
L2476: **Nonfatal:** The tumor was not a factor in the demise of the animal.
L2477: **Uncertain:** Not clear if the tumor was a factor in the demise of the animal.
L2478: Non-palpable non-fatal and fatal tumors were analyzed by the IARC asymptotic fixed interval-based prevalence and death rate tests respectively (Peto et al 1980).
L2479: The cut-off points for the interval-based test were Weeks 0 to 52, 53 to 78, 79 to 92, 93 to before terminal euthanasia, and the terminal euthanasia. Fatal and non-fatal tumors were analyzed together, with separate stratum for each. No tumors of uncertain context were noted. The test was implemented using PROC MULTTEST in the SAS system (SAS 2002-2008). In the case of sparse tables (&lt;10 total in a stratum), the exact form of the test was used for that stratum. Otherwise, the asymptotic version of the test was used.
L2480: Sponsor's findings: Sponsor concluded that oral (gavage) administration of 30, 100, or 300 mg/kg/day LOU064 to Crl:WI (Han) rats for up to 104 weeks resulted in no LOU064-related neoplastic findings in males or females.
L2481: ## 1.2. Reviewer's analyses
L2482: To verify sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses.
L2483: ## 1.2.1. Survival analysis
L2484: The survival distributions of three treated groups, vehicle control group, and water control group were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose response relationship in survival was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1 and 2 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 6 and 7 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals among the water control and the treated groups, and among the vehicle control and three treated groups are given in Tables 8 to 11 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2485: Reviewer's findings: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival were 26 (52%), 27 (54%), 25 (50%), 25 (50%) and 23 (46%) male rats, and 28 (56%), 29 (58%), 24 (48%), 26 (52%) and 25 (50%) in female rats in water control, vehicle control, low, mid and high dose groups, respectively.
L2486: For either male or female rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the water control group and treated groups. The pairwise comparisons also did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the water control group and any of the treated groups in either sex.
L2487: For either male or female rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups. The pairwise comparisons also did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control group and any of the treated groups in either sex.
L2488: ## 1.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L2489: The tumor data were analyzed for the positive dose response relationships and the positive pairwise comparison increases between each of the treated groups with control group. Both the dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-K method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993). In this method an animal that lives the full study period (w_{max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of s_h = 1. An animal that dies at week w_h without a tumor before the end of the study gets a score of s_h = \left(\frac{w_h}{w_{max}}\right)^k &lt; 1. The adjusted group size is defined as \sum s_h. As an interpretation, an animal with score s_h = 1 can be considered as a whole animal while an animal with score s_h &lt; 1 can be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size \sum s_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes were then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104
L2490: week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values the exact permutation method was used.
L2491: The tumor rates and the p-values for the positive dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons between the water control and the treated group, and between the vehicle control and three treated groups are listed in Tables 12 to 15 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2492: Adjustment for multiple testing: For the chronic study in rats, the adjustment of multiple testing of the dose response relationship for a submission with one chronic rat study, the more recently revised draft (January, 2013) FDA guidance for the carcinogenicity studies suggests the use of test levels \alpha = 0.005 for common tumors and \alpha = 0.025 for rare tumors for the chronic rat study. For pairwise comparisons for the chronic rat study in the above type of submission with one chronic rat study, the same guidance document suggests the use of test levels \alpha = 0.01 for common tumors and \alpha = 0.05 for rare tumors for the chronic rat study.
L2493: It should be noted that the FDA guidance for multiple testing for dose response relationship is based on a publication by Lin and Rahman (1998). In this work the authors investigated the use of this rule for Peto analysis. However, in a later work Rahman and Lin (2008) showed that this rule for multiple testing for dose response relationship is also suitable for Poly-K tests.
L2494: Reviewer's findings: The tumor types in Tables 2 to 5 below showed p-values less than or equal to 0.05 in the tests between water control or vehicle control and the treated groups for males and females.
L2495: Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed in the rats data analysis section, we make the following statistical conclusions:
L2496: - For male rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidence across the water control groups and the treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant
L2497: differences in tumor incidence between the water control groups and any of the treated groups.

- For male rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidence across the vehicle control groups and the treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in tumor incidence between the vehicle control groups and any of the treated groups.
- For female rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidence across the water control groups and the treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in tumor incidence between the water control groups and any of the treated groups.
- For female mice, the trend test showed a statistically significant positive dose response relationships in incidence of endometri adenocarcinoma in uterus (p-value=0.0021&lt;0.005). The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in tumor incidence between the vehicle control groups and any of the treated groups.

L2565: Figure 1: Dose-Response Relationship Based on Change From Baseline UAS7 Score at Week 4 in the Dose-Ranging Study A2201. The graph plots Change from Baseline in UAS7 score (Y-axis, ranging from -30 to 0) against Total Daily Dose (mg) (X-axis, ranging from 0 to 200). The legend indicates data points for q.d. (once daily) and b.i.d. (twice daily) dosing regimens. The dose-response curve shows the median value from 5000 bootstrap samples (solid line). The shaded area displays the 90% prediction interval from 5000 bootstrap samples. Dots with error bars represent the LS mean and 90% CI from the MMRM. The curve shows that the change in UAS7 score is significantly negative (indicating improvement) across all tested doses (10 mg, 35 mg, 100 mg QD; 10 mg, 25 mg, 100 mg BID) compared to placebo (0 mg), and the improvement is relatively flat across the tested dose range.

L2569: The figure displays the study design timeline for Trials A2301 and A2302. The timeline starts at W -4 (Week -4) and ends at W 56 (Week 56).
The study phases are:
Screening (Scr): Occurs from W -4 to D 1.
Double-blind treatment period: Runs from D 1 to W 24. Subjects are randomized (R 2:1) to receive either Pbo (Placebo) or Remibrutinib 25 mg b.i.d. (twice daily).
Open-label treatment period: Runs from W 24 to W 52. All subjects receive Remibrutinib 25 mg b.i.d.
Follow up or Extension: Runs from W 52 to W 56.
Key assessment points are marked on the timeline: W -4, D 1, W 12 (PE), W 24 (PA), W 52, and W 56. The Primary Analysis (PA) is conducted at W 24.
Study Design Schematic for Trials A2301 and A2302.

L2584: Figure 18 displays semilogarithmic plots of the arithmetic mean remibrutinib blood concentration-time profiles for Part 2 (MAD QD) and Part 4 (MAD BID) regimens in Study X2101.
Part 2 (MAD QD): The top row shows Day 1 and Day 12 profiles for LOU064 administered once daily (QD) at doses of 10 mg, 25 mg, 50 mg, 100 mg, 400 mg, and 600 mg. The Y-axis is Concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.1 to 1000). The X-axis is Hours post dose (0 to 24).
Day 1: All doses show rapid absorption, peaking around 2-4 hours. The 600 mg dose peaks highest (around 1000 ng/mL). The 10 mg dose peaks lowest (around 100 ng/mL). The 600 mg dose exhibits the longest half-life.
Day 12: All doses show sustained exposure. The 600 mg dose maintains the highest concentration (around 100 ng/mL) over 24 hours. The 10 mg dose maintains the lowest concentration (around 10 ng/mL).
Part 4 (MAD BID): The bottom row shows Day 1 and Day 12 profiles for LOU064 administered twice daily (BID) at doses of 100 mg and 200 mg. The Y-axis is Concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.1 to 1000). The X-axis is Hours post dose (0 to 24).
Day 1: Both doses show rapid absorption, peaking around 2-4 hours. The 200 mg dose peaks highest (around 1000 ng/mL). The 100 mg dose peaks lowest (around 100 ng/mL). The 200 mg dose exhibits the longest half-life.
Day 12: Both doses show sustained exposure. The 200 mg dose maintains the highest concentration (around 100 ng/mL) over 24 hours. The 100 mg dose maintains the lowest concentration (around 10 ng/mL).

L2594: Figure 25 displays two parts of data from Study X1101, showing the arithmetic mean (SD) of BTK occupancy and free BTK levels in peripheral blood across different LOU064 doses and placebo.
Part A consists of two graphs:
Left Graph (LOU064 BTK occupancy (%), peripheral blood): Shows BTK occupancy percentage over time (Visit). LOU064 200 mg achieves the highest occupancy (around 90-100%) by Day 1, 24 hrs, which then gradually declines. LOU064 30 mg achieves around 80% occupancy by Day 1, 24 hrs. LOU064 5 mg and 100 mg show lower occupancy (around 40-60%). Placebo shows negative occupancy (around -20% to -40%).
Right Graph (LOU064 Free BTK (ng/mL)): Shows Free BTK concentration over time (Visit). LOU064 200 mg and 400 mg show the lowest free BTK levels (near 0 ng/mL) by Day 1, 24 hrs, indicating high occupancy. LOU064 30 mg and 100 mg show moderate free BTK levels (around 40-80 ng/mL). LOU064 5 mg shows the highest free BTK levels (around 100-140 ng/mL). Placebo shows free BTK levels around 100-120 ng/mL.
Part B shows LOU064 Free BTK (ng/mL) over time (Visit) for LOU064 100 mg BID and Placebo BID. LOU064 100 mg BID shows a sharp decrease in free BTK levels starting around Day 1, 0.5 hrs, reaching near 0 ng/mL by Day 1, 24 hrs, and remaining low until Day 5, 48 hrs, before rising again. Placebo BID shows free BTK levels around 100-120 ng/mL throughout the study period.

L2598: Figure 29 illustrates the study design for Study X2105, a three-period crossover trial comparing three formulations of remibrutinib (LOU064). The study involves two cohorts (Cohort 1 and Cohort 2), each with N=16 subjects, undergoing a screening phase (Days -28 to -2).
The study is divided into three periods:
Period 1: Cohort 1 receives Day 1 Dose A/B2, followed by Washout1, Re-Baseline, Day 1 Dose B/A2, followed by Washout1, Re-Baseline, Day 1 Dose C2, followed by EOS (End of Study). Cohort 2 receives Day 1 Dose A/B2, followed by Washout1, Re-Baseline, Day 1 Dose B/A2, followed by Washout1, EOS.
Period 2: Cohort 1 receives Day 1 Dose B/A2, followed by Washout1, Re-Baseline, Day 1 Dose C2, followed by EOS. Cohort 2 receives Day 1 Dose B/A2, followed by Washout1, EOS.
Period 3: Cohort 1 receives Day 1 Dose C2, followed by EOS. Cohort 2 receives Day 1 Dose C2, followed by EOS.
Both cohorts proceed to a Follow up call (Last dose + 30 days).

L2601: Figure 32 displays two graphs showing the arithmetic mean (SD) blood concentration-time profiles of remibrutinib (LOU064) in fasted and fed states across two dose cohorts (A and B).

Cohort B (100 mg dose): The graph compares LOU064 100 mg fed (blue line, n=19) and LOU064 100 mg fasted (red line, n=20). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 400 ng/mL. L2612: Figure 43 displays two graphs showing the arithmetic mean (\pm SD) plasma concentration-time profiles of Digoxin in the presence and absence of Remibrutinib (LOU064).
The left graph, titled "Linear Scale (0-24 hr Profile)", plots Mean Digoxin Conc (pg/mL) on a linear scale (0 to 3000) against Nominal Time (Hours) (0 to 24). The right graph, titled "Semi-log Scale (0-192 hr Profile)", plots Mean Digoxin Conc (pg/mL) on a semi-log scale (1 to 10000) against Nominal Time (Hours) (0 to 192).
In both graphs, two profiles are shown: Digoxin alone (represented by diamonds) and LOU064 (remibrutinib) + Digoxin (represented by triangles). The Digoxin alone profile shows a rapid peak concentration around 2 hours, reaching approximately 2000 pg/mL, followed by a rapid decline. The LOU064 + Digoxin profile shows a significantly lower peak concentration (around 1000 pg/mL) and a slower decline, indicating a substantial reduction in Digoxin exposure when coadministered with remibrutinib.

L2633: Flowchart illustrating the disposition pathways of Remibrutinib at steady state, comparing Observed (white boxes) and PBPK predicted (blue boxes) pathway contributions to overall systemic clearance.
The Oral dose 1 splits into two main pathways:
Pathway 1 (Absorption): Oral dose 1 (88% PBPK predicted, 89% Observed) leads to First pass 2.
Pathway 2 (Excretion): Oral dose 1 (12% PBPK predicted, 11% Observed) leads directly to Feces (Parent).
From First pass 2, the drug proceeds to Systemic drug (34% PBPK predicted, 34% Observed).
Systemic drug undergoes metabolism:
Metabolism via CYP3A4 (~43% PBPK predicted, ~43% Observed) and Other metabolism (~55% PBPK predicted, ~55% Observed) leads to Feces (Metabolites).
Metabolism via CYP3A4 (~43% PBPK predicted, ~43% Observed) and Other metabolism (~55% PBPK predicted, ~55% Observed) leads to Urine (Metabolites).
Systemic drug (28% PBPK predicted, 26% Observed) leads to Urine (Parent).
Excretion pathways:
Feces (Parent): 12% PBPK predicted, 11% Observed.
Feces (Metabolites): 68% PBPK predicted, 57% Observed.
Urine (Metabolites): 31% PBPK predicted, 31% Observed.
Urine (Parent): 2% PBPK predicted, <2% Observed.

0.600-0.800
0.800-1.000
1.000-1.200
1.200-1.400
1.400-1.600
Both plots show that the Cmax Ratio (Sub) and AUC Ratio (Sub) are generally high (above 1.000) across the parameter space, indicating strong induction effects.
